# RELIABILITY_AUDIT.md — radon reliability audit (frozen contract)

Audited: 2026-08-08/09, HEAD `8eeee9b6` (main, includes the merged `test-audit-remediation` wave T-001…T-054).
Method: six parallel code-walk audits (connectivity/sessions, state/persistence, resource exhaustion, error handling, safety mechanisms, observability) + system map. Every claim was verified in code, not inferred from names. Category reports: session scratchpad `audit-*.md`.

---

## 1. Executive summary

Radon's *manual* order perimeter is genuinely disciplined — permId-confirmed placement, no-auto-retry of non-idempotent scripts, poll-confirmed cancels, idempotent journal writes keyed on IB execIds — but the *autonomous* exit-order handler skipped every one of those disciplines: it places live GTC orders with a 1-second sleep instead of an ack, marks the journal PLACED for orders IB may have silently discarded, and its only duplicate-placement guard is an in-memory dict that evaporates on every deploy. There is no kill switch, no cancel-all, and no server-side risk limit anywhere: every gate that matters for money is client-side display, and at least five code paths reach IB `placeOrder` with nothing but `quantity > 0` between them and the margin engine. Position reconciliation cannot detect same-symbol quantity drift, runs only when a browser loads `/journal`, and alerts no one when it finds a problem. The single scenario most likely to cause real damage: **a Turso blip during an exit-order placement followed by a routine deploy restart — the daemon re-places the same live SELL every 5-minute cycle until the DB recovers, and nothing pages**. Blast-radius profile: a fat-finger or runaway loop is bounded only by IB's margin engine; a silent exit-order drop is bounded only by the operator noticing a missing order in TWS.

## 2. System map

*(assembled from the A1 map agent + category walks; every claim cited)*

### 2.1 External dependencies

**IBKR client-ID ledger** (`scripts/clients/ib_client.py:73-113`): FastAPI IBPool sync=3/orders=4/data=5 (per-role asyncio.Lock, staggered 1s connects, 3 attempts — `scripts/api/ib_pool.py:108-162`); WS relay rotates from clientId 10 (`ib_realtime_server.js:104,230-233`); subprocess scripts + daemon handlers auto-allocate 20–49 (`ib_client.py:294-301`); `ib_reconcile.py` fixed 21; scanners 50–69; CLI 90–99. Steady state: 3 pool + 1 relay + transient sessions against one Gateway login at `127.0.0.1:4001`.

**IB Gateway**: Docker on Hetzner; `IB_GATEWAY_MODE=cloud` makes FastAPI observational-only (TCP probe, never starts — `ib_gateway.py:56, 237, 720-734`); lifecycle owned by `radon-ib-gateway-control` under deploy lock + 2FA lease (`cloud/scripts/ib-gateway-control.sh:393-467`); restarts guarded by the 2FA push lock + backoff ladder with auth-state tracking `{authenticated, awaiting_2fa, unreachable}` (`ib_gateway.py:105-115, 161-212`).

**Market data**: IB L1 via relay (L2 behind `RADON_DEPTH_ENABLED`); Unusual Whales with backoff on 429/5xx and `UWRateLimitError` (`uw_client.py:63-86, 178-182`); Yahoo/Cboe/FRED/FINRA fallbacks.

**Turso/libSQL — two transports by design**: bounded Hrana HTTP (4.0s socket timeout, `hrana_http.py:36`) for journal/portfolio/service_health writes (`writer.py:30-41`); unbounded native `libsql_experimental` for everything else — cannot be timeout-wrapped, holds the GIL while blocked (`client.py:18-24`). FastAPI must never import `db.client` (lint-enforced, `client.py:58-60`). Replica retired; `RADON_DB_NO_REPLICA=1` set fleet-wide by drop-in (`cloud/services/radon-.service.d/common.conf:11-12`). Test-pollution guards on both transports.

**Clerk**: Next.js Edge middleware perimeter; FastAPI `verify_clerk_jwt` 401s on invalid, trusted-loopback bypass via `is_trusted_local_request`, hard RuntimeError if JWKS env unset — fails closed (`auth.py:55, 101-110, 137-163`).

**systemd/clocks/locks**: ~35 units + 25 timers in `cloud/services/`; only hard dep is `Requires=docker.service` on the Gateway; market hours via `ZoneInfo("America/New_York")` with a fail-open fixed UTC-5 fallback (`daemon.py:87-95`); 2FA push lock, deploy lock + lease, fsynced deploy transition journal (`deploy.sh:564-586`).

### 2.2 Persistent state stores + invariants

| Store | Key/invariant | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| `journal` | `trade_id` PK (= IB execId for live ingest), idempotent upsert; canonical trade store for `/journal`, `/orders`, `/blotter` | `0001_init.sql:70-76`; `writer.py:46-53` |
| `open_orders` | `perm_id` PK; no-empty-window replace (upsert-first, delete-last, T-024) | `0004:16-20`; `writer.py:1100-1141` |
| `executed_orders` | `exec_id` PK, append-in-effect | `0004:22-34` |
| `position_execution_facts` | `(account_id, exec_id, revision)` PK; immutable — hash conflict raises, never overwrites | `0037:5-21`; `writer.py:984-988` |
| `portfolio_snapshots` | `taken_at` PK; archive-coordinated deletion only | `writer.py:361-464` |
| `cash_flows` | IB transactionID PK — idempotent Flex re-pull | `0002:13-25` |
| `service_health` / `_events` | `service` PK latest-state; transitions-only event trigger | `0001:103-110`; `0011:27-49` |
| `daemon_state` (Turso) | flattened `last_run/last_status` only — **lossier than the disk file** (no `known_orders`) | `0004:38-44`; `daemon.py:245-253` |
| `data/daemon_state.json` | per-handler `last_run` + `fill_monitor.known_orders` (fill-dedupe set, disk-only); corrupt file → one WARNING, all handlers start blank, no `.corrupt` backup | `daemon.py:169-170, 226, 268-278` |
| `data/*.json` scan caches | dual-write: Turso best-effort + disk unconditional via checksummed `atomic_save` | `scan_mirror.py:83-126`; `atomic_io.py:36-60` |
| `portfolio.json`/`trade_log.json`/`blotter.json`/`watchlist.json` | **no production writer** (lint-enforced demotion) — but `scripts/knowledge/sources/journal.py:16` still ingests the frozen `trade_log.json` | `test_flat_json_source_truth_contract.py:23-34` |

Divergence resolution in web reads is freshest-content-timestamp-wins with degraded-source gating (`dbFirstRead.ts:126-165`); `journal/blotter/portfolio/orders` routes are DB-only, no disk fallback.

### 2.3 Process boundaries + down-behavior

- **Next.js → FastAPI**: `radonFetch` 30s AbortSignal, structured `RadonApiError` unwrap (`radonApi.ts:37-75`). Placement timeout → 504 `UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_ORDER_INDETERMINATE` with the idempotency key held (`place/route.ts:369-384`). Post-place refresh failure swallowed as non-fatal (`route.ts:390-394`).
- **Browser → relay**: single-use 30s WS tickets minted by FastAPI (`ws_ticket.py:1-30`); client exponential backoff + jitter; relay stale-tick ladder 45s/K=3 → alert + lock-held `/ib/restart` escalation (`staleDataMachine.js:27-41`; `ib_realtime_server.js:636-757`); `WatchdogSec=45` — the only unit with one.
- **FastAPI → Gateway**: health = TCP + pool `managedAccounts()` (connected-but-empty = 2FA pending, `ib_pool.py:256-284`); pool down → IB routes 503, UW routes keep working; 15s recovery heartbeat, single-flight, cap-3 then `os._exit(1)` (`server.py:289-339`).
- **monitor_daemon → IB/Turso**: fresh auto-ID connection per handler cycle; IB failure → `result["error"]`, retried next cycle; journal upsert failures swallowed with Flex rehydrate as stated recovery (`fill_monitor.py:375-378`); dual-write catches `BaseException` (`daemon.py:229-261`).
- **Watchdogs**: four timer buckets + hysteresis + acks; `radon-ib-watchdog` is the only non-operator path allowed to cycle the Gateway (lease + preheld unit); `radon-nextjs-db-watchdog` restarts Next.js after 3 wedge cycles; `radon-health` :8330 stdlib-only, three-valued probe states, zero dependency edges.
- **Newsfeed → Turso**: bounded writes + 2 retries + 60s circuit breaker (`writer.js:17-132`).

### 2.4 Order lifecycle state machine (money path)

**Placement**: UI → Next.js `/api/orders/place` (schema validation → demo blockade → idempotency `runIdempotentOrder` — ⚠ in-process Map, wiped on restart/deploy, `orderIdempotency.ts:34-55` → `radonFetch` 30s) → FastAPI `/orders/place` (25s subprocess) → `ib_place_order.py` (fresh client 20–49, orderRef `radon-<uuid>`, confirm-poll to `permId≠0` or terminal, 12s combo/6s single) → disconnect.

States at placement: `PendingSubmit/ApiPending/Unknown` (limbo, poll) · `Submitted/PreSubmitted` (accepted) · `Filled` (immediate) · `Rejected/Cancelled/ApiCancelled/Inactive` (terminal-failed, 1.5s async-error grace) · `permId==0` past deadline → explicit error because disconnecting there makes IB silently drop the order (`ib_place_order.py:406-526`).

Crash exposure at placement: (1) subprocess killed mid-poll → order possibly live, no JSON; Next.js holds the idempotency key 60s (indeterminate); (2) Next.js restart wipes the idempotency Map; (3) silent-rejection race handled (`OrderRejectedError`, key cleared); (4) post-place refresh swallowed → `open_orders` lags ≤5 min.

**Cancel/modify**: subprocess reconnects as the original clientId (master can cancel, not modify); pool path confirm-polls both ops; crash point: modify transmitted but confirm-poll times out → order IS modified while caller sees error (`pool_order_manage.py:173-179`).

**Fills → journal (three overlapping paths)**:
- Path A `orders-sync`: 5-min market-hours loop + on-demand refresh, single-flight, no-empty-window replace + executed_orders + execution facts, heartbeat via `service_cycle` (`server.py:412-460`; `ib_orders.py:252-296`).
- Path B daemon: `fill_monitor` 60s (diffs open orders vs persisted `known_orders`; partial fills → inline journal upsert on synthetic conId+permId key; vanished → COMPLETED); `journal_sync` 300s + 15-min post-close grace (session-scoped `get_fills()`, dedupe on `ib_exec_id`, corrections supersede in place); `exit_orders` 300s (journal-payload scan → GTC SELL placement → row PLACED; T-010 in-memory guard).
- Path C reconciliation: `ib_reconcile` at startup + on `/journal` load (report-only); `journal_reconcile` daily Turso-only gap scan (alert-only); `POST /journal/rehydrate` Flex backfill 365d (manual, the ultimate recovery net).

Fill-side crash points: fills between cycles invisible until next session-scoped `get_fills()`; daemon_state loss blanks `known_orders`; multi-day outage needs manual rehydrate; production journal_sync has **no per-cycle re-attempt of swallowed Turso writes** (`journal_sync.py:154-158` dead-gated by `trade_log_path=None`, `run.py:99-101`); SIGTERM (every deploy) skips `finally: save_state()` (`run.py:243-245` vs `daemon.py:199-201` catching only KeyboardInterrupt).

### 2.5 Startup/shutdown

- **Topology**: no `BindsTo=`; two `PartOf=radon-ib-gateway.service` edges (relay, monitor) — Gateway stop cleanly stops both and `Restart=always` does NOT re-fire on clean stop (DUR-10 cascade-stop trap); `radon-api` is `After=`-only so the control plane survives Gateway stops. `StartLimitIntervalSec=300/Burst=5` fleet-wide; 60s oneshots get Burst=10.
- **Deploy** (`deploy.sh`, 900s bounded, fsynced journal): build staged release before teardown → `wait_for_gateway_ready` (60s, **warn-and-proceed** — the only data-plane gate) → stop timers-then-services (never the Gateway; refuses if a preheld 2FA restart is in flight) → promote + venv rebuild → batch restart core `{nextjs, api, relay, monitor, newsfeed}` → restore snapshot → gate on units + `/health/lite` + :3000/:8765 + 40s restart-counter stability; full `/health` deliberately excluded; fail → journal-driven rollback.
- **FastAPI lifespan**: observational gateway check → backgrounded pool connect (IB routes 503 until connected) → 15s recovery heartbeat → orders-sync loop → one-shot reconcile. Migration `ExecStartPre` accepts rc=124 so a Turso outage can't block startup.
- **monitor_daemon**: registers 11 handlers, loads state (missing = no-op, corrupt = warn + blank), 30s serial loop, no daemon-level per-handler timeout; starts "successfully" with IB and Turso both down.
- **Laptop switches**: `cloud.sh` goes through the control helper; `local.sh:53` bypasses the control plane (raw `docker compose down` over SSH, no lock/lease) and leaves `radon-ib-gateway.service` active so the PartOf cascade never fires.

## 3. Findings table

Severity model: **P0** can lose money or corrupt position/order state · **P1** outage or silent wrong behavior · **P2** operability.

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-001 | P0 | repo-wide (greps: no `reqGlobalCancel`/halt flag; routes `scripts/api/server.py:1381-4627`; admin `web/components/admin/Ib2faControls.tsx:152-193`) | No kill switch and no cancel-all exist. "Stop Gateway" severs the API session while GTC orders keep working at IB, unmonitored. |
| R-002 | P0 | `scripts/api/server.py:2046-2072`; `scripts/ib_place_order.py:204-216`; `web/lib/placeOrderBodySchema.ts:35-36`; `web/lib/order/risk/useOrderRisk.ts:568,681` | Zero server-side risk limits: no max qty, no max notional, no bankroll %, no order-rate cap. All Four-Gates enforcement is client-side display; `okToSubmit` gates only on data having loaded. |
| R-003 | P0 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:326-334, 394`; contract at `scripts/ib_place_order.py:406-490` | Exit orders placed with `sleep(1)` and no permId/ack confirmation; journal marked PLACED, then `finally: disconnect()` — IB silently discards unacknowledged orders on placer disconnect (documented 2026-05-27 MU failure mode). Position left unprotected while journal claims protection. |
| R-004 | P0 | `exit_orders.py:55-60, 348-367`; `handlers/base.py:264-287`; no broker cross-check in `execute()` | Duplicate-placement guard (`_unrecorded_placements`) is memory-only — not persisted via `get_state`/`set_state` (contrast `fill_monitor.py:407-423`). Restart after a journal-write failure re-places the same live order every cycle. A crash *between* place and journal-update arms no guard at all. No IB open-order cross-check before placing. |
| R-005 | P0 | `scripts/ib_reconcile.py:251-302, 309-312`; `scripts/api/server.py:2236-2244`; `web/lib/journalDb.ts:80-92` | Position reconciliation cannot detect quantity drift (`quantity_mismatch` initialized, never populated), runs only on browser load of `/journal`, discards its report (`{"ok": true}`), exits 0 on IB connect failure, has no service_health row, and pages no one on `needs_attention`. |
| R-006 | P1 | `scripts/api/server.py:1743-1756`; `scripts/workflow/nodes.py:97-149` | `/workflow/run` with `confirm_order: true` places one live order per scanner row — no per-run cap, no size validation, no idempotency at this layer. |

Unapproved 2FA push → orders work at IB with fill/journal/exit management all dead. |
| R-008 | P1 | `scripts/api/server.py:3849-3864` | Placement pre-flight checks `port_listening`/`upstream_dead` only; `awaiting_2fa` passes pre-flight, and a relay stale-feed farm-down does not block submission. |

| R-019 | P1 | `exit_orders.py:134-137` | `_load_pending_orders` DB failure → log + `return []` → green heartbeat. Turso outage silently disables exit-order placement with the watchdog seeing `ok`. |

### Audited, clean (silence ≠ clearance — these were walked and verified)

- **Manual placement path** `ib_place_order.py`: permId confirm-poll, refuses success on `permId==0`, async-error grace-wait, partial-fill surfacing, structured errors (`:406-508`).
- **No auto-retry of placement** after gateway recovery (`server.py:3783, 3924-3938`; pinned by test).
- **Cancel/modify**: poll-confirmed, clientId-scoped, never optimistic (`ib_order_manage.py:103-162, 194-253`).
- **Web order route**: idempotency keys held on indeterminate timeouts (504 `UPSTream_TIMEOUT_ORDER_INDETERMINATE`), dup-submit guards, demo blockade fail-closed (`web/app/api/orders/place/route.ts`).
- **Journal idempotency (primary path)**: PK = execId; correction supersede logic; DB-sourced dedupe means failed upserts retry next cycle (`journal_sync.py:556-635`).
- **executed_orders/open_orders**: PK'd, upsert-then-delete replace, revision+sha256 conflict detection (`writer.py:952-1142`).
- **Relay**: full resubscribe-on-reconnect, generation-guarded clients, bounded stale-tick ladder wired to `service_health`, backpressure with coalescing drops + hard terminate, systemd `Type=notify` `WatchdogSec=45` (`ib_realtime_server.js`, `lib/staleDataMachine.js`, `lib/sendBackpressure.js`).
- **Client-ID hygiene**: non-overlapping ranges, auto-allocation rotation on conflict, relay port-probe against duplicate instances (`ib_client.py:83-110, 291-333`).
- **Backoff ladders**: all bounded and error-classified (IB reconnect, gateway restart 2FA ladder + push lock, pool recovery cap-3 then `os._exit(1)`, Turso `_hrana_with_retry`).
- **Watchdog delivery discipline**: cooldown arms only on confirmed 2xx; P1 emergency retry-until-ack; IB-outage grouping; journal gap SLI ≤15min (`notify.py`, `journal_gap_sli.py`).
- **Half-open gateway detection**: CLOSE_WAIT scan / protocol probe / container health → `upstream_dead` consulted pre/post script runs (`ib_gateway.py:246-311, 375-397, 556-563`).
- **Subprocess hygiene**: every entry point `wait_for`-bounded with kill+reap (`subprocess.py:188, 261, 307`).
- **Bounded web DB layer**: 3s `dbExecute`, bounded undici pool, destroy-storm cooldown (`web/lib/db.ts:92-115`).
- **Log/disk**: journald capped 1G, container logs rotated, no unrotated file logs.
- **Timezones**: ET session-date discipline via zoneinfo (`handlers/base.py:50-63`); `time.monotonic()` for API cooldowns.
- **Test-pollution guards** on both DB transports.

## 4. Top 10 failure scenarios (probability × damage)

1. **Silent exit-order drop.** Gateway sluggish post-2FA; exit_orders places a GTC SELL at `exit_orders.py:326`, sleeps 1s, journal → PLACED, `finally: disconnect()` at `:394` tears down the client while the order is still `PendingSubmit` with `permId==0`; IB discards it. The position runs unprotected; the journal says otherwise; nothing reconciles PLACED-vs-IB. Damage: unbounded downside on the underlying move. (R-003)
2. **Exit-order double-place after deploy.** Turso blips during the journal UPDATE at `exit_orders.py:348`; guard arms in RAM; the nightly deploy restarts `radon-monitor`; row still PENDING → a second identical live SELL next cycle, and another every 5 minutes until the DB recovers. Both fill → oversold position/naked short, Gate 4 disabled. (R-004, compounded by R-012)
3. **Fat-finger with no backstop.** `quantity: 5000` instead of 50 passes the TypeBox schema, the route, and `quantity > 0`; risk UI shows a warning chip that gates nothing. $20M notional reaches IB; only its margin engine can refuse. No kill switch exists to unwind the aftermath quickly. (R-001 + R-002)
4. **Timeout → operator re-place → duplicate.** Combo placement exceeds the 25s budget post-transmit; subprocess SIGKILLed; route returns generic "Script timed out after 25s" (matches no classification pattern → no indeterminate warning); operator re-places; both orders live. The `orderRef` that could have answered "arrived or not" died with the subprocess. (R-009)
5. **Position drift invisible.** An assignment or manual TWS partial-close changes IB quantity; `quantity_mismatch` is never populated, reconciliation only runs when a browser opens `/journal`, its report is discarded, and no alert path exists. Basis, exposure, and exit sizing silently computed on wrong quantities indefinitely. (R-005)
6. **Daemon wedge, alive-but-dead.** Gateway TCP-alive but auth-wedged; `qualifyContracts` (no timeout) hangs the exit-orders handler inside the single-threaded loop; `Restart=always` never fires (process alive, no `WatchdogSec`); exit orders unmanaged mid-RTH until a staleness page ~30+ min later. (R-013)
7. **Mass false-completion.** A degraded session returns an empty `openTrades()` snapshot; fill_monitor declares every tracked order COMPLETED, notifies "filled Nx" with stale counts, deletes baselines; partials during the blip are never detected in real time. (R-016)
8. **DB outage mutes the pager.** Turso down during RTH: every handler's heartbeat write fails, and the watchdog that should page about it returns "skipped (off-window)" exit 0 — units alarm, external-probe read, digest all skipped. First signal is a GitHub Actions failure email up to ~80 min later. (R-021, R-019, R-020)
9. **After-hours fill lost.** A GTC with `outsideRth` fills at 16:20 ET; journal_sync's grace ended 16:15; next morning's fresh IB session no longer returns it; orders-sync was gated closed so `executed_orders` never saw it; journal_reconcile has nothing to diff. Invisible until someone manually runs Flex rehydrate. (R-011)
10. **Workflow mass-placement.** A workflow graph wired scanner→order with `confirm_order: true` returns 40 rows → 40 sequential live orders in one request; a retry places 40 more (no idempotency at the FastAPI layer, no per-run cap). (R-006)

## 5. Remediation backlog (frozen; PART B contract)

Order: reconciliation spine → remaining P0 → P1 → P2. Every task's acceptance criteria include the fault-injection proof.

| Task | Finding | Scope | Acceptance criteria (incl. verification) |
|---|---|---|---|
| REL-001 | R-005 | Reconciliation spine: populate per-contract `quantity_mismatch` in `find_position_discrepancies`; non-zero exit on IB connect failure; write a `service_health` row (`position-reconcile`); `needs_attention` ⇒ `state=error`. Schedule it (daemon handler or timer) so it runs without a browser. | Fault injection: fake IB positions vs snapshot with same-symbol qty divergence → discrepancy reported + error row written (test drives the reconcile with stubbed IB + scriptable DB seam, red first). Connect-failure injection → exit≠0 + error row. Restart drill: run on daemon start. |
| REL-002 | R-003 | Exit-order ack discipline: errorEvent capture + permId/terminal-state poll (6s, mirroring `ib_place_order.py:428-446`) before disconnect; unconfirmed/rejected ⇒ journal stays PENDING + `result["error"]`. | Fault injection: fake IB client that (a) never assigns permId, (b) emits rejection — journal must remain PENDING and heartbeat go error; red first against current 1s-sleep code. |
| REL-003 | R-004, R-012 | Durable duplicate-placement guard: persist `_unrecorded_placements` via `get_state`/`set_state`; before any placement, cross-check live IB open orders for a matching contract+action; exit-status journal update via CAS (`WHERE json_extract(payload,'$.exit_orders.status')='PENDING'` or `written_at` guard). | Fault injection: place → journal-write-failure → new handler instance (simulated restart) → second cycle must NOT re-place (fake IB open-orders returns the live order). CAS test: concurrent wholesale upsert does not resurrect PENDING. Red first. |
| REL-004 | R-001 | Kill switch: `trading_halt` flag (Turso + file fallback) checked at `/orders/place`, `/orders/modify`, `/workflow/run` emit_order, and exit_orders before placement; `POST /trading/halt` + `POST /orders/cancel-all` (master-scoped global cancel), operator-auth'd. | Fault injection: set halt → each path refuses with explicit error (4 call-site tests). Cancel-all drill against fake IB: N working orders → all cancelled, confirm-polled. |
| REL-005 | R-002, R-006 | Server-side order limits in FastAPI (single chokepoint used by place/modify/workflow/exit paths): max qty/order, max notional/order, max orders/min; workflow per-run cap. Env-tunable, fail-closed. | Fault injection: oversized qty, oversized notional, burst >N/min, workflow 40-row emit — each refused server-side with the UI bypassed (direct HTTP). Red first. |
| REL-006 | R-009 | Indeterminate-outcome discipline on timeout/exception: placement timeouts and post-transmit exceptions return `ORDER_INDETERMINATE` + "check open orders" + `orderRef`; route passes `orderRef` in so it survives subprocess death. | Fault injection: subprocess killed post-transmit (stub) → response carries indeterminate code + orderRef; classification test for "Script timed out". Red first. |
| REL-007 | R-019, R-020 | Heartbeat honesty: `_load_pending_orders` failure and journal-upsert failures set `result["error"]` (soft failure, no `last_run` latch); `imported` counts only successful writes. | Fault injection: DB seam raises on read/write → service_health row `state=error` written; `imported` excludes failures. Red first. |
| REL-008 | R-013 | Bound the daemon: `upsert_daemon_state` → hrana transport; per-handler hard deadline (thread+timeout); paginate the journal reads; add sd_notify + `WatchdogSec` to `radon-monitor.service`. | Fault injection: handler stub sleeps past deadline → daemon logs timeout, next handler still runs, heartbeat error. Unit-file assertion test (installed-units contract). |
| REL-009 | R-016 | fill_monitor session-sanity: empty-snapshot (or empty managedAccounts) ⇒ skip cycle, not mass-completion; completions cross-checked against `fills()` to distinguish cancelled vs filled. | Fault injection: degraded fake session returns [] with 3 tracked orders → no completions emitted, baselines retained; cancel event → reported as cancelled. Red first. |
| REL-010 | R-021 | Watchdog: `snapshot_unavailable` distinct from off-window; units alarm + external-probe + digest still run without the snapshot; N consecutive snapshot failures ⇒ direct Pushover P1 (no DB dependency). | Fault injection: DB seam raises → exit code ≠ "skipped", units alarm executed, P1 fired after N failures (fake Pushover records delivery). Red first. |
| REL-011 | R-010 | Merge fill_monitor/journal_sync journal rows: fill_monitor row keyed so the execId row supersedes it (or fill_monitor stops writing journal rows; increments priced from execution deltas). | Fault injection: partial fill seen by both writers → exactly one surviving row in blotter derivation; basis math unchanged vs single-writer baseline. Red first. |
| REL-012 | R-011 | Scheduled off-hours execution sweep: daily post-close orders-sync/journal_sync pass (or Flex rehydrate timer) so outside-RTH fills land in `executed_orders`/journal without an operator. | Fault injection: seeded after-hours execution missing from journal → sweep imports it; window-relative test dates. |
| REL-013 | R-014 | Wrap the index-chain fetch in `asyncio.wait_for` with role-reconnect on timeout. | Fault injection: stubbed hang → bounded failure, lock released (subsequent acquire succeeds). Red first. |
| REL-014 | R-015 | Decide `_on_disconnect`: register it with an event-loop-safe implementation or delete it and correct `scripts/CLAUDE.md`; fix `_subscriptions` leak. | Test proves registration fires on disconnectedEvent (or dead path removed and docs updated); subscription map shrinks on cancel. |
| REL-015 | R-017 | Pool acquire-time liveness for `orders`/`sync` roles: bounded `managedAccounts`/`reqCurrentTime` probe; reconnect on failure. | Fault injection: fake client TCP-connected but probe-dead → acquire reconnects instead of handing it out. Red first. |
| REL-016 | R-018 | Empty-snapshot guard in ib_sync: refuse zero-position upsert when prior snapshot non-empty (flag for explicit override). | Fault injection: degraded fetch returns [] with prior non-empty snapshot → no upsert, error surfaced. Red first. |
| REL-017 | R-008 | Placement pre-flight: refuse when `auth_state != "authenticated"`; surface relay farm-down as submit-blocking in the order UI. | Fault injection: awaiting_2fa state → fast refusal (no 25s burn); UI state test for stale-feed gate. |

history, not a shallow clone). The delta was anchored on `9c893621`, the last commit
touching the reliability documents. Prior entries left as written per the frozen-contract
rule; the correction is recorded as a new ledger line.

**Standing sweeps — all hold.** Halt chokepoints present (`ib_place_order.py:239`,
`exit_orders.py:394`, `server.py:2379/2549/2654`, `ib_execute.py:443`);
`_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` intact (`server.py:4541`); order limits wired
(`ib_place_order.py:252`, `server.py:2381/2566/2671`, `workflow/nodes.py:152`);
exit-order ack poll intact (`exit_orders.py:193`); `upsert_daemon_state` still on the
bounded hrana transport (`writer.py:1487`). All nine permanent fault-injection drill
suites still present. No new `placeOrder` call site bypasses the chokepoints. Both
watchdog catalogs are set-identical across all 50 `scheduled` services — every new
indicator/equibles service is registered in both.

### Delta findings table

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-048 | P0 | `scripts/api/subprocess.py:13-15, 204-205, 268-269`; `scripts/api/server.py:2739, 2752-2779, 2930, 3564` | The new global subprocess admission cap (`MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBPROCESSES`, default 4) is shared by all `run_script*` call sites and the slot is held for the subprocess's entire lifetime. `POST /orders/cancel-all` and `POST /trading/kill` both run `run_script("ib_cancel_all.py")`; the same delta raised `LEAP_PRESET_TIMEOUT_S`/`GARCH_PRESET_TIMEOUT_S` to 3600. Four routine hour-long scans pin every slot and the REL-004 kill switch's cancel path returns `Subprocess capacity exhausted` → 502 for an hour. The halt flag still sets (file write), so new placements stop — the working orders it exists to pull stay live. |
| R-049 | P0 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/evening_execution_sweep.py:245-251`; `scripts/backfill_journal_from_executed_orders.py:117-147, 341-344, 378-383`; `scripts/trade_blotter/flex_query.py:243`; `scripts/journal_rehydrate.py:181, 445` | The delta promoted `backfill()` from an operator script (`--dry-run` default) to an unattended `dry_run=False` call on every 20:30 ET sweep. Its only idempotency key is the raw `ib_exec_id` string, but the two writers use different namespaces: `executed_orders.exec_id` is the IB API execId while Flex rehydrate journals the same fill under the numeric Flex `tradeID`. After any `/journal/rehydrate` covering the trailing 7 days — the documented recovery net — every rehydrated fill that also sits in `executed_orders` is classified as an uncovered gap and journaled a second time. `journal` is the canonical store: net quantity, lot-matched basis, realized P&L and `isOpen` all double. `backfill` has no fingerprint fallback, unlike `journal_rehydrate._legacy_key`. |
| R-050 | P1 | `scripts/api/server.py:2546-2601` vs `:2317-2333, 2376-2382` | `/orders/replace` checks halt (`:2549`) and order limits (`:2566`) up front but never evaluates the REL-005 per-minute rate cap before the destructive step — it lives inside `orders_place`, called at `:2582`, *after* the loop at `:2575-2581` has cancelled every target. An exhausted rate budget (or a halt toggled mid-replace) leaves the operator's working orders cancelled and the replacement refused, surfaced as `REPLACE_PARTIAL` with the position unhedged. |
| R-051 | P1 | `web/lib/orders/orderIdempotency.ts:49-51`; `web/app/api/orders/place/route.ts:363-371, 440-451` | `CONTENT_HASH_TTL_MS` was raised from 4,000 ms to 300,000 ms. At five minutes a genuinely intended identical second clip — scaling into a position, re-placing a stop with the same payload after cancelling it — is suppressed and the route returns the *first* order's `orderId`/`permId` with `status: "ok"`. `deduplicated: true` is set at `route.ts:450` but no UI surface reads it, and only `web/lib/chat.ts:250` sends an explicit `idempotencyKey`, so every order-entry surface falls to the content-hash key. The operator believes two orders are live and holds half the intended position. |
| R-052 | P1 | `scripts/order_limits.py:59-73, 124-133`; `scripts/ib_place_order.py:299-307` | Commit 1db9f558 made a combo `limitPrice` a signed net price, but `order_notional` computes `abs(limitPrice) × quantity × 100`, so a combo's notional cap is now measured against the **net credit** rather than the risk. A 500-lot short strangle at $0.20 credit prices as $10,000 of "notional" and clears `RADON_MAX_ORDER_NOTIONAL` ($250k) with room to spare, leaving the contract-count cap as the only server-side bound on an undefined-risk position — with Gate 4 disabled, IB's margin engine is the backstop again, which is what REL-005 existed to stop being true. |
| R-053 | P1 | `scripts/api/server.py:2724, 2732, 2752, 2765`; no `web/app/api/trading*` or `.../cancel-all` route exists | The REL-004 kill switch has no operator-reachable client path. A repo-wide grep for `/trading/kill`, `/trading/halt`, `/orders/cancel-all` returns only `server.py`, its test, and the audit docs — zero UI or client callers. A browser request to `app.radon.run/api/ib/trading/kill` carries the Clerk cookie, but `verify_clerk_jwt` reads only `Authorization: Bearer` (`auth.py:270-272`) and Caddy stamps `X-Forwarded-For` so the loopback bypass denies (`auth.py:118`) — it 401s. Firing the control requires SSH, a tailnet curl, or hand-extracting a token from DevTools, in exactly the minutes it exists for. |
| R-054 | P1 | `scripts/api/auth.py:228-238, 307-321`; `web/middleware.ts:226-249`; `web/lib/routeAccess.ts:101-103`; absent from `cloud/config/required-env.txt` and `cloud/.env.example` | Three separate comments assert production sets `RADON_REQUIRE_OPERATOR_ALLOWLIST=1`, but the string appears only in those source files and their tests — no env template, no `required-env.txt` entry, no deploy preflight. The fail-closed interlock is inert. If `ALLOWED_USER_IDS` is ever blanked or typo'd on the `radon-api` env, `auth.py:321` returns the decoded payload for any valid Clerk JWT on the shared instance, and `cloud/caddy/Caddyfile:32-34` mounts FastAPI at `/api/ib/*` bypassing Next.js middleware — so a demo trial user could reach `POST /api/ib/orders/place` directly. |
| R-055 | P1 | `scripts/grok_page_responder.py:72-81, 154-163, 177-179`; `scripts/watchdog/pages.py:28, 78-83`; `cloud/services/radon-grok-page-responder.service:11-12, 21-23` | `GROK_PAGE_RESPONDER`, `GROK_PAGE_AUTOSHIP`, and `GROK_PAGE_AUTOPUSH` all default **True** when unset, so a missing or renamed `EnvironmentFile` yields maximum autonomy rather than stand-down. The responder invokes `grok --always-approve` on a prompt built from watchdog `last_error` text (third-party/exception content, defended only by prompt instructions) and on a `code_fix` disposition runs `git push origin main`, which triggers the production auto-deploy. Bounds are per-ticket only (3 attempts, one ticket per service/severity/kind/hour) — no global cap, so a weekend of hourly P1s is ~24 independent autoship-and-push runs. `NoNewPrivileges` blocks sudo and docker is `InaccessiblePaths`, but there is no `ProtectHome`/`ReadOnlyPaths`, so the 0600 `radon`-owned `.env` is readable by the agent despite the unit's stripped environment. |
| R-056 | P1 | `scripts/watchdog/grouping.py:205, 208-238, 283-293, 309-312`; `cloud/services/radon-api.service:13-14, 45-46`; `scripts/api/server.py:299-300, 322, 387-389` | The deploy-warmup suppression drops the grouped IB page *and* the per-service page for every absorbed service whenever `radon-api` entered active <180 s ago. That predicate is a pure timestamp read with no consecutive-suppression counter and no ceiling, so any restart cadence between ~60 s and 180 s suppresses every IB-outage page indefinitely. The pool-recovery ladder lands squarely in that gap: `os._exit(1)` after 3 verified failures at a 60 s cooldown yields a ~185-200 s restart loop, and the watchdog's 5-min sample almost always falls inside the window. `service_health` still goes red, so the dashboard stays honest — the pager does not. |
| R-057 | P1 | `scripts/watchdog/external_probe.py:40-66, 77-78, 154-173`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:345-354, 729-767, 1320-1323`; `scripts/health_service/serve.py:78`; `scripts/health_service/probes.py:35-45` | While `/home/radon/.radon-deploy-transition.json` exists, any off-box sample younger than 900 s counts as "inside a deploy window" and a validated `VERDICT_DOWN` with a 5xx reason is turned into `fired=False`. There is no now-cap on this branch and no staleness check on the journal, and an interrupted deploy whose `recover_pending_transition` fails leaves the journal on disk — a state that also makes every subsequent deploy refuse to run, so it cannot self-clear. The fail-closed `_local_aggregate_is_healthy` guard does not disprove the outage: the health daemon probes Next.js with a TCP liveness probe only, so a Next.js that boots and 500s on every route still reports `up`. Cancelled-deploy → corrupt Next build is the documented failure mode this rule mutes. |
| R-058 | P1 | `cloud/config/drift-allowlist.conf` (27 entries, 35 lines added this delta); `cloud/scripts/drift_audit.py:570-576, 701` | `gather()` filters any drift matching an allowlist line out of `drifts`, and `main()` sets `state = "error" if drifts else "ok"` — so allowlisted drift writes `config-drift = ok`. The allowlist has no expiry, no owning-SHA check, and no ratchet. It currently hides that the live host runs *older* copies of `radon-api`, `radon-monitor`, `radon-relay`, `radon-health`, `radon-drift-audit`, and `radon-nextjs-db-watchdog` — every unit this delta touched for security or reliability. Since neither CI deploy nor bootstrap installs privileged units automatically, the only signal that a unit fix never shipped is exactly the alarm the allowlist silences. |
| R-059 | P1 | `cloud/scripts/nextjs_db_watchdog.py:55, 129, 178, 184-192, 210`; absent from `scripts/watchdog/services.py` and `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts` | The new `unknown` verdict (missing/wrong `RADON_PROBE_FRESHNESS_TOKEN`, or 401/403) logs, re-saves state, and returns without ever calling `write_health_row` — unlike the ok and restart paths. `HEALTH_ROW_SERVICE = "nextjs-db-read"` is in neither watchdog catalog, so the resulting stale row is never staleness-checked either. A token rotated on the Next.js side alone silently and permanently disables the Turso-wedge auto-restart: the unit exits 0 green every 60 s with nobody watching the row. A *missing* token is deploy-gated by `required-env.txt:32`; a *wrong* one is not. |
| R-060 | P1 | `scripts/api/routes/historical.py:81-102`; `scripts/api/ib_pool.py:219-248`; `scripts/clients/ib_client.py:265`; `scripts/api/ib_gateway.py:1080-1101` | `_bounded_pool_call` bounds the IB call at 15 s but its recovery is broken: `pool.retire()` only pops the client from `_clients`, and the only `client.disconnect` sits behind `await asyncio.shield(task)` — i.e. behind the very call that hung. The zombie holds clientId 5's socket, and the next `acquire("data")` reconnects with the same fixed id, which IB rejects as already in use, so every data-role consumer 503s until the wedged call returns. The self-heal cannot fire either: `_pool_has_connected_accounted_slot` is ANY-role, so healthy `orders`/`sync` slots make `recover_stuck_pool` report `recovered=True`, the 3-strike `os._exit(1)` ladder never counts a failure, and `pool.reconnect_all()` instead tears down and rebuilds the live money-role sessions every 60 s — the connect-churn pattern that triggers Gateway 2FA cycles. |
| R-061 | P1 | `scripts/ib_realtime_server.js:2436-2455, 2705-2716, 2746-2753`; `scripts/lib/staleDataMachine.js:136-144, 192-197, 256-262`; `web/lib/probeFreshness.ts:156-162` | Commit 3cd40d18 redefined an active subscription as `state.tickerId != null`, but the IB error handler nulls `tickerId` on code 200 and code 354 while leaving the symbol in `symbolSubscribers`. A market-data entitlement or farm failure that 354s every symbol therefore collapses `activeSubscriptions` to 0; the idle guard substitutes `lastTickAt = now`, `hasHealthyDataPlane` returns true, and the relay writes `ib-realtime-relay = ok` with `tick_age_secs: 0` every 60 s while `clearError` clears any escalation the stale ladder had already latched. `evaluateRelayTick` short-circuits on zero active subscriptions, so `/api/probe/freshness` reports fresh too. Every browser shows frozen prices with green health everywhere. The row is also self-contradictory: `last_tick_at` uses the real global timestamp while `tick_age_secs` uses the idle-substituted one. |
| R-062 | P1 | `scripts/clients/uw_client.py:198-201`; `scripts/utils/uw_budget.py:98-116`; `web/app/api/ticker/info/route.ts:135, 154, 169`; `web/app/api/previous-close/route.ts:142`; `web/app/api/ticker/seasonality/route.ts:212`; `web/app/api/ticker/news/route.ts:84` | `record_hit()` is called from exactly one place — inside `UWClient._get`. Six production UW call sites fetch UW directly from Next.js route handlers and increment nothing. `should_block_universe_scan` trips at 20,000 of a 40,000 daily cap and `usage_snapshot` backs the operator's `/uw/usage` panel, so browsing-driven UW traffic (three UW GETs per `/api/ticker/info`, unrate-limited) is invisible to both the gauge and the only automatic brake. The fleet can pass the real cap while the counter reads comfortably low; the daily-cap 429 is then raised and never retried, and `UwEmbargo` takes `skew` and `oi-changes` down until the 20:00 ET reset, carrying skew2d with them. |
| R-063 | P1 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/expiry_sweep.py:179-180, 190-212, 397-413`; `scripts/tests/test_monitor_daemon/test_expiry_sweep.py:430-484` | The sweep drops `CLOSED` rows *before* the comp/plain pairing in `_net_open_quantity`, and that pairing is the only defence against the same fills being journaled under both id conventions. A Flex-rehydrated completed round trip is a single `CLOSED` row, so it contributes nothing and cannot pair against the real-time daemon's duplicate `SELL_TO_OPEN` row for the same sale; `unpaired_comp` is never set so `ambiguous` stays False. Net then reads as a genuine short, the contract becomes a candidate, and `_write_close_row` upserts a `BUY_TO_CLOSE` at `fill_price: 0.0` for a contract never held — a fabricated windfall in realized P&L plus a phantom position event. Same dual-convention root cause as R-049. |
| R-064 | P2 | `scripts/watchdog/units.py:167, 172-173, 225-233` | `_is_deploy_collateral`'s kill-before-green branch returns from `marker - failed_at` alone; the now-cap is applied only to the in-flight and kill-after-green branches. Both inputs are frozen while the unit stays failed, so a `Result=signal` failure landing in the 60 minutes before the last green marker is downgraded P1→P3 (digest tier) for as long as the unit does not run again and no deploy moves the marker. The stated exit — "recovers on the next timer fire" — is an assumption, not a check; if the same deploy left the timer disabled, a permanently failed unit becomes one line in a daily digest. |
| R-065 | P2 | `scripts/incident_watchdog/__main__.py:34-37`; `scripts/incident_watchdog/store.py:94-95`; `scripts/incident_watchdog/probes.py:149-160` | `allow_resolve` requires that no finding is `unknown`, and `probe_freshness` returns `unknown` both when its token is missing and on any non-200. A rotated token, a 401, or a removed `/api/probe/freshness` route makes `allow_resolve` false on every subsequent cycle, so no open incident in `data/incidents/` can ever be marked resolved. Dishonest state rather than a missed page, but the responder's `status == "open"` selection and the operator's incident list both read that flag. |
| R-066 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-skew2d.service:3-4` vs `cloud/services/radon-skew.service:10-11` | `Requires=radon-skew.service` is the delta's only new hard dependency edge. `radon-skew` is a per-minute RTH oneshot deliberately carrying `StartLimitBurst=10` because hitting the start limit *parks* the unit until a manual `reset-failed`. While the parent is parked, `Requires=` fails the 21:50 UTC skew2d job with a dependency error instead of letting it run against the `skew_history` rows it already has — defeating at the unit layer the "hold through a parent problem" behaviour commit a173289d added at the application layer. `Wants=` + `After=` gives the same ordering without shared fate. |
| R-067 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-db-backup.service:18, 25`; `radon-db-retention.service:14, 20`; `radon-portfolio-archive.service:24, 31` | All three serialize on `/run/lock/radon-db-maintenance.lock` with `flock -w 7500`, but their `TimeoutStartSec` ceilings are 12000 / 10000 / 8000 s — every ceiling exceeds the peers' wait. A `flock` timeout exits non-zero, so the loser enters `failed` and raises a P1 rather than deferring to its next timer slot; the 05:40 / 08:10 / 09:00 UTC schedule leaves single-digit minutes of margin in the worst case. `radon-db-retention.service:3`'s `After=` is inert for timer-initiated starts. |
| R-068 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-signals-refresh.timer:5, 11`; `scripts/run_signals_refresh.sh:5, 8-13`; `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:241, 244`; `cloud/services/radon-signals-refresh.service:23` | The new autonomous timer's only `service_health` output is via the FastAPI scan endpoints, which write `theta-harvester` and `strength-confirmation` as `category: "on-demand"` — rendered as a dormant amber chip and excluded from `services.py` by design. If the hourly timer stops firing, the Top-candidates panel silently freezes; a timer that never fires leaves the unit `inactive`, not `failed`, so `units.py` does not catch it either. The unit description and wrapper comment also still say "15 minute" while the timer is hourly. |
| R-069 | P2 | `scripts/utils/uw_cache.py:23, 103-105, 108-137`; `scripts/clients/uw_client.py:209`; `scripts/host_metrics_sampler.py` | `data/uw_http_cache/` (new this delta) writes one SHA-256-named JSON file per distinct endpoint+params tuple on every successful UW GET, and an expired entry is treated as a miss and returned as `None` *without* being unlinked. A repo-wide grep finds no pruner, no `tmpfiles.d`, and no retention timer. Every cache key carrying a date or expiry parameter produces a file that is dead the next session and never removed. Compounding it, the host-metrics sampler collects CPU, memory, swap and load but no disk usage, and the watchdog has no `df`/`disk_usage` check — so this fills the VPS root filesystem with no warning, taking journald, deploys, and every local write path with it. |
| R-070 | P2 | `scripts/theta_harvester_scanner.py:708-721, 802-813, 815-857`; `scripts/strength_confirmation_scanner.py:880-893`; `scripts/utils/scan_coverage.py:46-62`; `scripts/api/server.py:3170-3185` | A budget-blocked scan prints to stderr, returns a zero-candidate payload, and exits 0. `save_cache` then refuses to persist the empty payload, so `mirror_scan_snapshot` is never reached and **no `service_health` row is written at all** — neither ok nor error. The same holds when UW 429s every ticker and coverage falls below 0.5. The route returns 200 with the stale cache and stamps `_theta_last_scan`, consuming the 1 h cooldown for a scan that never ran. Detection depends entirely on a staleness window eventually noticing an unmoving row, with no signal distinguishing "quota-blocked, self-clears at 20:00 ET" from "scanner broken" — the distinction `UwEmbargo.record_error` publishes for `skew`/`oi-changes` but was never wired into these two. |
| R-071 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-bpi.service:21-24`; `cloud/services/radon-bpi.timer:9, 14` | `TimeoutStartSec=9000` (150 min) exceeds the 120-min gap between the 21:30 and 23:30 UTC fires, against a unit whose own comment documents a measured 105-min tarpitted Yahoo sweep. systemd will not stack a second oneshot instance, so a slow 21:30 run silently swallows the 23:30 fire — and that 23:30 pass exists specifically to recover last-bar nulls Yahoo had not settled at 21:30. On exactly the days the run is slow, the catch-up designed for that condition is the one dropped. |

Quarantined files are therefore neither counted toward the 500-file cap nor eligible for deletion, and accumulate permanently in `data/price_history_cache/`. Low rate, but a hole in the REL-021a remediation of R-033 with no other reaper for that directory. |
| R-073 | P2 | `scripts/lib/checkpoint.py:38-51, 80-100, 102-113, 115-134, 164, 178, 226-240` | The new checkpoint library fsyncs the temp file descriptor but never the parent directory after `os.replace` — the exact gap REL-021a closed in `atomic_io.py`. `json.loads(state_path.read_text())` raises out of `__init__`, so a malformed `state.json` kills the constructor *before* the WAL-recovery helpers can rebuild the completed set, with no `.corrupt-<ts>` backup (contrast REL-021a's `daemon_state.json` handling): the module's header promises a kill "can neither duplicate a finding nor lose one", but a corrupt state file makes the job unstartable. Separately, `_repair_torn_findings_tail` reads the whole WAL into RAM and `checkpoint()` re-renders `SUMMARY.md` by parsing every line on every recorded finding — O(N²) over a job. No production importer today. |

Every guard test in the suite uses a strictly-later date; same-day settlement is uncovered. |
| R-075 | P2 | `scripts/backfill_journal_from_executed_orders.py:125-127, 330, 378`; `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/expiry_sweep.py:120-128`; via `evening_execution_sweep.py:254-256` → `journal_sync.py:820-830` | Two newly-automated handlers put unpaginated full-table journal payload reads on the unbounded native libsql transport: `SELECT payload FROM journal WHERE payload LIKE '%ib_exec_id%'` (rebuilt once per gap row, not cached) and a 1100-day payload scan. Both violate the Hrana I/O bounding rule and now run daily inside a 120 s handler deadline on a money path. This is NF-2's class with two new instances. |
| R-076 | P2 | `scripts/db/retention.py:107-137`; migrations 0039–0048 | None of the ten new migrations adds a retention policy. `cor_history` (a changed-source run rewrites ~5,180 sessions), `vol_cone_history`, `skew2d_history`, the five `equibles_*` tables, `cot_positioning`, and `watchdog_pages` all grow without bound and land whole in every nightly `db_backup.py` dump. (`app_preference_events` is intentionally append-only.) |
| R-077 | P2 | `scripts/db/writer.py:1301-1313`; `scripts/rehash_position_execution_facts.py:77-90` | When a pinned execution hash differs but economics match, the writer returns `False` without updating `payload_sha256` *or* the stored `payload`, so the row stays permanently mismatched-but-tolerated and late IB enrichment (`perm_id`, `order_ref`) never reaches the ledger columns. The rehash script re-derives from the *stored* payload, so it converges on the stale value rather than the current one; it also pages with `LIMIT/OFFSET` over a non-unique `ORDER BY ingested_at`, so ties spanning a page boundary can be skipped. |
| R-078 | P2 | `scripts/api/routes/preferences.py:38-52, 136, 142`; `scripts/api/server.py:612-613`; `web/app/api/preferences/route.ts:106, 132` | The middleware's trusted-local branch returns before setting `request.state.user`, and the only production caller is Next.js on loopback, so `_actor()` always resolves to the literal `"trusted-local"`. Forensics only — the registry bands cap the values and discard out-of-band DB rows. |

Contrast `web/lib/publicShareRoutes.ts`, whose allowlist *is* filesystem-pinned and fails until a new share route is classified. |
| R-080 | P2 | `web/app/api/orders/place/route.ts:102-107, 162-200`; `web/lib/routeAccess.ts:99`; `web/middleware.ts:239-246` | `operatorOnly: true` now runs before the demo blockade and rejects any non-allowlisted user unconditionally, independent of `NEXT_PUBLIC_RADON_DEMO`. Fail-closed, so no money risk — but the paper-fill engine the demo advertises is dead and the blockade logic below the gate is now unexercised in production. |

| R-082 | P2 | `web/lib/usePrices.ts:31, 215, 614-623`; `scripts/ib_realtime_server.js:431, 1296-1311, 1545-1549` | `MAX_CONCURRENT_DEPTH = 3` is global to the relay, not per client. Two sessions (desktop + mobile, or two tabs) each holding a 2-leg implied spread book request 4 tickets, so `evictOldestDepth` recycles the other session's leg; the client deletes the book on `reason: "recycled"` and never re-requests, silently losing half a montage with no error surface. Extends R-036 — the delta tripled the demand side without touching the cap. |
| R-083 | P2 | `web/lib/preferences.ts:82` vs `web/lib/radonApi.ts:76-96` | The new preferences client calls bare `fetch(input, { cache: "no-store", ...init })` with no `AbortSignal`, so a wedged `/api/preferences` hangs the operator preferences UI indefinitely. The same delta's `radonFetchText` does this correctly with `AbortSignal.timeout` + `AbortSignal.any`. |

### Audited, clean in this delta (walked and verified — silence ≠ clearance)

- **`app_preferences` cannot widen a cap.** Bands are code constants (`app_preferences.py:128-243`); `coerce_value` refuses out-of-band writes (`:612-620`) *and* `_resolve_from` discards an out-of-band DB row on the read path (`:487-502`), so hand-editing the Turso row falls through to env/default rather than clamping up. Env values are clamped, never trusted. Store unreachable/malformed → last-known-good or env/default; `resolve()` never blocks and never raises.
- **Preferences cannot touch the halt.** `trading_halt.py` is unchanged in the delta — a file flag, not a registry key, still failing closed on unreadable/malformed input.
- **`/workflow/run` order placement is hard-disabled** — `executor.py:_validate_graph` raises on any `node_type == "order"` and `execute_graph` is the sole entry (`server.py:1937-1940`), so `nodes.emit_order` is unreachable. R-006's mass-placement scenario is closed by construction.
- **Stop orders take the same chokepoint** — `place_order` runs halt then `check_order_limits` before any type branching, and `order_notional` falls back to `stopPrice`.
- **New combo bounds are real** — 2-8 legs, integer ratios 1-100, and `effective_contracts = quantity × max(ratio)` against the contract cap (`order_limits.py:95-122`), mirrored in the TypeBox schema. New `_contract_identity_mismatch` refuses a caller-supplied conId disagreeing with symbol/secType/expiry/right/strike.
- **b4ba6006 cancels nothing** — the "drop stale DAY working orders" change is a read-side display filter (`utils/working_orders.py:33-48`); it does not feed the REL-018 restart inventory or the REL-003 exit-order cross-check.
- **`exit_orders.py` is unchanged** — REL-002 ack discipline, REL-003 durable guard + IB cross-check, and the halt check all intact. `preset_rebalance.py` places no orders.
- **Migrations 0039–0048 all record themselves** in `schema_migrations`; no destructive DDL; every table has a real PK. R-047's 0026 gap is additionally backstopped by `migrate.py:146-149`.
- **No money/health write moved off hrana.** New indicator writers correctly use chunked native-libsql multi-row upserts; `upsert_journal_entry` and `record_service_health` remain bounded.
- **Execution identity re-pin is safe** — `_LIFECYCLE_HASH_FIELDS` is a fixed tuple with hard `KeyError` on a missing field; the rehash script only `UPDATE`s `payload_sha256`, never inserts, and a second run finds zero stale.
- **Retention cannot outrun the archive** — no new policy was added, `portfolio_snapshots` is still excluded, and journal/`executed_orders`/`position_execution_facts` are untouched. `db_backup.py`'s OFFSET→keyset rewrite closes a prefix-duplication hole.
- **Vol-cone checkpoints to Turso**, not disk. No new persisted trading/scanner state lands only on host-local disk.
- **REL-013/014/015/017 verified intact** (`server.py:1288,1326`; `ib_client.py:422`; `ib_pool.py:287-315`; `server.py:4649-4659`). REL-008's `Type=notify` + `WatchdogSec=900` and the relay's `WatchdogSec=45` both still present; `radon-health.service` still carries zero `radon-*` dependency edges.
- **No new DUR-10 cascade edge** — `PartOf=` exists only on the two pre-existing units; none of the 20 new unit files carries `PartOf=`, `BindsTo=`, or a Gateway `Requires=`.
- **Both watchdog catalogs are set-identical** across all 50 `scheduled` services; every new indicator and equibles service is registered in both with a matching window. The `on-demand` category is deliberately excluded from `services.py` (documented contract), so `analyst-ratings`/`chronos-forecast`/`flow-surprise`/`gamma-rotation-scan`/`gex-scan` are not gaps.
- **CI gates were not weakened** — the `.github/workflows/ci.yml` delta is action-pin bumps plus `fetch-depth: 0`; `needs: [secret-scan, web-tests, py-tests, perimeter-smoke]`, the vitest coverage run, `--cov-fail-under=56`, and `pytest cloud/tests` are byte-identical to the anchor. The "unblock deploy" commits did so via drift-allowlist entries — which is R-058.
- **`wait_for_gateway_ready` still fires first** and no new step was added between teardown and the health gate; the tracked-drift guard refuses only on genuine content divergence.
- **`is_trusted_local_request` cannot be spoofed off-box** — uvicorn `--forwarded-allow-ips 127.0.0.1` plus `_arrived_via_proxy`; the new `_is_browser_page_request` closes the same-machine-browser CORS hole.
- **Preference writes have no bulk/reset endpoint**; both `PUT`/`DELETE` are `operatorOnly` and fail closed on an empty allowlist. New FastAPI routers are inside the perimeter (absent from `AUTH_EXEMPT_PATHS`). No new production-reachable auth bypass.
- **`wsTrust.js` fails closed** — missing Clerk config requires an explicit non-production opt-in plus trusted loopback; the production relay unit `exit(78)`s otherwise.
- **UW budget survives deploys** (`git reset --hard` in place, `data/*.json` gitignored) and is genuinely shared across processes via `flock`; `mirror_scan_snapshot` does not fake freshness on a re-persisted payload.
- **New units are runtime-bounded** — every new `.service` carries `TimeoutStartSec` and `StartLimitIntervalSec`/`Burst`; `Type=oneshot` prevents stacked instances fleet-wide.
- **`equibles_client.py` is fully bounded** — timeout on every request, bounded retries, `Retry-After` clamped to 60 s, typed error classification, bounded pagination.

## Delta audit 2026-08-22

**Range:** `c529c92a..c0e7e4f5` (verified `git rev-parse --verify c529c92a^{commit}`; anchor is an ancestor of HEAD). The range contains the 2026-08-16 remediation itself (REL-023…REL-038, PRs #28/#36/#43/#44) plus 50 post-remediation feature commits (`ecc6985f..c0e7e4f5`, 512 files). The remediation commits were re-verified by the standing sweeps below; the agents were scoped to the 50 feature commits by subsystem ownership (money path, IB/UW connectivity, new indicator pipelines, Flex/TWR, control plane, web/daemon resources).

**Standing sweeps (all held):** halt chokepoint present in `ib_place_order.py:240-242` and `exit_orders.py:433-435`; `_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` intact at `server.py:4678` and consulted at `:4787, :4865`; `check_order_limits` wired at `ib_place_order.py:253-255`, `server.py:2416, :2602`, `:2718`; exit-orders ack-poll (`_confirm_placement`, `_ack_perm_id`) intact at `exit_orders.py:187-206`; `upsert_daemon_state` still routed through `_hrana_execute` (`writer.py:1824`); no new `placeOrder|place_order` call site in the range outside tests; every new `service_health` writer (`credit-spread`, `iei-hyg`, `ivrank`, `trin`, `vol-cone-intraday`, `flex-web-service`) is present in both `scripts/watchdog/services.py` and `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts`.

**NEW_FINDINGS re-triage:** NF-1 (Gate-3 bankroll-% cap) still open and now compounded by R-087 below (combo risk cap moved to a hardcoded $10M). NF-2 (unpaginated journal reads) still open; one new instance filed as R-120 (`trin_daily` unbounded read). NF-3 (relay stale-feed does not gate the order UI) still open, unchanged. NF-4 (aggregated Flex buckets) still open; R-088 is a sibling defect on the same backfill path. REL-021b remainder: all P2, no change.

### Delta findings table

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-084 | P0 | `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:137-138, 639, 690-723`; `cloud/config/sudoers.d/radon-deploy:1` | The new root verb `install-units` (eb5cc11f) reads **both** the unit content (`UNIT_SOURCE_DIR=/home/radon/radon/cloud/services`) and the digest that gates it (`UNIT_MANIFEST=/home/radon/radon/cloud/config/installed-units.sha256`) from the checkout that `deploy.sh` `git reset --hard`s **as `radon`**. The verb is NOPASSWD-granted to `radon`, copies any `radon-*.{service,timer}` to `/etc/systemd/system` as root:root, and `enable --now`s new timers. Anything running as `radon` (the grok responder with autoship, a FastAPI worker, a scan script with an injection sink) writes a unit with `ExecStart=/bin/sh -c …` and no `User=`, appends its digest to the manifest, runs `sudo -n radon-deploy-root install-units`, and has root. The sibling verb `sync-scheduled-units` (`:763-885`) gets this right: pins `HEAD == ls-remote main`, sources unit + allowlist + manifest via `cat-file blob` from that tip. `install-units` does none of the three. Confirmed by the main context. |
| R-085 | P0 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:391-411, 506-517, 584-592`; `grep -rn ocaGroup scripts/` → 0 hits | 904f2f18 keyed broker-truth adoption on the limit price (fixing T-055), which removed the property that made REL-003 safe: previously *any* working SELL on a contract was adopted, so at most one exit order could exist per contract. Now `_find_active_sell` returns `(None, False)` whenever the working SELL's readable price differs, and the caller falls straight through to `client.place_order` with a bare `LimitOrder(SELL, totalQuantity=contracts, GTC)` — no `ocaGroup`, no `parentId`, no held-size check. Target and stop sections for one contract, both inside the 40% `max_gap_pct` window, are both placed at full size; a whipsaw fills both and leaves the account **short `contracts` naked options**. Same path re-places instead of adopting whenever a prior cycle's live order price no longer equals `section["price"]` (edited target, IB tick normalisation, manual TWS edit) once the in-memory guard is lost to a redeploy. |
| R-086 | P1 | `scripts/order_limits.py:113-128 (predicate :126), 170-174, 236-244`; reachable via `web/app/api/orders/modify/route.ts:182-198` → `server.py:2602` | `_combo_credit_per_unit` returns a credit when `signed_price < 0 **or** action starts with SELL`. IB BAG sign convention has four quadrants; the fourth — SELL envelope at a **negative** net price, i.e. closing/rolling a short structure for a debit — is booked as *collecting* `|price|×100`/unit, and that phantom credit is subtracted from `risk_per_unit`. A 500-lot SELL combo on a $5-wide short vertical at `limitPrice=-5.00` yields `combo_max_loss == $0` on an order paying $250k; `order_notional` ($250,000) equals, not exceeds, the default cap, so it transmits. |
| R-087 | P1 | `scripts/order_limits.py:41, 93-94, 144-155, 158-174, 203-214` | 38ccbcbf made `order_notional` premium-only and moved combo risk to a hardcoded `_MAX_COMBO_LOSS_DOLLARS = 10_000_000` with no `app_preferences` key — a 40× widening from the $250k notional cap that previously bounded combos, and no longer operator-tunable without a deploy. A 500-lot short strangle on a $50 underlying at $0.20 credit prices at $4.99M → passes; under the anchor it was refused. Compounding: `combo_max_loss` returns `None` (→ **no loss check at all**) when any leg lacks a positive `strike` (`:93-94`) and `check_order_limits` never validates `strike` (`:203-214`); that hole is now the *sole* combo risk gate. NF-1 folded in. |
| R-088 | P1 | `scripts/backfill_journal_from_executed_orders.py:490-493` vs `:508-516, :543-548`; accounting `:157-169` | b81dfef7's counted fingerprint coverage is never *spent* on the exact-`exec_id` skip path (`continue` at `:493` before `claim_fill`). IB slices one order into fills A and B (identical fingerprint F); journal already has A. Processing A → `covers_exec_id` → skip, `claimed[F]` stays 0. Processing B → `covers_fill` sees `fingerprints[F]=1 > claimed[F]=0` → skipped as "already journaled under another id convention" and **never inserted**. The missing row flows into `compute_open_basis_for_ticker` and overrides IB's own `avgCost` — permanently wrong basis/P&L for that ticker. This is the T-056 defect surviving on the most common real path (re-run after a partial previous run). |
| R-089 | P1 | `scripts/clients/ib_client.py:178-204 (fallback :200-203), 449-470`; consumers `ib_sync.py:664-677, 848-875, 865-866, 894-897, 1626-1636` | 1979a0d5 replaced `client.sleep(3)/(2.5)/(2)` with `wait_until(ticker_has_quote, …)`, and `ticker_has_quote` is satisfied by `last`/**`close`** alone. IB delivers the CLOSE tick (type 9) in the first burst after `reqMktData`, hundreds of ms before a two-sided book on anything illiquid, and it is the only prompt tick under `reqMarketDataType(4)`. `wait_until` evaluates the predicate before the first `ib.sleep` (`:462-463`) and returns on the first satisfied poll, so the sync proceeds with `bid/ask` still NaN; `_resolve_market_price` falls to its *last* branch and stamps `pos['marketPrice']` = **prior session close**, `marketValue`/unrealized/day P&L/Return % computed off it, during a live session. `marketPriceIsCalculated` is written but no production web code reads it (`grep -r marketPriceIsCalculated web` → tests only), so the stale mark renders identically to a live one. Silent by construction. |
