| R-129 | P2 | `scripts/utils/flex_embargo.py:49-54, 61-62, 84`; `scripts/perf_twr_builder.py` (`_cache_nav_to_disk`) | The embargo sidecar is written with `Path.write_text` (truncate-then-write) while the repo ships `utils.atomic_io.atomic_save`; `_read_sidecar` swallows `ValueError` → `None` → "no lockout". `radon-api`'s `is_blocked()` or the perf-twr timer reading in the microseconds after truncation sees empty JSON, returns False, and SendRequests into a live lockout (which R-100 then extends). Same non-atomic pattern on the NAV disk cache. |
| R-130 | P2 | `scripts/utils/flex_embargo.py:10-11, 94-98, 105-122`; `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:143` | `clear()` only unlinks the sidecar; `_heartbeat` is called only from `record_lockout`, always with `error`, so the `flex-web-service` row is never cleared — after a lockout lapses the banner stays red ~24 h inside its 8-day window with a `next_attempt_at` in the past. And the dual-write the module header says exists "so the watchdog can see the deadline after a deploy wipes `data/`" is write-only: nothing reads it back, so anything that removes the gitignored sidecar silently drops a live 7-day lockout and the next caller SendRequests into it. `seed_state_after_corrupt_load` shows the rehydration shape. |
| R-131 | P2 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/flex_token_check.py:87-88`; `web/app/api/flex-token/route.ts:56-64`; `web/components/FooterTelemetryStrip.tsx:74`; `.gitignore:101`; 851f5af3 deleted `data/flex_token_config.json` | With the config deleted and gitignored, `flex_token_check` returns `{"status": "skip"}` — `BaseHandler` has no `skip` special-case, so the cycle records a healthy heartbeat and the 25 h window stays green while checking nothing; `/api/flex-token` maps `ENOENT` to `should_warn: false, expired: false`. The token's 30/14/7/1-day reminders are permanently silent; the first symptom is a Flex 1012 outage across cash flows, TWR and the blotter simultaneously. Same "returned skip, reported healthy forever" class the cash-flow handler fixed at `cash_flow_sync.py:493-505`. |
| R-132 | P2 | `scripts/portfolio_performance.py:361-403 (:391), 490-527 (:505), 647-654`; `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/_throttle_backoff.py:37-39` | Three raw Flex paths with no `flex_embargo` import and no 1025 detection; the poll loops `continue` whenever `<FlexStatements` is absent — exactly what a 1025 error envelope looks like — so one invocation fires 1 SendRequest + up to 30 GetStatements against a locked token, each extending it. `_throttle_backoff.py` names this file as the known uncontrolled consumer; the range fixed the other three and left it. CLI-only today (hence P2), but one `python scripts/portfolio_performance.py` from a 7-day token-wide outage. |
| R-133 | P2 | `scripts/trade_blotter/blotter_service.py:160-222 (:219)`; `scripts/trade_blotter/cli.py:227` | f236e662 added `raise_if_blocked`/`record_lockout` to `flex_query.py` only. The second independent `FlexQueryFetcher` in `blotter_service.py` has no embargo check and no 1025 handling, and treats the presence of `FlexStatementResponse` — the **error** envelope — as *ready*, so a 1025/1012 body parses to zero `.//Trade` nodes and is returned as a successful empty execution list. |
| R-134 | P2 | `scripts/utils/flex_embargo.py:21, 101-102`; `scripts/cash_flow_sync.py:193-195`; `scripts/perf_twr_builder.py:286`; `scripts/trade_blotter/flex_query.py:112, 117, 160`; `scripts/api/server.py:5061-5068`; `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/cash_flow_sync.py:113` | The 1025 taxonomy is quadruplicated and the shared `is_lockout_code` has zero production callers; `LOCKOUT_DAYS` is duplicated as `LOCKOUT_EMBARGO_DAYS`. They already disagree: `flex_query.py` detects 1025 only when `Status != Success` (a bare `ErrorCode` falls to `RuntimeError`), `perf_twr_builder` only when `ReferenceCode` is absent, and `server.py` `is_throttled` still sets on `1001|1018|1019` (contradicting 436dcdc1: only 1018 is a rate limit) and omits 1025 — so the 5-minute soft 1001 renders the amber "don't manually retry" lozenge while the 7-day lockout falls to generic red. |
| R-135 | P2 | `web/components/CashFlowsSection.tsx:127-149 (:132)` | `if (!lastSyncedRelative) return null;` short-circuits before the `isThrottled`/`isErrored` branches, so a fresh environment (or truncated `cash_flows`) that hits a 1025 on its first pull shows an empty table with no error, no retry hint and no tone for the full 7 days. The failure state is visible only when a *prior success* exists. |
| R-136 | P2 | `scripts/api/subprocess.py:363-369, 428-434` vs `:351-356, 419-425`; slot release `:304, 374, 441` | 8ec3a2d7's cancel path: `proc.kill()` is unguarded (a child that exits between the `returncode is None` check and the signal raises `ProcessLookupError` out of the `except CancelledError`, breaking `wait_for`/TaskGroup cancellation semantics — the timeout branch guards this); `await proc.wait()` is unbounded, so a SIGKILLed child stuck in uninterruptible I/O holds the cancelled task and its subprocess slot indefinitely; and only the direct child is signalled (no `start_new_session` + `killpg`), so the comment's claim about bounding "the reserved order lane" holds only for single-process scripts. |
| R-137 | P2 | `scripts/newsfeed/push_media.js:16, 73-83, 97`; `scripts/newsfeed/mediaPermissions.js:5, 18-41` | e191c402's permission repair is a silent no-op on the default remote route: `localMediaDest` returns `null` for `radon@ib-gateway:/…`, so `ensurePublicMediaPermissions` never runs on the documented laptop→Hetzner path and nothing is logged. `rsync -a` implies `-p` so new files inherit the source mode, and `--ignore-existing` means every pre-fix 0600 image is never re-pushed — those stay 403 on media.radon.run permanently. When it *does* run, it chmods **every** regular file in an operator-supplied directory to 0644 with no extension allowlist (a stray `storageState`/cookie jar under a Caddy web root becomes world-readable), with an O(N) stat sweep per cycle over a never-pruned tree. |
| R-138 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_catalysts.py:76, 397, 452-457, 463-465, 469-475` | The FRED actual-overlay swallows every failure (`except Exception: cache[series_id] = None`) with no log and no health signal, so a revoked `FRED_API_KEY`, a 429 or an outage is indistinguishable from "not released yet" — the `actual` column stays blank forever (the sibling `_safe_call` 30 lines below logs for exactly this reason). And `if canonical(value) == canonical(row["prev"]): continue` discards a real print whenever it equals the prior one — UNRATE repeats month-over-month routinely, ICSA ties weekly — so a released, unchanged figure reads "no print". |
| R-139 | P2 | `web/lib/useSnapshotStaleness.ts:27, 30, 42` | `setTick` fires unconditionally every 30 s and `tick` is in the returned memo, so `WorkspaceShell` and every non-memoised child re-render every 30 s even when `lastSync` is null or fresh, in every open tab. No timer leak (cleared on unmount); cost only. |

### Audited, clean in this delta (walked and verified — silence ≠ clearance)

- **`ib_place_order.py` (1979a0d5) adds only `PhaseTimer` marks** — the permId poll, the REL-006 disconnect contract and the stdout-is-result-JSON rule are untouched; `timer.emit()` writes to stderr inside `finally` before `client.disconnect()`.
- **`exit_orders.py` ack discipline intact** — `_confirm_placement`, `_TERMINAL_FAILED_STATUSES`, the int-not-bool `_ack_perm_id` guard and `_cancel_unconfirmed` all run before any journal write; `_unrecorded_placements` is persisted (`:318-337`) and consumed in `_load_pending_orders` (`:124-134`); the identity-unresolved defer (`:409-410, 509-521`) fails safe when IB reports no readable price.
- **`/orders/replace` (`server.py:2582-2646`)** reserves the rate slot before the destructive cancel, runs limits + what-if before the first cancel, and re-reads the halt at the last instant. Only its error *rendering* is broken (R-090). `/orders/modify` (`:2692-2757`) is unchanged and correct.
- **`web/app/api/orders/modify/route.ts`** — demo blockade is still the first statement that can reach IB; `operatorOnly` + durable rate tier C; `normalizeCancelTargets` dedupes on permId-then-orderId; the replace payload rejects zero/non-finite limit and <2 legs.
- **`detectExecutedGroupStructure` (a86bc21c) is label-only** — feeds a description string, never a payload, price or gate. **`MobileOrderTicket` (6b89929d)** passes raw per-leg counts per the `ChainOrderLeg` contract and the risk layer re-derives `comboQuantity` as the GCD. `structureMatchesHeldCombo` is a strict bijection.
- **`journal_sync._contract_multiplier` (c5d7f294)** rejects `bool`, non-numeric, non-finite and `<= 0` and falls back to the sec_type default. **Backfill `absorb`** is idempotent under repeated delta re-scans (`Counter.__ior__`) — only the exec-id skip path leaks (R-088).
- **`writer.py` delta is indicator/cash-flow writers only** — chunked at `_PRICE_HISTORY_INSERT_CHUNK_ROWS`, parameterised, `ON CONFLICT(date|ts) DO UPDATE` idempotent; `upsert_cash_flow_rows` surfaces its dedupe-drop count. No order/journal write path touched; no money/health write moved off hrana.
- **`IBClient.get_positions`** correctly waits for `positionEndEvent` + finite `avgCost` rather than returning on the first `positionEvent`; `bind_event`'s `TypeError` path degrades to "no event" → full cap, the fail-safe direction.
- **`ib_realtime_server.js` / `wsTrust.js` untouched in range** except a test-only commit — subscription state machine, depth-ticket cap, reconnect ladder and health-row writing are byte-identical to the anchor.
- **UW budget (09a168a4)**: `record_hits` is flock-serialised, `_write_unlocked` is tmp+`replace` so unlocked readers cannot tear; `MAX_TRACKED_KEYS` bounds growth; 429 handling raises immediately on the daily-limit marker and retries non-daily 429 exactly once; `countedUwFetch` is fire-and-forget with a 3 s bound and cannot block the data path. (Two P2 edges filed as R-117-class follow-ups in the log: the history archive is a non-atomic rewrite under the lock, and `/uw/usage/record`'s unbounded `flock` runs on the default executor shared with `_bounded_pool_call`.)
- **Demo mirror retry mechanics (da31f6ae)** bounded at `maxAttempts` (env-validated 1..5), backoff capped, `INSERT OR REPLACE` + same-transaction prune so a retried write is idempotent. Only the purge is outside the ladder (R-097). `isTransientTursoError`'s bare `instanceof TypeError` is over-broad (retries programming errors as outages) — log follow-up.
- **Knowledge-ingest SQLITE_BUSY retry (2f03e46a)** is bounded (4 attempts) and double-write-safe (`_pre_filter` skips already-written docs; keyed upserts). Flat 1 s unjittered backoff cannot span the 6 s contention the commit itself measured — log follow-up.
- **LEAP zero-result path** preserves the last-good cache and exits 1 before any write — it is the *non-zero* path that lost the alarm (R-095).
- **`web/app/api/options/_shared.ts`** 503-vs-504 mapping is correct; its 50 s/40 s assumption is what R-096 breaks upstream.
- **Data-source priority holds** in every new fetcher: IB → UW → Yahoo; DXY reaches Yahoo only after IB's `DX`/`DXY` candidates fail and UW explicitly cannot serve it; `ivrank` and `trin` never call Yahoo.
- **All five new upserts idempotent on natural key**; `upsert_scan_snapshot` prunes to `SCAN_SNAPSHOT_KEEP=30` per service so TRIN's 108 snapshots/day are bounded even though its source tables are not; `ivrank`/`trin` history reads are keyset-paginated and horizon-bounded; `ensure_no_replica_for_writers()` precedes every new write path; all JSON caches are tmp+`os.replace`; migrations 0051-0054 each carry a PK, index and `schema_migrations` footer.
- **`TimeoutStartSec` fits every new timer interval** (`trin` 240 < 300 s; `vol-cone-intraday` 600 < 900 s); RTH/holiday/**half-day** gating in `fetch_trin` resolves early closes off the IBKR calendar cache.
- **Both watchdog catalogs agree** on every new service with matching `requires_ib`; only `trin`'s closed window is wrong (R-122).
- **`sync-scheduled-units` is correctly hardened root-side** — GitHub-origin pin, `HEAD == remote main tip`, allowlist + manifest + unit blob all via `cat-file blob` from the tip, control-plane refusal, digest verified on the staged temp, no start/stop/enable. `auto-sync-units.txt` scopes it to one non-control-plane unit. R-084 is the *other* verb.
- **`installed-units.sha256` is internally consistent** — all 101 present entries hash-match their `cloud/services/` file; only `radon-ivrank.*` (R-092) and the deliberately-gated `radon-llm-index.*` are absent.
- **REL-032 drift ratchet intact** — no-expiry entries never honoured, expired entries surface dated, unmatched entries raise `stale-allowlist:`; every entry added in the range carries `expires=`.
- **af734d83's P3 widening does not hide a repeated genuine failure** — gated on `Result == "signal"` and still requires a marker within `KILL_BEFORE_GREEN_FROZEN_CAP_SECS`; exit-code failures, signal kills with no marker, and kills older than 24 h all still page.
- **`wait_for_gateway_ready` still fires first** in `stop_services_for_transition` before `stop-clean`; `radon-health.service` remains bootstrap-owned and excluded from `install-units`.
- **Responder (REL-030) fail-closed defaults intact**; rerun requires `autoship_enabled()` and refuses during a deploy transition; the polkit grant is `start`/`reset-failed` only on a fixed 24-unit table with no money-path unit; sandbox not loosened toward secrets (`ReadOnlyPaths=/home/radon`, `InaccessiblePaths=…/radon-cloud`, `GROK_PAGE_NO_DOTENV=1` unchanged; only `ReadWritePaths=…/.grok` added).
- **CI gates strengthened, not weakened** — five money-path e2e specs added to the P0 subset; `needs:`, coverage floor and deploy trigger unchanged; `CODEOWNERS` newly added.
- **`run_signals_refresh.sh` exit-code discipline is correct** — per-scan failures counted independently, exits 1, direct fallback refused on anything but `curl 7` so an accepted in-flight scan is never duplicated. The timeout arithmetic is the defect (R-093).
- **Flex/TWR**: a lockout does not leave the performance page stale-labelled-fresh (NAV bounded to flows coverage, `NAV_STALE` floors status); a partial Flex fetch cannot publish a valid-looking TWR (`FlowSet.failed → degraded, twr: null`); poll loops are all bounded in *count*; `perf_twr_builder` refuses `FlexStatementResponse` as ready; the backoff shrink (f826e719) cannot hammer a throttled token (ladder monotonic, reset only on success); `subperiod_return` BOD switch guards every zero/non-finite input; `IMPLAUSIBLE_ALPHA`/`FLOW_CONVENTION` parity holds between `twr_gates.py` and `performanceTwr.ts`; one Flex document per run (`flows_query_id` collapses to NAV id); daily-pull move 17:00 → 08:00 ET is DST-correct.
- **`/api/service-health` probe bearer (83de48e3)** — SHA-256 constant-time compare, unset token fails closed, scope limited to the two-entry `PROBE_BEARER_API_ROUTES` allowlist re-verified in the route; exposed data is `service_health` with `scrubSecrets` on `last_error`. No new bypass.
- **Web hooks in range** — `useOrders` (in-flight guard, `AbortSignal.timeout` on both verbs), `RouteRefreshContext` (pathname-only key, no timers), `useMarketHours`, `InfoTooltip`, `ScannerModeTabs`, `Sidebar`, `useCatalysts`/`catalystUpcoming` all clean up their timers/listeners/observers on unmount.
- **P&L gate cannot blank a real RTH session** — the holiday table has full closures only (half-days stay live) and a missing year falls back to weekday-only, i.e. fails open. The gate's defect is the *inverse* (R-107).
- **`subprocess.py` slot accounting** — claim/release balanced on every return path including the pre-claim early return.

## Delta audit 2026-08-22 (second pass)

Range: `c529c92a..HEAD` (`c0e7e4f5`) — the same range as the section above.

**Why there are two sections for one date.** Two rounds of the Saturday audit ran
against this delta. The first (R-084…R-139, backlog REL-039…REL-052) scoped its agents
to the 50 post-remediation commits `ecc6985f..c0e7e4f5`; this one scoped seven walks by
subsystem ownership across the full 203-commit range. The first round is the audit of
record and is left exactly as written, per the frozen-contract rule. This section carries
only what the second pass found that the first did not: 58 findings of 81 raised, after
dropping 23 that the first round had already filed. Where a finding narrows or builds on
one of theirs, the row says so explicitly.

**One severity disagreement, recorded not re-filed.** The first round's R-125 (four
indicator routes compute `maxAgeMs` and then serve on `result.ok`, discarding
`result.fresh`) is rated P2 there. The second pass rated the same defect P0 on the TRIN
instance, because it composes with the writer half: `_nothing_to_persist` refuses only a
totally empty run, so with IB sampling dead `persist_result` still upserts `scan_time = now`
and records `ok` every 5 minutes, and `useTrin.ts:15` maps the section clock to
`data.scan_time`. The operator therefore reads a tradeable `IN ZONE`/`ELEVATED` regime call
off a frozen bar behind an advancing clock, and the mobile grid shows no timestamp at all
(`TrinPanel.tsx:170-186`). No new R-number: the finding is R-125. REL-053 below carries
the writer half, which R-125's own scope does not.

Method: seven parallel read-only category walks (safety/money path, state/persistence,
connectivity/sessions, resources/quota, error handling/degradation, control plane/
observability, auth perimeter), each handed an explicit file list plus the commits to
trace. Every claim cites file:line in current code. Deduped against R-001…R-083, against
the shipped REL-001…REL-038 fixes, and against R-084…R-139 above. Every P0 and every
severity change was re-verified in the lead context before landing; two severities were
raised on that check (R-141 and the R-125 note above), and six agent findings were merged
into three because two walks reached the same defect from different directions.

**Ledger anchor.** `c529c92a` resolves (`c529c92ac11294c381c14d56261a635a68c297f6`),
verified with `git rev-parse --verify` before scoping.

**Standing sweeps — all hold, one gap.** Halt chokepoints present (`ib_place_order.py:240`,
`exit_orders.py:433`, `server.py:2379/2414/2585/2618/2701`, `ib_execute.py:443`);
`_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` intact (`server.py:4678`, enforced `:4787`/`:4865`); order
limits wired (`ib_place_order.py:253`, `server.py:2371/2416/2602/2718`,
`workflow/nodes.py:152`); exit-order ack poll intact (`exit_orders.py:187-204`);
`upsert_daemon_state` still on the bounded hrana transport. All nine permanent drill
suites present. No NEW `placeOrder`/`place_order` call site bypasses the chokepoints —
the two that do (`exit_order_service.py:398`, `ib_order_manage.py:244`) are pre-existing
R-038/R-039. All five new indicators plus `vol-cone` and `vol-cone-intraday` are in BOTH
watchdog catalogs, and every new unit carries `RADON_DB_NO_REPLICA=1`, `TimeoutStartSec`
and `StartLimitBurst`. **The gap:** `radon-perf-twr`, whose timer this delta installed, is
in neither catalog and writes no `service_health` row at all — R-160. The sweeps are run
in the lead context precisely because no scoped agent's file list contains both a unit and
the two catalogs.

**Zero rule-7 violations.** All five new fetchers were walked source-leg by source-leg.
`fetch_credit_spread.py:283-300` and `fetch_iei_hyg.py:243-272` are IB → UW (SPX/DXY
excluded) → Yahoo, and `_take` only ever passes still-missing tickers down the ladder, so
Yahoo is never called on an IB hit. `fetch_ivrank.py:493-568` is IB → UW → cached-as-stale,
no Yahoo leg. `fetch_trin.py:304-330` is IB snapshot → StockCharts for daily history only.
`fetch_vixcor.py:437-456` is Cboe-only for VIX/COR3M, the authoritative publisher, which
rule 7 explicitly permits.

**Standing candidates re-triaged — all still open.** NF-1: no bankroll/net-liq term exists
anywhere in `order_limits.py`, so the documented Gate-3 2.5% cap is still unenforced
server-side. NF-3: `SingleLegOrderTicket.tsx:452, 461` gate submit on form validity and
`loading` only. NF-4: `journal_rehydrate.py:118-126` `_group_key` still carries no date
component. R-037 (`RADON_AUTH_DISABLED` unrefused in hetzner mode, REL-021b remainder) also
still open — `server.py:635-637` returns `call_next` with no `RADON_MODE` check.

### Second-pass findings table

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-140 | P0 | `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:634-731` (`install_manifest_units`), `:271-275`, `:14-36`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:872, 884-888`; `cloud/config/installed-units.sha256:60, 63` | `install_manifest_units` walks the WHOLE manifest, skipping only `CONTROL_PLANE_SOURCES` and `unit_is_excluded` (`radon-beta-*`, `radon-ib-gateway.service`, preheld). `radon-nextjs.service` (manifest:63) and `radon-newsfeed.service` (manifest:60) are in neither list, so a PR that edits them and bumps the hash now has an unattended deploy `mv` them into `/etc/systemd/system` and `daemon-reload` — while services are stopped, immediately before `start_services_after_transition`. There is no `systemd-analyze verify` anywhere in the helper (grep count 0, versus 2 in `bootstrap-control-plane.sh`) and no known-good snapshot/restore, unlike `publish_caddy` which does `caddy validate` and restores on failure. `daemon-reload` never fails on a malformed unit body, so a hash-matching but broken `radon-nextjs.service` installs cleanly and Next.js will not start. Rollback does not recover it: `restore_release_backup` does `git reset --hard` plus artifact moves and never reinstalls unit files, so recovery restarts onto the same broken unit and exits 3 "manual intervention required". `app.radon.run` is down until a human SSHes as root. Extends the first round's R-084 (same verb, the trust-source half) and R-091/R-094 (which cover the control-plane timer gap and persistent-timer firing): this is the missing `systemd-analyze verify`, the missing unit snapshot/restore, and the fact that two core non-control-plane services are in scope. |

The only code in the repo that writes an `exit_orders` section is the legacy `exit_order_service.py`, which writes `status: "PENDING_MANUAL"` (`:132`) and key `target_price` (`:180`), and writes no `contract_spec` at all — a repo-wide grep for `contract_spec` outside tests returns three hits, all READS inside `exit_orders.py`. Every candidate therefore falls to the `no_contract_spec` skip at `:667-673`. Automated exit protection does not exist, while `service_name = "exit-orders"` is registered in both watchdog catalogs and reports healthy. Every hardening layer applied to this file across the delta (REL-002 ack discipline, REL-003 durable guard and broker cross-check, the halt check) protects a path with no producer, and `RELIABILITY_AUDIT.md:157` still models "silent exit-order drop" as a live P0 scenario. **Severity raised P1→P0 on lead verification.** |
| R-142 | P0 | `scripts/api/ib_gateway.py:770-787`; contrast `scripts/api/ib_pool.py:32-42`; consumers `ib_gateway.py:1111, 1331`, `scripts/api/server.py:378` | `_do_probe` is dispatched with a bare `asyncio.to_thread`. `ib.connect()` reaches `ib_insync.util.getLoop()`, which raises `RuntimeError` in any non-main thread with no loop installed; the `except Exception: return (False, [])` at `:779-780` swallows it and reports `authenticated=False`. `ib_pool._connect_in_thread` knows this and installs a loop first, with the comment "ib_insync needs an event loop in the connecting thread. When called from asyncio.to_thread(), the thread has no loop by default" — `_do_probe` did not inherit it, so the outcome is thread-affinity-dependent. Three load-bearing consumers, none self-healing: `restart_ib_gateway` records a SUCCESSFUL restart as `awaiting_2fa`, arms the backoff and skips `release_2fa_push_lock`, so the 2FA push lock stays held and REL-017 placement pre-flight refuses orders; `recover_stuck_pool` never calls `pool.reconnect_roles`; and `server.py:378` treats it as a genuine 2FA wait so the 3-strike self-heal ladder never counts a failure. Persists until an operator restarts `radon-api`. |
| R-143 | P0 | `scripts/api/routes/historical.py:99, 105-107`; `scripts/api/ib_pool.py:235-248` | A timed-out `_bounded_pool_call` permanently burns a thread from the SHARED default executor, and nothing caps quarantined workers. On timeout the wedged `asyncio.to_thread` worker is shielded and reaped, and `pool.retire` fires a SECOND `asyncio.to_thread(client.disconnect)`; neither can be cancelled, as the design comment at `historical.py:74-78` states. Both run on the default executor — no `set_default_executor` exists anywhere in `scripts/api/` (grep count 0) — so `max_workers = min(32, cpu_count+4)`, single digits on a 2-vCPU Hetzner box. That same executor serves Clerk JWKS verification, Turso writes, the IB port probes, pool connects, and `pool_order_manage` cancel/place. After N wedged data-role calls every `await asyncio.to_thread(...)` in FastAPI queues forever, cancel and replace included. No counter, no ceiling, no alarm. Requires a `radon-api` restart. |
| R-144 | P0 | `scripts/run_leap_refresh.sh:109-119`; `scripts/run_garch_refresh.sh:104-114`; contrast `scripts/run_signals_refresh.sh:158-163` | The LEAP and GARCH wrappers treat every non-2xx identically and fall through to a DIRECT scanner invocation. A `502 Subprocess capacity exhausted` from `_claim_subprocess_slot` — the one response that means the box is already running too many scans — therefore launches `leap_scanner_uw.py` / `garch_convergence.py` outside `MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBPROCESSES` and outside the FastAPI cooldown, as does a `curl -m 3610` timeout while FastAPI's child may still be mid-flight. `run_signals_refresh.sh` got exactly this guard ("A timeout or HTTP response means FastAPI may have accepted the scan… not launching duplicate"); leap and garch did not. This has already run in production: `docs/incident-runbook.md` records it for 2026-08-18/19/20. One duplicate `largecaps` run is 518 names × 3 UW GETs ≈ 1,554 extra requests, ~3.9% of the daily cap, and garch fires 3×/trading day. It also makes an hour-long scanner invisible to the REL-023 admission counter that the reserved order slot's assumptions rest on. |
| R-145 | P1 | `scripts/api/server.py:2716-2723`; `scripts/order_limits.py:249-256`; contrast `server.py:2602` and `ib_place_order.py:253-257` | `/orders/modify` enforces only the contract-quantity cap. `check_quantity_limit` hardcodes `{"type": "option", "quantity": quantity, "limitPrice": 0}`, so `order_notional()` returns `None` (premium is 0) and `combo_max_loss()` returns `None` (type is not `combo`) — both the notional and max-loss branches are skipped. `newPrice` is bounded only by `> 0`. Modifying a working 1-lot BAG with a short 195 put leg to `newQuantity: 500` is accepted, and the assignment exposure the place path would have measured is never computed. REL-005's contract named modify as a chokepoint for max qty AND max notional; only qty landed. The route already holds `new_price` and can read the working order's type from the snapshot it fetches. |
| R-146 | P1 | `web/lib/positionUtils.ts:237-243, 282-293, 304-312`; consumers `PositionTable.tsx:383-384`, `ticker-detail/PositionTab.tsx:255`, `mobile/MobilePositionList.tsx:120` | `3b5d05ff` replaced `isFullLossDebit` — which admitted only `risk_profile === "defined"`, a single long stock leg, or an all-long option position — with `isNetDebitPaid`, which admits ANY position with `resolveEntryCost(pos) > 0` except a single-leg short. There is no `risk_profile` check on the new path. A 1×2 call ratio (buy 1 @ $10, sell 2 @ $4.50) has `resolveEntryCost` = +$100 and unbounded upside risk; Return % is computed on a $100 denominator and published as "Return on debit paid · exact". A deliberate refusal ("Return unavailable: no verified capital basis") became a four-digit percentage on the position table, the ticker-detail tab and mobile. The label is true of the debit and false of the risk it is presented as a return on. |
| R-147 | P1 | `scripts/lib/twr_math.py:981`; contrast the sibling at `:308`; publication `scripts/perf_twr_builder.py:1344-1351, 1392-1397`; web gating `web/lib/performanceData.ts:807-818` vs `:821-828` | `modified_dietz` guards only `if denominator == 0`. `subperiod_return`, in the same module for the same `B + C` quantity, guards `if denominator <= 0` with the docstring "a period cannot start with no capital". A weighted outflow exceeding `begin_nav` — plausible when a Flex NAV document double-counts a transfer across its three sections, the hazard CLAUDE.md flags for `IB_FLEX_NAV_QUERY_ID` — yields a negative denominator and a sign-inverted return. Separately, `twr.cum_return` and `twr.annualized` are both plausibility-gated but `mwr.period_return` and `mwr.annualized` pass through `gatedValueFrom` with no bar at all, so the MWR/IRR card can render an absurd or inverted figure beside a TWR that was correctly suppressed. |
| R-148 | P1 | `scripts/api/ib_pool.py:380-399` | `_PoolContext.__aenter__` acquires the role lock at `:381`, then awaits `_is_live` (up to `LIVENESS_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SECS`) and `_reconnect` (up to a 5 s connect plus a stale disconnect). `release()` appears only on the three explicit error branches — there is no `try/finally` and no `except BaseException`. A `CancelledError` at either await (request-task cancellation, an enclosing `wait_for`, shutdown) propagates with the lock still held, and `asyncio.Lock` has no owner and no timeout, so every subsequent `pool.acquire(role)` blocks forever. For `orders`/`sync` that is the money path. No watchdog covers it — `_pool_disconnected_roles` reads `is_connected`, which is still true. Requires a `radon-api` restart. |
| R-149 | P1 | `web/lib/useSnapshotStaleness.ts:35, 37` | `if (!lastSync) return { isStale: false, ... }`, and the same for an unparseable date — so the total-blackout case reports FRESH. `lastSync` is null exactly when the producer has never written a snapshot this session, when the GET returned an error body, or when `extractTimestamp` yields null. The stale pill stays hidden, `useAutoSyncOnStale` is gated on stale so it never fires, and `syncLabel` degrades to the reassuring "Awaiting first sample". A new instance of the R-061 honesty class in a file this delta rewrote; R-061/REL-035 covered only the relay. Adjacent to the first round's R-105, which covers a failed sync returning 200; this is the distinct case where `lastSync` is null and staleness reports FRESH. |

When `remaining < 2000` the run holds, prints one stderr line, then falls through to `_write_intraday_db_cache`, which unconditionally calls `record_service_health("vol-cone-intraday", "ok")` at `:483`. The watchdog window is 45 min open / 3 days closed and is keyed on row freshness, so a fresh `ok` every 15 minutes means the staleness detector can never fire for a quota outage. `payload["market_status"]` is also stamped `"closed"` on the budget-hold branch — a factually wrong label while the market is open. The VOL CONE tab silently reverts to last night's cone for the rest of the session behind a green chip. This is the R-070 class, in a job that shipped after REL-036 closed it for theta/strength. |
| R-151 | P1 | `scripts/fetch_vol_cone.py:93` vs `scripts/utils/uw_budget.py:26`; timer `cloud/services/radon-vol-cone-intraday.timer:9`; caps `fetch_vol_cone.py:91` | Two brakes on one UW budget with incompatible thresholds. `should_block_universe_scan` refuses theta/strength/discover at 20,000 used, reserving the top half; `_INTRADAY_UW_FLOOR = 2_000` lets `vol-cone-intraday` keep spending until only 2,000 remain, i.e. freely consume the entire 20,000–38,000 band the universe brake exists to protect. No reservation, no priority, no per-caller cap. Rate arithmetic: the timer fires `09..16` at `:00,:15,:30,:45` Mon–Fri, gated to ~26 spending cycles per weekday; each cycle issues one `fetch_greeks` per pair up to `_INTRADAY_PAIR_CAP = 80` plus one `fetch_spot` per unique ticker, and the 60 s cache TTL carries nothing across a 15-minute cycle — 81–160 requests/cycle, 2,106–4,160 UW requests/weekday, entirely new in this delta. Once intraday crosses 20,000, theta-harvester and strength-confirmation write `uw-budget-blocked` rows until the 20:00 ET reset while the job that spent it runs on. |

The unit also sets no `RADON_UW_CALLER`, so even counted it would be unattributed. On a 429 the per-date `except Exception` swallows it and immediately tries the next date. |
| R-153 | P1 | `scripts/db/migrations/0050_host_metrics_disk_pct.sql:13, 15`; runner `scripts/db/migrate.py:133-150`; `scripts/db/migrate.ts:108-126` | Migration 0050 is the only real `ALTER TABLE` in 54 migrations (0003's hit is the comment warning against exactly this). The runner executes statements one at a time and records the version only after the loop, so a connection drop or a kill between the committed `ADD COLUMN disk_pct` and the version row leaves version 50 unrecorded. The next run re-applies 0050 → `duplicate column name: disk_pct` → the `raise` at `migrate.py:141` aborts `main()`, so migrations 0051 credit_spread, 0052 ivrank, 0053 iei_hyg and 0054 trin never apply. `migrate.py` is `radon-api`'s `ExecStartPre`, so the API then fails to start on every boot — a full control-plane outage plus four indicator writers failing on missing tables, until hand-repaired. `0003_phase2_snapshots.sql:61-62` codified the rule this file breaks. |
| R-154 | P1 | `scripts/fetch_ivrank.py:121-135, 155-176, 530-533, 588-621`; writer `scripts/db/writer.py:1265-1273` | The ivrank bad-print repair is clobbered by the next IB incremental fetch. `merge_history` refuses only `uw`-over-`ib` (`:132-133`); the reverse — the daily IB "1 M" fetch restating the same bad bar as `source='ib'` — overwrites the stored repaired `uw` row. `repair_outliers` then re-detects it and re-calls the UW lookup; if UW raises or returns `None` the loop `continue`s, `_rows_changed` is still `True`, and `_write_db` upserts the bad IB print back over the good value. Once the date ages out of the 1-month IB window it freezes at whatever the last run wrote. A 0.2443 print against a ~0.12 series sets the `max` of the 252-session window, distorting `iv_rank` and the SUPPRESSED/NORMAL/ELEVATED/EXTREME label for every one of the next 252 sessions. |
| R-155 | P1 | `scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/expiry_sweep.py:414, 419`; `last_date` built at `:272-274` | The R-074 `>=` guard now matches the OPENING fill. `last_date` is `max(payload["date"][:10])` across ALL journal rows for the contract, openers included. The guard was widened from `>` to `>=` so a closing fill booked on expiry day counts as handled — but an opening fill booked on expiry day now satisfies the same condition, `_is_guarded` returns `True`, the sweep skips, and the expiration is never written. Same over-match on the executed-orders leg at `:419`. Every 0DTE that expires worthless stays permanently open in the journal: `/journal`, `/portfolio` and open-basis all carry a phantom position and realized P&L never books the loss. The sweep is the only mechanism that would; recoverable only by hand. |
| R-156 | P1 | `scripts/ci/check_demo_isolation.py:42-75` (esp. `:53-60`); claimed shipped at `docs/demo-environment.md:27, 83`; consumers `web/app/api/{portfolio,performance,blotter,journal}/route.ts`; resolution `web/lib/dbExecute.ts:17, 51` → `web/lib/db.ts:380-386` | Two independent failures in the guard that is the only layer keeping demo-tier users off the operator's account figures. (a) A repo-wide grep for `check_demo_isolation` returns the module, its own unit test and two doc references — no workflow, deploy script or preflight invokes it. (b) Even if invoked it never asserts that the PROD var `TURSO_DB_URL` is not the production database: `:53-60` fires only when `prod_url == demo_url`, so a demo env carrying the real `TURSO_DB_URL` alongside a distinct `TURSO_DEMO_DB_URL` returns zero violations. `getDemoDb()` is used only by `/api/admin/demo-users`; every account route goes through `dbExecute` → `getDb()` → `TURSO_DB_URL`. On demo.radon.run `ALLOWED_USER_IDS` is absent by design and the account reads carry no `operatorOnly`, so DB isolation is the single layer between a trial user and the operator's real positions, TWR curve, NAV and blotter. `docs/demo-environment.md:27` states a CI isolation guard "rejects any demo deploy whose env carries a prod TURSO_DB_URL"; it does not exist. |

Related, same file: `report()` swallows every `gh` failure, and both setup scripts set `fail=1` on a failed `gh auth status` but never exit, so an expired token makes the dead-man a silent no-op indistinguishable from "the runner did not fire". Distinct from the first round's R-116, which covers the shared-clone locking: this is `testing_weekend.sh`'s top-level ERR trap posting a false CRASHED dead-man on every failed or timed-out run, plus both setup scripts not exiting on a failed `gh auth status`. |
| R-186 | P2 | `web/app/api/service-health/route.ts:118-125, 143-158, 172-181` | The probe bearer now returns the full operator diagnostic payload, unrate-limited. Before `83de48e3` a bearer passed middleware and then 401'd in-route; now `probeAuthorized` short-circuits `requireRouteAccess` entirely, so one shared static token yields every service's `last_error` (writer-supplied diagnostic text, only `scrubSecrets`-filtered at `:144`), `error_summary`, and both attempt timestamps — the same body a Clerk operator gets. It also skips the `ALLOWED_USER_IDS` check and the `RADON_REQUIRE_OPERATOR_ALLOWLIST=1` interlock this same delta made deploy-required. `requireRouteAccess(request)` is called with no options, so no rate limit applies on either path. Verified correct: the comparison is constant-time over SHA-256 digests, the middleware exemption is exact-pathname, and an unset token fails closed. |
| R-187 | P2 | `scripts/watchdog/services.py:186-193`; `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:285-286`; `cloud/services/radon-signals-refresh.timer:11`; `scripts/run_signals_refresh.sh:96-102` | `theta-harvester` and `strength-confirmation` carry a 4-day freshness window against an HOURLY timer — a 96× slack. R-068 chose 4 days because the wrapper exits 0 on holidays and off-hours without writing a row, so a tight window would false-page every Monday; the consequence is that a dead `radon-signals-refresh.timer` reads fresh for four days and the Top-candidates panel serves a frozen snapshot for up to 96 hourly slots before anything fires. The fix is to make the wrapper heartbeat on its skip path, then tighten both windows. |
| R-188 | P2 | `cloud/config/auto-sync-units.txt:1-4`; `cloud/tests/test_sync_scheduled_units.py:162-184`; `scripts/grok_page_responder.py:127-128, 353-358` | `auto-sync-units.txt` has no membership ratchet, and `install-units` needs no allowlist at all. The only test validates each entry's shape, non-control-plane status and manifest hash — nothing bounds the list's growth or requires a second sign-off, while the grok responder can `git push origin main` directly with `GROK_PAGE_AUTOPUSH=1`. More importantly the tight allowlist is not where the blast radius is: per R-140, `install-units` installs the entire manifest minus control-plane, so `auto-sync-units.txt` documents a contract ~95 units narrower than the deploy actually has. |
| R-189 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_trin.py:169-180` vs `:269-272` | TRIN treats a NYSE generated index's bid/ask as a spread in one function and as two distinct counts in another. `_bid_ask` encodes the actual IB convention for these contracts — `adv, dec = bid, ask` for AD-NYSE, `up_vol, down_vol = bid, ask` for VOL-NYSE: two unrelated quantities, not a quote around a price. `extract_index_value` nonetheless falls back to `(bid + ask) / 2.0` for whichever contract it is handed, TRIN-NYSE included. If TRIN-NYSE follows its siblings' packing (the precedent the comment at `:170-171` cites) and `last`/`close` are both empty, the stored TRIN is the mean of two unrelated counts — a large positive finite number that clears `_positive_finite` and is written as a legitimate sample, dragging MA(10) far above `ZONE_HIGH = 1.50` and pinning `state: "elevated"`. Latent rather than active (needs both `last` and `close` unpriceable), but there is no sanity band on the stored value. |
| R-190 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_credit_spread.py:305-306, 383-384`; `scripts/fetch_iei_hyg.py:273, 361-362` | IEI/HYG and CREDIT lose per-leg provenance, so a mixed IB+Yahoo ratio is stored indistinguishably from an all-IB one. The cascade tracks a per-ticker `sources` dict, then `combine_source` collapses it to a sorted set-join before it reaches the payload, and the persisted row tuple carries no source column. A run where IB serves IEI and Yahoo serves HYG yields `source: "ib+yahoo"` with no way to attribute. The ratio and the divergence are computed across two providers with different close conventions and the record does not say which; forensics on a suspect session are impossible after the fact. |
| R-191 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_ivrank.py:75-88, 104-118, 425, 444-445` | `pct_window` never returns `None` where `rank_window` does, so a degenerate window emits `iv_pct = 0.0` and unlocks the 1-year low/high block. `rank_window` correctly returns `None` when `high == low`; `pct_window` on the same window returns `0.0` (nothing is strictly below the current value). `build_current` then computes `has_rank = cur["iv_rank"] is not None or cur["iv_pct"] is not None` — the `or` makes `0.0` sufficient — and publishes `iv_1y_low` / `iv_1y_high` off a window the ranker just declared unmeasurable. `iv_pct: 0.0` reads as "1M IV is below every one of the trailing 252 sessions", the cheapest-premium signal the indicator can print, beside `iv_rank: null`. Contrived for SPY (needs 252 identical closes), so filed for the inconsistency between the two window functions rather than for likelihood. |
| R-192 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_vixcor.py:230-238, 493-500`; `scripts/fetch_ivrank.py:369-370, 621-622` | A failed Turso row upsert still writes `data/*.json` and exits 0, diverging the two stores silently. `_write_db` wraps the whole writer block — `upsert_*_rows`, `upsert_scan_snapshot` AND `record_service_health` — in one `except Exception`, so if the row upsert throws (Hrana 502, "stream not found") the snapshot and the heartbeat that follow it never execute either, the exception goes to stderr, and `run()` proceeds to `persist_json(payload)` unconditionally and returns 0. Systemd records success. Turso holds day N-1, the JSON holds day N, and routes prefer the DB. No heartbeat is written, so the staleness banner is the only thing that eventually notices — after crossing its own threshold, with no error text to explain why. Both files' docstrings state the heartbeat runs "every cycle"; on this path it does not. |
| R-193 | P2 | `web/app/api/credit-spread/route.ts:53-58`; `web/app/api/iei-hyg/route.ts:45-50`; `web/app/api/trin/route.ts:44-50`; `web/app/api/vol-cone/route.ts:57-62`; contrast `web/app/api/vixcor/route.ts:75` | The same four routes omit the `isDegraded` guard, so a fresher degraded row can outrank an older real one. `pickFresherSource` (`dbFirstRead.ts:157-177`) prefers a healthy source over a fresher degraded one only when `isDegraded` is supplied; `vixcor` and `ivrank` wire `isDegraded: isMissingPayload` with an explicit rationale, the four newer routes do not (grep count 0 each). A `missing:true` heartbeat row written to Turso after a failed run then beats a genuinely populated disk snapshot on timestamp alone, degrading a recoverable "DB row is a stub, disk still has real data" situation to an empty panel. Visible rather than wrong, but the asymmetry is unexplained and will be copied by the next indicator. |
| R-194 | P2 | `web/app/api/vixcor/route.ts:25-36`; `web/app/api/ivrank/route.ts:25-35, 75`; `web/lib/trin.ts:52-59`; `web/lib/ieiHyg.ts:47-54` | Stale collapse wipes all provenance, so "feed died 3 days ago" is indistinguishable from "job never ran". The two routes that DO gate on `fresh` replace the stale payload with a constant whose `scan_time`, `as_of` and `status` are all `null`, discarding the `result.timestampMs` `dbFirstRead` already computed and returned. The panel then renders `SectionEmptyState` "Waiting for the radon-trin sampler" — identical copy for a never-provisioned indicator and a three-day-dead feed. The operator cannot triage from the UI and needs journald to distinguish. |
| R-195 | P2 | `web/lib/vixcor.ts:254-258`, rendered at `web/components/VixCorPanel.tsx:138` | `parentLagCopy` renders hardcoded cadence copy on the unknown-lag branch. `const lag = lagSessions ?? 0;` collapses `null` (the job could not determine parent lag) into `0` (parent is current), and both return the literal `"DAILY SERIES SINCE 2006-01"` — a hardcoded cadence claim, which the CLAUDE.md UI Copy Rules forbid. The function's own docstring at `:250-253` claims it is "derived from the payload's session count rather than any hardcoded cadence copy". The operator reads a confident "daily series, up to date" sub-label on a panel whose parent COR3M lag is unknown. |
| R-196 | P2 | `web/lib/regimeRail.ts:94-110, 116-118`; rendered at `web/components/RegimeRail.tsx:83` | The rail's "N ELEVATED" summary is computed over 22 tabs but only 4 can ever be flagged. `elevatedCount` filters `REGIME_TABS` (22 entries), but `buildRailStatuses` only ever emits `cri`, `cor`, `vcg` and `gex`; the five indicators added in this delta are registered as tabs and have zero status producers, so they are silently counted as calm. The header reads `22 INDICATORS · 0 ELEVATED` while TRIN is `IN ZONE` and IEI/HYG is at a new 52-week high. The docstring at `:89-93` ("Missing data yields no entry — the rail renders an honest neutral row") is honest per-row and dishonest in the aggregate. |
| R-197 | P2 | `web/lib/creditSpread.ts:63-74, 78` | `classifyRegime` returns a real regime for missing inputs: null or non-finite `spxRet`/`hygRet` returns `"coupled"` — a benign, tradeable regime — rather than `null`, and `regimeColor` then paints it `var(--positive)`. The web-side twin of R-161. Currently reached only from `web/tests/credit-spread-panel.test.tsx`, so latent — but it is a lying default sitting one import away from the panel, and becomes a P0 the moment the panel derives its own regime instead of reading `current.regime` off the payload. |

### Audited, clean (second pass)

Areas the second pass walked and cleared, beyond the first round's list:

- **`scripts/ib_sync.py`** — the `1979a0d5` event-driven waits (`:149-156, 848-874, 891-896, 960-971, 1610-1618`) all bind through `IBClient.wait_until`, hard-capped at the prior sleep budget, and every `bind_event` precedes its `req*` call, so there is no lost-wakeup race. The residual issues are the predicate quality (first round R-089) and the ignored return value (first round R-118), not the wait structure. The historical path is the one exception, first round R-117.
- **`scripts/api/subprocess.py`** — the REL-023 order-lane reservation is correct and atomic (no await between check and increment). The exploit is in the wrappers (R-144), not here.
- **`scripts/db/writer.py`** — all six new upserts use 400-row chunked multi-row INSERTs with `ON CONFLICT(<pk>) DO UPDATE`, genuinely idempotent on re-run; `upsert_scan_snapshot` still self-prunes to 30 rows per service; `upsert_journal_entry` and `record_service_health` remain on the bounded Hrana transport.
- **`scripts/knowledge/ingest.py`** — the 4-attempt retry re-runs the whole `ingest_source`, but `_load_existing:190-204` re-reads each attempt and `_pre_filter:207-223` skips already-written docs, so a replay after a client-side-ambiguous write neither duplicates rows nor re-spends the distill/embed budget. `_vanished_keys:265-284` refuses to prune on an empty fetch.
- **`scripts/rehash_position_execution_facts.py`** — `--dry-run` default, keyset on the full PK triple (R-077's row-value fix is correct, no OFFSET), updates only `payload_sha256`, idempotent, pytest write guard at `:60-66`.
- **`scripts/backfill_journal_from_executed_orders.py`** — the `written_since` delta re-check merges into the cached coverage rather than replacing it, so the guard set is a superset of the old full rescan; the change can only over-skip, never over-insert. (The fingerprint-accounting defect is first round R-088.)
- **`scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/journal_sync.py`** — `_contract_multiplier:1017-1036` correctly rejects `"0"`, negatives, non-finite and `bool`, falling back to the sec_type default rather than propagating a zeroed basis.
- **`scripts/lib/checkpoint.py`** — the R-073 fixes are sound: `_atomic_write` fsyncs fd then parent dir, `_read_state` preserves a malformed file as `.corrupt-<ts>` and rebuilds from the WAL, WAL-before-state ordering holds. Only `_tail_findings` was missed (R-177).
- **`scripts/utils/price_cache.py`** — the R-072 quarantine sweep is correct; `_is_quarantined` matches the naming and the 24 h forensic window is honoured. Only the concurrency placement is cited (R-174).
- **`scripts/utils/presets.py`** — the `VIRTUAL_UNIONS` refactor is behaviour-preserving; the traversal guard `_resolve_preset_path:150-162` still runs before any union dispatch.
- **`scripts/host_metrics_sampler.py`** — `disk_pct` threads cleanly through `ROW_COLUMNS`; `read_disk_pct` returns `None` on `OSError` matching the `loop_lag_ms` NULL convention; `prune_old_rows` is hourly, bounded, best-effort.
- **`scripts/watchdog/grouping.py:207-244, 301-375`** — the R-056 warmup ceiling is sound; counter-storage failure fails toward paging.
- **`scripts/watchdog/external_probe.py:36-42, 76-86, 199-219`** — the R-057 stranded-journal bound is implemented correctly. It is `units.py` that never inherited it (R-157).
- **`cloud/scripts/drift_audit.py`** — the R-058 ratchet is correct end to end; expired and no-expiry entries both surface. The residual gaps are the missing `is-enabled` dimension (R-184) and the reconciliation behaviour (first round R-113).
- **`cloud/config/polkit/50-radon-services.rules`** — rule 1 returns `NOTHING` for non-gateway units so rule 2 is reachable; rule 2 is exact-name plus `start`/`reset-failed` only, pinned to `RERUNNABLE_ONESHOT_UNITS` by test.
- **`cloud/config/sudoers.d/radon-deploy`** — eight fixed invocations, no wildcards, no argument-injection surface. The escalation in the first round's R-084 is what one of those verbs does, not how it is granted.
- **`scripts/incident_watchdog/{__main__,classify,probes}.py`** — per-incident probe-scoped resolution and the definitive-vs-transient `unknown` split are correct; the REL-037 fix holds. Only `store.py` retention is cited (R-178).
- **`web/middleware.ts`** — the matcher covers `/api/*` unconditionally (`:422`); `isPublicRoute` is a 5-entry explicit allowlist; the probe gate fails closed on an unset token and 401s a wrong bearer.
- **`web/lib/probeAuth.ts`** — SHA-256 digest compare is constant-time and length-blind; unset/empty `expectedToken` returns `false`. No fail-open.
- **The five new indicator routes are correctly classified for authz** — all in `MIDDLEWARE_PERIMETER_ONLY_ROUTES`, all GET-only snapshot reads, all genuinely covered by the middleware matcher. `b3dacd32`'s `ivrank` classification matches the other four. Their defects are freshness (first round R-125), not access.
- **`web/app/api/orders/{place,modify,cancel,whatif}/route.ts`** — the guard is the first statement on every exported verb; the demo blockade is the first IB-reaching statement in `place`, and `resolveDemoOrderDecision` fails closed on an unknown cohort.
- **`web/app/api/admin/trading/[action]/route.ts`** — action allowlist, `operatorOnly` on both verbs before any work, explicit `{confirm: true}` on kill/cancel-all, no upstream-body passthrough.
- **`scripts/api/server.py` authz perimeter** — `AUTH_EXEMPT_PATHS` unchanged in the delta; the middleware denies 503 when JWKS is unconfigured; `test_route_authz_matrix.py` enumerates `app.routes` at runtime, so a new FastAPI route cannot ship unclassified.
- **`web/lib/regimeLiveStrip.ts`** — fully clean. Both derived ratios divide-guarded, history access length-checked, and `marketOpen=false` correctly nulls all live values rather than reusing closes.
- **`web/lib/volCone.ts`, `web/lib/ivrank.ts`** — every formatter null/NaN-guarded, both division sites guarded, nulls preserved rather than zero-filled, no `.at(-1)` / bare `reduce` / `Math.max(...[])`.
- **`scripts/lib/twr_gates.py`** — constants only; the parity contract with `web/lib/performanceTwr.ts:20-42` holds.
- **`scripts/clients/journal_basis.py`** — `contract_fill_fingerprint` correctly returns `None` for `CLOSED` round trips and zero-signed rows, so it cannot swallow a directional fill.
- **`scripts/lib/{wsTrust.js,depthBudget.js,flex_flows.py}`, `web/lib/{pricesProtocol.ts,book/depthDerivations.ts}`, `scripts/clients/ib_timing.py`** — pure or fail-closed; no timers, no network, no state.
- **`scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/menthorq_login_probe.py`** — bounded `(5, 90)` timeout, 5-minute retry embargo and 3/day budget both persisted through `get_state`/`set_state`.
- **`scripts/monitor_daemon/handlers/expiry_sweep.py` keyset pagination** — correct: `journal.trade_id` is `TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, so `trade_id > ''` matches every row. The `round_trip_comp` absorption at `:240-243` only ever reduces net open quantity, so it can miss a genuine short but cannot fabricate a `$0.00 BUY_TO_CLOSE`. The defect is the guard's opener/closer conflation (R-155).

## Delta audit 2026-08-26

**Range:** `c0e7e4f5..HEAD` (`1b326772`) — 100 commits, 662 files. Anchor verified
(`git rev-parse --verify c0e7e4f5^{commit}` resolves; `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirms
it is an ancestor of HEAD). Per the 2026-08-22 lesson the range was split at the last commit
touching the reliability documents (`e0f508bd`, the REL-052/068/069 remediation merge): the
50 remediation commits below it were re-verified by the standing sweeps, and the nine agents
were scoped to the 50 feature commits `e0f508bd..HEAD` (304 files, 131 source files after
dropping tests, `web/e2e`, docs, `site/` and JSON fixtures).

**No branch collision.** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'reliability/weekend-*'` returned
nothing before numbering began; the 2026-08-22 branch has been merged and deleted. Numbering
starts at R-198 / REL-070, continuing the frozen contract.

**Method.** Nine subsystem-scoped read-only walks, each capped at 21 files with an explicit
file list, the commits to trace, and a pre-filtered list of the already-filed R-### findings
touching its files: money-path server, order-entry UI, state/persistence, connectivity/sessions,
ingestion/resources, serving-side error handling, control plane/deploy, observability/weekend
loops, and serving UI. The standing sweeps were run in the lead context, before the walks
reported, so their output could be cross-checked against the findings. Every P0 and several
P1 claims were re-verified in the lead context against the code at HEAD before landing;
those rows say so.

**Standing sweeps.** Sweeps 1-5 and 7 all held:

```
SWEEP 1 halt chokepoints — HOLD. is_trading_halted() present at ib_execute.py:443-444,
  ib_order_manage.py:181-183, ib_place_order.py:240-242, exit_orders.py:475-477,
  api/server.py:2490-2499 with _refuse_if_trading_halted() at 2525, 2696, 2729, 2831.
SWEEP 2 _NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS — HOLD. api/server.py:4887 frozenset({"ib_place_order.py"}),
  enforced 4996 (auth_state gate) and 5074.
SWEEP 3 order_limits wiring — HOLD on the funnel. ib_place_order.py:253-255,
  api/server.py:2482-2485 wired at 2527 (place) and 2713 (modify), check_modify_limits 2854.
  GAP: ib_execute.py has zero check_order_limits references -> new finding.
SWEEP 4 exit_orders ack-poll — HOLD. exit_orders.py:180-218 _ack_perm_id/_confirm_placement,
  used at :678-692, journal left PENDING on no-ack.
SWEEP 5 hrana on daemon_state — HOLD. db/writer.py:1921-1934 upsert_daemon_state -> _hrana_execute.
SWEEP 6 new order-placing call sites — no call site added in e0f508bd..HEAD.
  Standing gap found: ib_execute.py:456 places live with halt but no order_limits.
SWEEP 7 service_health writers vs both catalogs — CLEAN for the delta.
  bpi-scan / vol-cone / credit-spread / iei-hyg / trin / equibles-13f /
  equibles-filing-forensics / perf-twr all present in scripts/watchdog/services.py
  AND web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts. perf-twr (last run's REL gap) now in both.
  informed-flow is py=N ts=Y but on-demand by design (serviceHealthWindows.ts:327-333) — not a gap.
  No new units added in range (drop-in .example files only).
```

Sweep 6 produced one finding (R-250): `ib_execute.py` honours the halt but has zero
`check_order_limits` references, and `risk_reversal.py:501-504` renders a `--yes` invocation
of it into the operator's own report. Not new in this delta and not previously filed, so it
is filed here — the sweeps run regardless of diff. Sweep 7 was clean for the delta's own jobs
but **missed** `breadth-scan` (R-236), which is absent from `SCHEDULED_SERVICES` entirely;
REL-088 widens the sweep to every timer-backed writer rather than only the delta's.

**Cross-agent duplicates, merged centrally.** The money-path and order-UI walks reached the
combo quote-timestamp fabrication from opposite ends (`quoteTelemetry.ts` writer vs the four
call sites); filed once as R-208 with both sets of cites, because a fix on either side alone
leaves the defect live. The state and connectivity walks both reached the `flex_embargo`
fail-open (R-212). Independent numbering from every walk was discarded and reassigned
centrally; the emitted ids were asserted strictly ascending before the commit.

**One severity downgraded from the agent's rating, recorded not hidden.** The connectivity
walk rated the Caddy order-POST retry (R-220) P0 on the theory that the 15 s `lb_try_duration`
on the final `handle` block replays a `POST /api/orders/place` severed by the bounded Next.js
shutdown. The lead context could not confirm the mechanism: without `retry_match`, Caddy is
documented to retry dial errors only, and a mid-flight severed connection would not qualify.
It is filed P1 with the contingency stated in the row. The defect that remains regardless is
real — order non-duplication is being protected by an unpinned third-party default, this repo
pins no Caddy version, and `cloud/tests/test_caddyfile.py`'s mechanism test drives only a GET
against a dead port. REL-082's acceptance criteria cover the POST case in both directions.

**NEW_FINDINGS re-triage.** NF-1 (Gate-3 bankroll-% cap) still open, and R-250 widens it:
the static caps that were standing in for it do not apply to `ib_execute.py` at all. NF-2
(unpaginated journal reads on the unbounded transport) still open with one new instance,
R-203 (`journal_realized_pnl_for_fills`), on a money path — notable because the same delta
converted the sibling `journal_basis` to a keyset pager. NF-3 (relay stale-feed does not gate
the order UI) still open and materially worse: R-214 gives the relay a concrete mechanism for
reporting a fresh `last_tick_at` having never received a tick, so the banner NF-3 says is the
only cover does not appear either. NF-4 (aggregated Flex buckets) unchanged. NF-5, NF-6 and
the REL-021b remainder unchanged (all P2).

Concretely: BUY 10 @ $1 (kept) + BUY 10 @ $3 (dropped, e.g. `fill_price` null on a rehydrated row) + SELL_OPTION 10 → `avg_basis_per_unit` at `:226` is $1 not $2, the `qty (10) > abs(position_qty) (10)` test at `:229` is false, and `pnl` at `:238` is overstated by the whole missing basis with no warning logged. Worse, the signal tier at `:395-399` requires `pa.lagger_iv_rank < 30` for `STRONG` — a fabricated `0.0` is the **most** bullish possible value — and `current_iv` is fabricated to `0.0` on the same path, which maximises `pa.lagger_hv_iv_gap` too. A quota-exhausted run therefore manufactures `STRONG` long-vol trade ideas on the Opportunities → GARCH tab. The operator sees a normal green scan with unusually many STRONG pairs; nothing in `to_json` (`:715-721`) carries `error`, so the persisted cache and the UI are indistinguishable from a healthy scan, and `0.0` is a legal real value so no downstream sanity check can separate them. EMPTY_CRI`) whose `cri` is `{score: 0, level: "LOW", components: {vix:0,vvix:0,correlation:0,momentum:0}}` (`:59`), `crash_trigger.triggered: false` (`:62`) and `cta.exposure_pct: 200` (`:60`), **overwrites `market_open` with the live session state** (`:298`) so the payload looks current, and returns HTTP 200 with `cacheState: "HIT"` (`:311`). `RegimePanel` branches on `data?.cri ?` (`:318`) — always truthy because the route always ships the object — and carries its own second `{score: 0, level: "LOW"}` default on the same line, then renders `cri.score.toFixed(0)` as `0 /100` with `levelColor("LOW")` and four `ComponentBar`s at `0.0/25`, each coloured `var(--positive)` by the `score < 8` test at `:126`. This voids the contract stated in the file header at `:3-6` ("`git push origin main` only reaches the VPS if the full Vitest + pytest suites are green"). | R-202 | P0 | `scripts/ci/path_filter.py:16-32, 62-68`; `vitest.config.ts:18`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:84-85, 618`; `pyproject.toml:36-50` | `scripts/lib/**/*.test.js` is a vitest-only suite that `path_filter.py` routes to the **python** gate, so changes to those modules deploy to production with their tests never executed. `PYTHON_PREFIXES` contains `"scripts/"` (`:26`) and `WEB_PREFIXES` contains `"lib/"` but not `"scripts/lib/"` (`:16-24`); `_matches` is a plain `startswith`, so `"scripts/lib/staleDataMachine.js".startswith("lib/")` is False and `classify` returns `python=True, web=False`. But `vitest.config.ts:18` includes `"scripts/lib/**/*.test.js"`, and there are eleven such files — `staleDataMachine`, `wsTrust`, `reconnectGate`, `sendBackpressure`, `ibTickHandler`, `demoMirrorReliability`, `depthBudget`, `depthLadder`, `forwardSubRegistry`, `futuresRollover`, `marketCalendar` — covering the WS relay's stale-tick recovery ladder, reconnect gate and backpressure. pytest collects none of them (`norecursedirs`, and they are `.js`). `web-tests` is gated on `needs.changes.outputs.web == 'true'` (`ci.yml:84-85`) so it is **skipped**, and `deploy` explicitly accepts `needs.web-tests.result == 'skipped'` (`ci.yml:618`). A push to main touching only `scripts/lib/staleDataMachine.js` therefore shows an all-green CI in which its entire test suite never ran, and ships. **This delta is an instance**: it changed `scripts/lib/staleDataMachine.js` and `scripts/lib/demoMirrorReliability.test.js`. Both tickers passed the per-ticker sufficiency test, so neither appears in `insufficient_data`. The operator reads a Gate 3 report with zero clusters and zero insufficient tickers — a clean bill of health — while the concentration the gate exists to surface is structurally invisible. |

With `BACKFILL_MAX_SYMBOLS_PER_RUN = 4` the worst case is roughly 4 × (10 s connect + 20 s history + UW + 30 s Yahoo) ≈ 240 s of blocking work inside `attach_correlation_risk_report`, which the module's own comment at `:66-68` says `ib_sync` invokes every minute during RTH. `_ib_reachable:449-457` returns `True` when `ib_auth_state()` is `None` ("an unreachable /health proceeds optimistically"), so a **dead** gateway is the expensive case: four sequential 10 s connect timeouts before the ladder even reaches UW. | R-207 | P1 | `web/lib/order/positionTrade.ts:214-222` (`:220`); `web/components/ModifyOrderModal.tsx:396-399` | `isExcludedOrder` treats `orderId` as an identity equal to `permId`, OR'd rather than as a fallback: `if (exclude.permId != null && …) return true; if (exclude.orderId != null && Number(row.orderId) === exclude.orderId) return true;`. `permId` is globally unique at IB; `orderId` is per-client-session and is reused across `clientId`s and across restarts, and `open_orders` is a full multi-client snapshot. So when the permId branch misses, the orderId branch can still match a **different** working SELL BAG on the same ticker and silently subtract its `totalQuantity` from `workingSellUnits` at `:203`. That reopens the R-112 over-close hole for that order: `findHeldComboForClose` sees more free held units than exist, sets `closeOut`, and `whatIfKey` returns `null` whenever `closeOut != null`, so the broker what-if never runs to catch it and `okToSubmit` is asserted on structure match alone. | R-208 | P1 | `web/lib/quoteTelemetry.ts:88-116` (`:94-95`, `:114`); `web/components/ticker-detail/OrderTab.tsx:784-796`; `web/components/ticker-detail/PositionTradeTicket.tsx:66-81`; `web/components/mobile/MobileOrderTicket.tsx:405-418`; `web/components/ticker-detail/OptionsChainTab.tsx:333-341` | The combo freshness gate is structurally unreachable, so a stale combo book renders as a live quote on every surface a trader prices a combo from. `comboQuotePriceData` stamps `timestamp: new Date().toISOString()` at `:114` on every call, and `hasLiveQuote()` (`:165-176`) rejects a `PriceData` only when its `timestamp` is older than `LIVE_QUOTE_MAX_AGE_MS` (5 min) — so a synthesized combo quote is always "now" and the `CLOSE / HIGH / LOW` downgrade branch can never fire for a combo, no matter how old the leg quotes that produced `netBid`/`netAsk` are. The call sites destroy the evidence before the model is built: `useTargetQuote`'s combo branch (`PositionTradeTicket.tsx:66-81`) reads `lp.bid`/`lp.ask` off each leg's `PriceData` and then returns `priceData: null`, keeping only the numbers, and `OrderTab.tsx:784-796`, `MobileOrderTicket.tsx:405-418` and `OptionsChainTab.tsx:333-341` do the same with `netPrices`/`signedNetPrices`. Each is a `useMemo` keyed on the price **values**, so when the relay stops ticking the memo never recomputes and the panel freezes at the last-known book. `lastIsCalculated: true` at `:95` is likewise set unconditionally, mislabelling a genuine traded `last` (the `last?: number \| null` field at `:79` exists for it) as derived — honest today only by accident of the four call sites all passing `mid`. Raised independently by the money-path and order-UI walks from opposite ends; filed once because a fix on either side alone leaves the defect live. |
| R-209 | P1 | `web/components/ticker-detail/PositionTradeTicket.tsx:168, 169-206, 350-358, 361-370` | `const okToSubmit = riskState?.okToSubmit !== false;` fails **open**: any state other than an explicit `false` — including `null` — permits submission. Every sibling surface uses the opposite polarity (`SingleLegOrderTicket.tsx:34`, `MobileOrderTicket.tsx:385`, `OrderTab.tsx:283`, `OptionsChainTab.tsx:242`, `ListedContractOrderForm.tsx:145`, `ModifyOrderModal.tsx:519`, `ChatPanel.tsx:485` all use `=== true`), and `web/CLAUDE.md:53` states the contract as "Parent surface MUST disable submit when `state.okToSubmit !== true`". Two things make the `null` case reachable rather than theoretical. First, `<OrderRiskGate onState={setRiskState}>` is mounted only inside `{confirmStep && (...)}` at `:350` and publishes through a post-commit `useEffect` (`web/lib/order/risk/OrderRiskGate.tsx:86-89`), so on the first commit of the confirm view `riskState` is still `null` and the "Confirm Order" button at `:366` is already enabled. | R-237 | P1 | `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:173, 190-194`, `:241-250`; `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:54-62, 232-236, 288-294` | A `ground_truth` failure in the audit phase kills the entire cycle, and the remediate phase is never run and never reported. In `run_phase`, `trap on_crash ERR` is armed (testing `:192`, reliability `:234`) and then `ground_truth` is called (`:194` / `:236`); `on_crash` calls `report` and returns 0, but under `set -Eeuo pipefail` the ERR trap runs and the shell then exits anyway, so `run_phase audit` never returns and `:246` / `:294` (`run_phase remediate`) is never reached — contradicting the comment two lines above it ("Remediate runs regardless of the audit rc — backlog may exist even when the audit phase failed"). Testing is far more exposed than reliability because `testing_weekend.sh:173` is a bare single-attempt `git fetch origin --quiet`, while `reliability_weekend.sh:54-62` got `fetch_origin_with_retry` (3 × 60 s) in 3f96dc31 for exactly this failure — the 2026-08-23 port-22 blackhole. The operator observes one `**audit** … CRASHED (exit 1)` comment on the `testing-weekend` issue and then complete silence: no `**remediate**` comment, no remediate Pushover. That is indistinguishable from the remediate phase hanging, because the only remediate-phase notification lives inside the function that was never called. |
| R-238 | P1 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:113-121` (`:116-119`), `:131-132`, `:244-256` (`:253`), `:288-294`; `config/com.radon.reliability-daily.plist:6`; `scripts/setup_reliability_weekend.sh:121-122` | The 20 h cycle budget cannot bound the run under 24 h, so a long cycle silently eats the next day's launchd fire. `CYCLE_DEADLINE=$((SECONDS + CYCLE_BUDGET_SECS))` with `CYCLE_BUDGET_SECS=72000` is tested at exactly one place — `:253`, **after** `run_round` has already returned, inside the continuation-round `while :` loop. It is not tested before `run_phase remediate` at `:294`, not tested inside `run_round`, and not passed to `timeout`. With `CAP_SECS=21600` and `MAX_ROUNDS=8`, a deadline check that passes at `SECONDS=71999` launches round 8, which runs a further 21600 s: total ≈ 93,600 s = 26.0 h against a `StartCalendarInterval` period of 24 h. The comment at `:116-117` asserts "20h leaves the next 00:00 fire clear"; the effective cap is `CYCLE_BUDGET_SECS + CAP_SECS`. launchd will not start a second instance of `com.radon.reliability-daily` while one runs, so the 00:00 fire is dropped with no record anywhere — and `setup_reliability_weekend.sh:121-122` documents that the resulting quiet day is ambiguous between "did not fire" and "still running", which is precisely the state that cannot be triaged after the fact. |
| R-239 | P1 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:19-21, 92-121` (`:92-96`, `:99-102`, `:106`, `:109-111`), `:234`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:28-30, 83-107, 192` | Everything between `main()` entry and `run_phase` runs with **no** ERR trap and no notification path, so a whole class of runner deaths exits silently. The `trap on_crash ERR` that produces the dead-man issue comment and the Pushover is armed only at `:234` (reliability) / `:192` (testing), inside `run_phase`. Before that point the script does `cd "$REPO"`, the marker-file check that `exit 2`s, `acquire_runner_lock` that `exit 3`s, `mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"`, and the log-rotation pipeline under `set -o pipefail` — every one of these can fail on a full disk, a moved clone, or a held lock, and every one exits with nothing but a line on stderr. The lock branch is the expensive instance: `acquire_runner_lock` only reclaims when `kill -0 "$held"` fails, so if the recorded pid has been reused by any live unrelated process it returns 1 forever and **every subsequent daily fire exits 3 in under a second** — no Pushover, no issue comment, no partial log. The file headers at `:19-21` / `:28-30` claim "every outcome (incl. crash) is reported to the rolling GitHub issue, and each phase also pages Pushover, so a silent-dead runner is visible without waiting for the next run"; for the prologue that claim is false. |
| R-240 | P1 | `config/com.radon.testing-daily.plist:26-27`; contrast `config/com.radon.reliability-daily.plist:26-27`; `scripts/setup_testing_weekend.sh:43, 88-89` | The testing loop's launchd job has no `bun` on `PATH`. Commit 3f96dc31 appended `:__HOME__/.bun/bin` to the reliability plist only — `git show 3f96dc31 -- config/` touches exactly one file — and the testing plist's `PATH` is still `/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:__HOME__/.local/bin`. The testing loop is the one whose entire remit is JS/vitest suite health: `vitest.config.ts` is driven through `bunx`, and `setup_testing_weekend.sh:88-89` itself runs `bun install --frozen-lockfile`. The failure is invisible at install time because `setup_testing_weekend.sh:43` runs `check "bun" command -v bun` in the operator's **interactive** shell, which does have `~/.bun/bin` on `PATH`, so setup prints `ok bun` and then installs a plist under which `bun` is not resolvable. The operator observes the daily testing cycle completing with `FAILED`/`OK` statuses where the agent could not execute any JS test, and no wrapper-level signal that the toolchain rather than the suite is the cause. |

The trader sees a clean page asserting a successful IB Gateway sync at a recent wall-clock time, with a flat book. | R-252 | P2 | `web/app/api/orders/cancel/route.ts:75-84`; `web/app/api/orders/modify/route.ts:214-221, 271-278`; `web/lib/orders/ordersReadCache.ts:9-17`; `web/lib/orders/readOrdersFromDb.ts:110, 121-125` | When the `/orders/refresh` round-trip throws, the empty `catch` swallows it and the `finally { invalidateOrdersSnapshotCache() }` runs anyway, so the immediately following `readOrdersSnapshotBestEffort()` reads Turso **before** the broker state has been mirrored back and returns the pre-cancel/pre-modify book. Separately, `readOrdersFromDb` returns `null` (→ cached `EMPTY_ORDERS`) at `:110` when the JS-side filters `isPriorSessionDayOrder` (`:91`) and `filterExecutedToEtToday` (`:108`) strip everything, so a non-empty Turso snapshot can be pinned as "no orders" process-wide for 2 s. Before this delta each request re-read; the blast radius of one bad read is now every concurrent reader. |
