The `Index` suffix in the caption is the only disclosure, and it sits inside the panel the numbers are in. |
| R-255 | P2 | `web/components/ModifyOrderModal.tsx:328-335, 628, 677-693`; `web/lib/modifyOrderQuote.ts:31-42` | The nine-field panel in the modify modal is fed `priceData`, which is not the market — it is `applyRestingLimitToQuote({ priceData: marketPriceData, action: order.action, limitPrice: order.limitPrice })`, which clamps `ask` down to the resting limit on a SELL and pushes `bid` up to it on a BUY, then collapses the other side if it crosses. Commit 8ef78f81 replaced the four-field `ModifyOrderQuoteTelemetry` with `<OrderQuoteTelemetry priceData={priceData} label={order.symbol} density="tight" />` at `:628`, so the doctored quote now carries the same nine-field weight — and a full `SPREAD` — as a genuine market panel on every other surface, captioned with the order's own symbol. A SELL resting at 5.00 against a true market of 7.00/8.00 prints `ASK 5.00`, `MID 6.00`, `SPREAD 2.00 / 33%`; the MID quick-button at `:684` then writes that same self-referential 6.00 into the replacement limit, and each repricing pulls the displayed MID further toward the operator's own order. The same line also captions a BAG's signed net (built at `:274-297`, potentially negative for a credit combo) with the bare underlying symbol, so a credit risk reversal renders negative BID/ASK under what reads as the stock's ticker. |
| R-256 | P2 | `web/lib/backgroundScan.ts:29-45` (`:31-33`, `:42-44`); constructed at `web/app/api/gex/route.ts:136-139` | The single admission gate for the GEX / VCG / regime stale-while-revalidate can latch permanently closed. `inFlight = true` is set at `:31` and `run()` is invoked at `:33` **outside any try/catch**; `inFlight = false` lives only in the `.finally` of the promise chain. A synchronous throw from `run` — the module-level triggers are constructed once per process with `run: () => radonFetch(...)`, and `radonFetch` builds a `new URL` from env — propagates straight out of `trigger()` before any handler is attached, leaving `inFlight` true for the life of the Node process. From then on `trigger()` returns `false` on every poll with no log line, no backoff timestamp and no error, so the three routes serve their last cached snapshot indefinitely while appearing to revalidate. The `blockedUntil` backoff at `:38` is also armed only from `.catch`, so a `run()` that resolves with a failure **body** (HTTP 200 carrying an error payload) arms nothing. |
| R-257 | P2 | `web/lib/portfolio/readPortfolioSnapshot.server.ts:41-57` (`:50`), `:75-93`, `:96-116` (`:114-115`) | `readPortfolioFromDb` calls `JSON.parse(row.payload)` at `:50` with no guard. A truncated or non-JSON `portfolio_snapshots.payload` raises `SyntaxError` inside the cache fetcher, so `readPortfolioSnapshot`'s `staleWhileError` path cannot help — the throw is not a transport error, and the retry at `:86` re-reads the same corrupt row and throws identically — and the route surfaces it as `DB_UNAVAILABLE` / "Turso portfolio read failed: Unexpected token…", filing a persistence-corruption incident as a database-availability one. On the RSC seed path the same exception is swallowed whole by the bare `catch { return undefined; }` at `:114-115`, so the server-rendered portfolio page silently degrades to the client GET with no log and no trace that a stored snapshot is unparseable. |
| R-258 | P2 | `cloud/caddy/Caddyfile:9, 28-30, 42, 77` | `grace_period 10s` is shorter than the `lb_try_duration 15s` it now has to accommodate. On `systemctl reload caddy` — which the deploy runs whenever the Caddyfile itself changes, i.e. concurrently with the upstream restarts the retry is meant to absorb — old servers are hard-cut at 10 s, so an in-flight retry loop can never complete its 15 s window during the one event most likely to need it. The same 10 s also silently truncates the `handle /ws*` block at `:28-30`: every browser's relay WebSocket is severed on reload with no drain and no close-frame negotiation, and the comment at `:3-8` documents this as the **intent** (bounding the reload) without noting that the price feed for every session dies with it. |
| R-259 | P2 | `scripts/api/server.py:3036-3052`, `:910-915`; `scripts/api/scan_gate.py:45-49` | Two ordering and truthiness flaws in `_admit()`. First, `gate.in_backoff()` is evaluated **before** `gate.in_cooldown()`/`read_cached()`, and `mark_failure()` does not clear `_last_success` — so after any single scan failure all four routes return a hard 429 for 60 s even when a good cache written seconds earlier is sitting on disk and would have been served. The gate converts a transient capacity shed into a blank or error panel rather than stale-but-correct data. Second, the hit is tested with `if hit:` at `:3047` and `:3051`, not `if hit is not None:`; `_read_cache` returns whatever `json.loads` produced, so an empty-object cache (`{}` — a truncated write, or a scan that legitimately produced no rows) is falsy, `_admit()` reports a miss while `in_cooldown()` is still True, and every 5 s client poll falls through to `gate.lock` and spawns another 120 s `run_script`. The lock serialises them, so it is a permanent back-to-back subprocess treadmill on the general lane rather than a fan-out — invisible in the response (callers get 200s) and indistinguishable from normal operation except by the load it feeds into the two findings above. |
| R-260 | P2 | `scripts/garch_convergence.py:887-889`, `:891-893`; `scripts/leap_scanner_uw.py:937`; contrast `scripts/bpi_scan.py:48, 678` | Both UW scanners persist their canonical artifact with a plain, non-atomic `cache_path.write_text(json.dumps(json_data, indent=2))` while the repo ships `utils.atomic_io.atomic_save` and `bpi_scan.py:678` uses it for exactly the same job. `data/garch_convergence.json` is explicitly "canonical" (`:891-892` — there is no garch table in Turso), so a SIGTERM/SIGKILL or a full disk during that single `write_text` truncates the only copy; `/api/garch-convergence` then serves a `JSONDecodeError` and, because the file is canonical, there is no DB row to fall back to. `mirror_scan_snapshot("garch-scan", ...)` at `:893` runs **after** the write, so a truncated file is also not paired with any health row recording the fault. |
| R-261 | P2 | `scripts/newsfeed/index.js:149-166, 168, 242-247`; `scripts/newsfeed/cycle.js:79, 141, 202`; `scripts/newsfeed/scheduler.js:38-40`; `scripts/newsfeed/index.js:296-298` | `recordServiceHealth` is only ever invoked from inside `runScrapeCycle` (passed in at `index.js:189`, called at `cycle.js:79/141/202`), but `scrapeOnceAttempt` does three things that can throw **before** reaching it: `authenticateIfNeeded()` (`:153`, the themarketear login flow), `getBrowser()` (`:154`), and `handle.page.goto(..., timeout: 30_000)` (`:158-161`). The two most likely sustained failures — an expired or rejected session, and an unreachable themarketear — therefore write **zero** rows to `service_health.newsfeed-scraper`; `onCycleError` (`:246`) only `console.error`s. The last successful `ok` row simply ages, so the operator's health surface reports "no recent update" rather than "auth is broken", with no error text anywhere but the journal. Combined with `scheduler.js:38-40` throwing after three consecutive failures → `index.js:296-298` `process.exit(1)` → `Restart=on-failure`, the unit re-enters the same blind loop roughly every six minutes indefinitely, the restarts far enough apart never to trip systemd's start limit. |
| R-262 | P2 | `scripts/newsfeed/scheduler.js:57-69` (`:64-67`); `scripts/newsfeed/index.js:288-298, 241-250` (`:248-250`) | `createShutdown` aborts the controller and then calls `exit(0)` after `graceMs` unconditionally — it never checks whether a cycle is in flight, and the abort signal is never threaded into `scrapeOnce` (`index.js:242-245` passes `signal` to `runForever` only, which checks it between cycles at `scheduler.js:29/42`). A SIGTERM landing mid-cycle therefore kills the process between `persistPosts` (disk) and `upsertPosts` (Turso), and it exits **0**, so systemd's `Restart=on-failure` treats a truncated cycle as a clean stop and neither the `console.warn` at `:65` nor any `service_health` row records that work was dropped. The only forensic trace of a lost cycle is the delivery-state sidecar, and `run()`'s `finally { await closeBrowser() }` may itself be cut short by the same timer. |
| R-263 | P2 | `web/lib/useOrders.ts:13, 85-96` (`:95`), `:61`, `:161-177`; contrast `web/lib/usePortfolio.ts:91-103, 280-288` | `useOrders` has no visibility gate and no market-state gate of any kind: `pollCached` unconditionally re-arms `scheduleNext(POLL_INTERVAL_MS)` after every fetch (`:95`), and the only brake is the `active` prop, which reflects route or panel selection, not tab visibility. A backgrounded tab left on the orders surface issues a `no-store` GET to `/api/orders` — a live Turso read via `readOrdersSnapshotFromDb` — every 30 s through the night and the weekend, when the open-order snapshot cannot change. It also has no backoff on failure: `!res.ok` throws at `:61` (the route answers a Turso outage with 503 by design), the catch only sets `error`, and the next poll is still exactly 30 s later, forever. `usePortfolio` honours `Retry-After` on 429 (`:91-103`) and re-arms on visibility (`:280-288`); `useOrders` was left out of both changes. |
| R-264 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:1114-1130` (`:1119`, `:1121-1122`, `:1127`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml:116-117` | Two operability defects in `payload_paths_changed`. (a) `site/*` is grouped with `scripts/tests/*\|cloud/tests/*\|web/tests/*\|web/e2e/*` on the `continue` line at `:1119`, but `site/` is a full Next.js application tree (`site/app`, `site/components`, `site/lib`, `site/next.config.ts`) and its `site/lib` modules are executed by the root vitest config (`ci.yml:116-117` names `site/lib/theme` explicitly). A push touching only `site/` runs the full test gate, promotes the tree, writes a green marker, and restarts nothing. (b) The multi-line case pattern at `:1121-1122` uses a backslash continuation ending in `\| \`; bash strips only the backslash-newline, so the six-space indent of the next line becomes part of the pattern — the effective alternative is `"      requirements*"`, and `requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `bun.lock`, `package.json` and `*.service` can never match that branch. It is currently masked only because the unconditional `return 0` at `:1127` catches everything the earlier `continue`s did not; anyone who later adds a case arm below it inherits a silently dead pattern. |
| R-265 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-flow-refresh.service:16-20`; `scripts/run_flow_refresh.sh:106` | `SuccessExitStatus=75` makes systemd report **any** exit-75 as `Result=success`. The comment justifies it as the wrapper's `SHED_EXIT=75` leaking, and `run_flow_refresh.sh:106` does define `SHED_EXIT=75` — but 75 is `EX_TEMPFAIL` generally, and after this change a flow-refresh that is **permanently** shedding (the subprocess lane full on every fire, which is itself the incident) is indistinguishable from a clean run in `systemctl is-failed`, in the unit watchdog, and in the journal's `Result=`. There is no counterpart assertion that the run actually wrote a `service_health` row, so the unit can be dark indefinitely while paging nothing. |
| R-266 | P2 | `scripts/setup_testing_weekend.sh:18-23, 56-61, 83-86`; `scripts/setup_reliability_weekend.sh:13-18, 56-61`; `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:82-86`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:9-16, 75-77` | The two loops are not as isolated as `testing_weekend.sh:9-16` claims: `WEEKEND_VENV="$WEEKEND_ROOT/venv"` and `WEEKEND_ENV="$WEEKEND_ROOT/.env"` are literally the same paths in both setup scripts (`setup_testing:20,23` vs `setup_reliability:15,18`), and both wrappers prepend that shared venv to the running agent's `PATH`. The in-flight guard at `setup_testing_weekend.sh:56-61` only checks `$WEEKEND_REPO/.weekend-runner.lock` — the **testing** clone's lock — and then unconditionally runs `python3.13 -m venv "$WEEKEND_VENV"`, `pip install --upgrade pip` and `pip install -r requirements.txt` at `:83-86`. Running `setup_testing_weekend.sh` while the reliability cycle is live therefore mutates the interpreter and site-packages the running reliability agent is executing against, mid-run, with no warning; `setup_reliability_weekend.sh:56-61` has the mirror-image blind spot. The lock guard was written for the clone (the comment at `:56-57` says "A live cycle owns this clone") and nobody extended it to the shared `~/radon-weekend/` root both loops depend on. |
| R-267 | P2 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:105-111` (`:109-111`), `:196`, `:220-222`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:96-102`, `:264`; `config/com.radon.reliability-daily.plist:49-52`; `config/com.radon.testing-daily.plist:50-53` | The log rotation deletes the launchd stdout/stderr sinks, which are the only forensics for the prologue deaths above. `LOG_DIR="$REPO/logs/reliability-weekend"` and the rotation is `ls -1t "$LOG_DIR" \| tail -n +31 \| while read old; do rm -f -- "$LOG_DIR/$old"; done` with no exclusion list — and the plists point `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` at `__WEEKEND_REPO__/logs/reliability-weekend/launchd-cycle.log` and `.err`, inside that same directory. `launchd-cycle.err` only gets an mtime bump when something actually writes to stderr (the wrapper's normal chatter goes through `tee` to stdout, and `timeout claude … >> "$RUN_LOG" 2>&1` at `:220-222` is redirected away), so once 30 newer per-phase logs accumulate — two per day, roughly 15 days — the stale `.err` sorts into `tail -n +31` and is unlinked. It is unlinked at `:109-111`, i.e. **before** `run_phase`, so any stderr the rest of that same invocation emits goes to a deleted inode that launchd still holds open. The `git clean` exclusions at `:196` and `:264` were carefully written to protect `logs/`; the wrapper's own rotation was not. |
| R-268 | P2 | `scripts/watchdog/units.py:3-4, 12, 18-19, 317-322, 325-335`; `cloud/services/radon-api.service:13-14, 45-46`; `cloud/services/radon-nextjs.service:10-11, 30-31`; `cloud/services/radon-newsfeed.service:11-12, 26-27` | The `flap` bucket — advertised in the module docstring at `:18-19` as the P1 "sustained crash loop" signal — cannot fire for any DUR-02-braked unit. `_flap_alert` requires `SubState == "auto-restart"` observed in **two consecutive** watchdog cycles (`:325-330`), and the bucket runs every five minutes (`:3-4`). The `auto-restart` substate is occupied only for `RestartSec` per restart: `radon-api` 5 s, `radon-nextjs` 5 s, `radon-monitor` 10 s, `radon-health` 2 s. With `StartLimitIntervalSec=300` / `StartLimitBurst=5`, systemd parks a crash-looping `radon-api` as `failed` roughly 25 seconds into the loop — three orders of magnitude before two five-minute samples could both land inside a five-second window. The real coverage is the `Result=start-limit-hit` branch at `:317-322`, which does page P1, so this is not an outage gap; it is a dead bucket that makes the module read as having two independent P1 signals when it has one, and it means an unbraked (`StartLimit`-less) `radon-*` unit that loops with a long `RestartSec` is the only shape `_flap_alert` could ever catch. |
| R-269 | P2 | `web/components/WorkspaceSections.tsx:1263-1266` | The flow-report footer emits a hardcoded window string: `` `Report Generated: ${new Date(lastSync).toLocaleString()} • Source: UW API • Dark Pool Lookback: 5 Trading Days • ${totalScanned} Positions Scanned` ``. The timestamp and `totalScanned` are payload-derived, but "5 Trading Days" is a literal in the JSX — nothing in the flow payload is consulted for it. If the producer's lookback changes, or a given scan covered fewer sessions because upstream data was short, the footer still asserts five trading days with the authority of the two adjacent real numbers. CLAUDE.md's UI Copy Rules forbid hardcoded freshness/cadence copy for exactly this reason. |
| R-270 | P2 | `web/components/PositionTable.tsx:181, 394, 396, 481`; `web/lib/positionUtils.ts:16` | Last Price for an options position is `mv! / (pos.contracts * getMultiplier(pos))` with no guard that `pos.contracts !== 0`, and the sort extractor at `:181` repeats the same expression. `pos.contracts` is typed `number` with no positivity constraint, so a snapshot row carrying a zero quantity — a position flattened mid-sync, or a partial payload — yields `Infinity`, or `NaN` when `mv` is also 0. `:481` tests only `lastPrice != null`, so the value reaches `fmtPriceOrCalculated` → `fmtPrice` (`positionUtils.ts:16`), which calls `.toLocaleString()` on the non-finite number and prints `$∞` / `$NaN`, and the same value feeds `usePriceDirection` at `:396` where it drives the up/down flash. No finiteness check exists on either path. |
| R-271 | P2 | `web/components/TickerDetailContent.tsx:39-55` (`:54`); consumer `web/components/ticker-detail/BookTab.tsx:541, 564` | When the realtime feed has no `PriceData` for a leg, the component synthesizes one whose `last` is `leg.market_price` — the mark captured at the last portfolio sync, potentially hours old — and stamps it `timestamp: new Date().toISOString()` (`:54`), i.e. the render clock. The object is then handed to consumers as an ordinary `PriceData`, and `BookTab.tsx:541,564` reads `.timestamp` off exactly this type. It does set `lastIsCalculated: true`, so the price itself carries a "C" marker, but the **age** is fabricated: any consumer that ages this quote concludes it is milliseconds old. The correct value — the portfolio snapshot's `last_sync` — is available in the same tree and is not used. Same anti-pattern as the combo-quote finding above, at an independent site. |
| R-272 | P2 | `web/lib/scannerHero.ts:28-32`; `web/components/dashboard/ScannerHero.tsx:158-159, 164-167` | `coneFillPct` returns `0` when `conePosition` is null — i.e. when `atm_iv`, `p10` or `p90` is missing, or the cone is degenerate (`p90 <= p10`). But `0` is also the legitimate output for a name sitting at or above the p90 ceiling, the richest and least tradeable print. ScannerHero renders that directly as the bar width at `:166`. A candidate whose cone bounds failed to compute but whose `atm_iv` is present therefore shows a real IV number next to an empty bar that, per the comment at `:158-159` ("longer is better"), reads as maximally rich. The panel has no separate rendering for an unavailable cone. |
| R-273 | P2 | `web/components/VolConePanel.tsx:72-74, 488-496` (`:490`); `web/lib/volCone.ts:12` | `finiteBrushValue` maps a null or non-finite `atm_iv` to `0`, and the result is passed as the `values` array to `BrushMinimap` at `:490`. `VolConeSeriesPoint.atm_iv` is explicitly `number \| null` and the type comment at `volCone.ts:12` says "Nulls are preserved for chart gaps" — the main chart honours that (`buildVolConeChartRows` passes nulls through), but the range-selection minimap plots a missing session as 0% implied vol, a floor spike. The brush is what the trader drags to pick the window feeding the chart, so gaps in the series read as crash-low vol prints rather than absent data. |

### Delta remediation backlog (continuing REL-numbering)

| Task | Finding | Scope | Acceptance criteria (incl. verification) |
|---|---|---|---|
| REL-070 | R-201, R-202, R-264 | **CI gate integrity — untested code must not reach the VPS.** (a) Give `stage-release` a `needs:` on the same gate list `deploy` carries, or delete the job; while it has none it SSHes to production and executes the pushed commit's own `deploy.sh` before any test runs. (b) Add `scripts/lib/` to `WEB_PREFIXES` in `path_filter.py` (before the `scripts/` python match) so the eleven `scripts/lib/**/*.test.js` relay suites gate the deploy. ## Delta audit 2026-08-27

**Range:** `1b326772..HEAD` (`789aabea`) — 43 commits, 279 files. Anchor verified
(`git rev-parse --verify 1b326772^{commit}` resolves to `1b326772a3dab8bec00b10f2db9fa25dc9bdc8dd`;
`git merge-base --is-ancestor 1b326772 HEAD` confirms it is an ancestor). Per the 2026-08-22
lesson the range was split at the last commit touching `RELIABILITY_LOG.md`, which this time is
HEAD itself (`789aabea`, the squashed REL-070…REL-093 remediation merge, PR #100): the six
subsystem walks were scoped to the 42 feature commits `1b326772..789aabea^` (145 files, 81 source
files after dropping tests, `web/e2e`, docs, `site/` and JSON fixtures), and a seventh walk was
pointed at the remediation half as a regression review rather than leaving it to the sweeps alone.

**No branch collision.** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'reliability/weekend-*'` returned only
`reliability/weekend-2026-08-26`, which is merged (`789aabea`) and carries no findings above
R-273. No `reliability/weekend-2026-08-27` existed before numbering began. Numbering starts at
R-274 / REL-094, continuing the frozen contract.

**Prior backlog closed.** REL-070…REL-093 are all recorded DONE in `RELIABILITY_LOG.md` (one
PARTIAL with a corrected remedy, one split as NOT-A-DEFECT), so this audit carries no
stragglers forward. REL-021b (all P2) and NF-1…NF-8 remain the standing candidates.

**Method.** Seven read-only walks, each capped at ~15 files with an explicit file list, the
commits to trace, and a pre-filtered list of the already-filed R-### findings touching its files:
the order ticket rail (money path), positions/P&L, Python ingestion, control plane/CI, the new
freshness rail and panels, the share/OG and agent surfaces, and a regression review of last
week's own five P0 fixes. The standing sweeps were run in the lead context before the walks
reported. Every P0 and P1 claim that could be settled by reading the code was re-verified in the
lead context at HEAD before landing; three findings were raised entirely in the lead context
because they span a unit, a test and two catalogs and were therefore invisible to every scoped
walk.

**Standing sweeps.** All seven held:

```
SWEEP 1 halt chokepoints — HOLD. is_trading_halted() at ib_execute.py:443-444 and
  ib_place_order.py:240-242; ib_order_manage.py and monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py
  both carry it. Web /orders/place reaches it through FastAPI, not a second copy.
SWEEP 2 _NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS — HOLD. api/server.py:4955 frozenset({"ib_place_order.py"}),
  enforced at :5064 (auth_state gate) and :5142.
SWEEP 3 order-limits wired — HOLD, and wider than last week. check_order_limits now called
  from ib_place_order.py:253-255, api/server.py:2518-2520 AND ib_execute.py:454-455 (the
  REL-074 fix; sweep 6 flagged its absence a week ago).
SWEEP 4 exit_orders ack-poll — HOLD. monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:180-218 ack
  polling and the never-acked cancel; _confirm_placement wired at :678-690.
SWEEP 5 hrana on daemon_state — HOLD. daemon.py:416-431 -> db/writer.py:1921-1934 via
  _hrana_execute.
SWEEP 6 new order-placing call sites — HOLD. No NEW bypass in the delta. The two standing
  gaps are already filed: exit_order_service.py:398 (R-038/R-141, legacy, no producer) and
  ib_order_manage.py (halt present, limits absent) — unchanged in this range.
SWEEP 7 timer-backed service_health writers vs both catalogs — the REL-088 test passes
  (7/7). BUT the sweep was then distrusted per the 2026-08-26 lesson, and its own scope is
  defective: see R-277. Eight timer-backed units resolve to an empty name set and are
  therefore asserted on by nothing.
```

**Regression review of the 2026-08-26 remediation.** Four of the five P0 fixes were verified
holding end to end at HEAD: REL-072 (GARCH refuses a `None` IV rank before gate 4 and the
signal tier, and no caller re-substitutes), REL-073 (`/api/regime` unavailability reaches a real
empty state in `RegimePanel.tsx:499-509`), REL-074 (`check_order_limits` precedes the only
placement branch in `ib_execute.py`, with `--dry-run` correctly exempt), and the `path_filter`
half of REL-070 (`scripts/lib/` is a `WEB_PREFIXES` prefix match, so all eleven relay suites arm
the web gate). The other two did not hold: the `stage-release` half of REL-070 is R-299 and
REL-071's completeness guard is R-274. Noted for the record and NOT filed as a defect:
`web/app/api/internals/route.ts:612` still carries the `result?.data ?? EMPTY_CRI` shape REL-073
fixed elsewhere, but `InternalsPanel.tsx` renders nothing derived from `cri`/`cta`/`crash_trigger`,
so no false-calm reading surfaces from it today.

**Cross-walk duplicates merged centrally.** Three defects were reached independently from two
directions and carry one R-number each: the `stage-release` gate drift (control-plane walk and
the remediation-regression walk, R-299); the nulled CTA percentile re-substituted as a confident
50 (the Python ingestion walk and the share-surface walk found the two language halves — filed as
ONE finding with both cites at R-291, because a fix on one side leaves the other live); and the
`generate_regime_share` / `cri_scan` consumers of the same null (R-292, R-293).

**One contested claim rejected.** A walk filed the all-or-nothing aggregation in
`oldestQuoteTimestamp` as a defect — one unparseable leg timestamp collapsing a live 4-leg BAG to
a CLOSE render. Lead verification rejected that half: `web/lib/pricesProtocol.ts:230-233`
documents the fail-closed intent verbatim ("Unknown leg age is not 'fresh'"), and fail-closed is
the correct direction on a money surface. Only the second half — that the freshness signal never
reaches the priced BID/MID/ASK — is filed, at R-282, with the rejection recorded in the row.

### Delta findings table

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-274 | P0 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:227-243`, `:188-224`; `scripts/clients/journal_basis.py:90-99`; persisted at `journal_realized.py:145-152` | REL-071's dropped-row guard is blind to the largest class of dropped row, so the R-198 fabricated-P&L outcome is still reachable. `_unusable_fill_key` returns `None` — "excluded BY DESIGN" — whenever `_bucket_key(payload)` is `None` (`:239-241`), and its docstring asserts that case is only "BAG envelopes, stock". It is not: `_bucket_key` returns `None` if ANY of ticker/expiry/right/strike fails to normalise, and `_normalize_right` (`journal_basis.py:90-92`) returns `""` for anything outside `{C,P}` while `_normalize_strike` (`:96-99`) returns `None` for anything non-floatable. A journal row for an OPT contract whose `right` arrived as `""`/`null`, whose `strike` is `null`, or whose `expiry` has a digit count other than 6 or 8 is dropped from the replay AND never lands in `unusable` — byte-for-byte indistinguishable from a BAG envelope at that call. The contract then replays missing that row's quantity and its cost together, which is exactly the cancellation R-198 named, and `apply_journal_realized_pnl` writes the fabricated figure to `executed_orders.payload` with `realizedPNLSource: "journal"`, displacing IB's correct number. The guard added for a P0 covers the payload corruptions that are cheap to detect and misses the contract-identity corruptions that produce the identical silent wrong number. |
| R-275 | P1 | `scripts/ib_reconcile.py:476` (`try:`) and `:542` (`finally:`), against `:473` and `:506` | The position-reconcile spine writes NO `service_health` row when the run fails mid-flight. `main()` guards only two paths: connect failure (`_record_health("error", …)` at `:473`) and successful completion (`:506`). Everything between is a `try:` at `:476` whose only companion is `finally:` at `:542` — there is no `except`. | R-288 | P1 | `scripts/utils/cta_percentiles.py:115`, limit at `:28`; consumed at `scripts/generate_cta_share.py:158-160` | The z-score disagreement gate is one-sided, so the entire lower third of the fractional range still publishes inverted. Take the exact failure the module was written for — a fractional cell of `0.30` that the vision extractor rounds to the integer `0`: `_normalized_trio` sees three integers, `fractional` is False, the trio stays `[0, 0, 0]`, the row's own z of `-0.52` implies 30, the gap is 30, under 35, `contradicted` is False, and `percentile_3m` persists as **0**. Every true fractional percentile from 0.00 through roughly 0.35 sails through. The high band does not: 0.65 rounds to 1, giving a gap of 64, and is nulled. | R-289 | P1 | `scripts/utils/cta_percentiles.py:113`; counter at `scripts/fetch_menthorq_cta.py:244` | The check that makes a percentile trustworthy is optional, and its absence silently grants trust. `contradicted` requires `implied is not None` (`:113`), so when `z_score_3m` is absent, non-numeric or non-finite — the same vision extraction that mangled the percentile column — `percentile_from_z` returns None, `contradicted` is False, and the rounded `0` or `1` is written through unchanged. The module docstring calls the z-score "the check"; the path where the check does not exist is the PASS path, not the reject path. Nothing counts these rows either: the WARN at `fetch_menthorq_cta.py:244` enumerates only rows that WERE nulled, so a row that was never verifiable is indistinguishable from a verified one. |
| R-290 | P2 | `scripts/utils/cta_percentiles.py:73` | The fractional-scale detector is blind to exactly the values that carry the largest inversion cost. `fractional` additionally requires `any(not float(v).is_integer())` (`:73`), so a row correctly extracted from a fractional card whose three cells are all integral floats (`0.0`, `1.0`, `0.0`) fails the test, is treated as the 0-100 scale, and `1.0` becomes the **1st** percentile instead of the 100th — the precise int-1-vs-float-1.0 inversion the docstring immediately above it warns about. With no z-score present it publishes inverted; with one, it is nulled and correct data is discarded. |

The `__main__` path at `:245` passes `sys.argv[1:]` and is unaffected, so the divergence only exists on the library path the refactor just created. |

Every other entry in that map is a read-only data-fetch oneshot; this one now carries `ExecStartPre=… migrate.py --demo`, so an automated failure-page response applies schema migrations with no operator in the loop. The polkit list and the Python set are in sync so `test_allowlist_matches_the_polkit_grant` passes; nothing asserts anything about what the granted units DO. The allowlist's stated scope is "benign timer-owned data-fetch oneshots". |
| R-303 | P2 | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:641-644` (comment) vs `:645` and `:672` | The only written derivation of the deploy timeout no longer matches the timeout. The rationale block still describes the pre-T-160 budget — "SSH gets 36m and the job gets 40m" — while `timeout-minutes: 60` sits at `:645` and `command_timeout: 55m` at `:672`. `scripts/tests/test_ci_deploy_concurrency.py:82-96` asserts the real values, so the code is right and only the operator-facing explanation is wrong; but that comment's arithmetic is what a future tightening would be reasoned from. |
| R-304 | P1 | `web/components/IvRankPanel.tsx:144-146` (and `:75`); writer at `scripts/fetch_ivrank.py:632`, `:604`; passthrough at `web/app/api/ivrank/route.ts:65-67` | A total IV-rank feed outage paints an identical panel with a clock that reads as a run which just finished. `scripts/fetch_ivrank.py:632` sets `payload = {**cached, "scan_time": scan_time, "status": STATUS_STALE_SOURCE}` when BOTH IB and UW are dead — the previous payload re-served with a brand-new `scan_time`. The route deliberately passes both `stale_source` and `degraded_uw` through untransformed, making the panel the only place they could surface, and `grep -c status web/components/IvRankPanel.tsx` returns **0**: the component never mentions `status` at all. It builds the header clock from the restamped `scan_time` at `:144-146` (`toLocaleTimeString`, rendered as a bare "6:10 PM" with no date), so the panel shows the same IV RANK, the same regime colour, and a just-now timestamp. `degraded_uw` (IB dead, UW fallback in use) is equally invisible. The panel cannot say "this failed". |
| R-305 | P2 | `web/lib/freshnessRail.ts:53`, `:58`; `web/lib/marketSession.ts:33-49` | The rail's one coloured state fires roughly ten false alarms a year, which is how a warn tone gets trained away. `behind`/`overdue` are decided against `lastCompletedSessionDate(now)`, and that helper documents "Holidays are not modelled … a holiday weekday resolves to itself". On Thanksgiving, Christmas or Good Friday, after 16:00 ET `latestSession` becomes the holiday date, `asOf` (the previous real session) compares less-than, and the rail flips to `behind` — "Awaiting 2026-11-26" — then to amber `overdue` once the 22:10 UTC slot passes, for a session the exchange will never print, until the next evening. The mirror case: on an early-close day (13:00 ET) the 16:00 boundary reports "Current" for three hours after the session actually closed. |
| R-306 | P2 | `web/lib/freshnessRail.ts:53`; `web/components/FreshnessRail.tsx:61`, `:69-73`, `:96`; pinned by `web/tests/freshness-rail.test.ts:72-77`; reachable via `web/components/IvRankPanel.tsx:229` | The rail's entire job is to distinguish "current" from "unknown", and unknown resolves to current. `behind` is gated on `Boolean(asOf)`, so a null or empty `asOf` can never be behind and therefore never overdue. The component computes `state = "current"` and `anchorNote = "Current"` while the anchor itself renders `"---"`, with an `aria-label` reading "Data as of unknown. Next sample 3h 10m from now." — the healthy state complete with a ticking countdown. Reachable whenever `data.as_of` is null and `current.date` is an empty string, since the `??` at `IvRankPanel.tsx:229` does not catch `""` and neither does `asOf ?? "---"`. The current contract is pinned by `freshness-rail.test.ts:72-77` ("survives a missing data date" asserts `behind === false`), so that case needs rewriting onto the intended shape rather than deleting. |
| R-307 | P2 | `web/components/FreshnessRail.tsx:63-68`; `web/lib/useIvRank.ts:16`; claim at `web/lib/freshnessRail.ts:78-80` | A healthy night shows the alarm state for up to a full hour, every night, which is indistinguishable from the outage the state exists to report. `overdue` becomes true the instant `now` crosses the 22:10 UTC slot, because `asOf` is still yesterday's session until a new fetch lands — but `asOf` arrives only from the hourly poll (`useIvRank.ts:16`, `interval: 3_600_000`), and the timer carries `RandomizedDelaySec=120` plus job runtime on top. The 1s interval only re-renders the countdown; it does not refetch, so nothing shortens the false window. The comment at `freshnessRail.ts:78-80` claims "the DUE state absorbs" the randomised delay, but DUE is the alarm state, not a neutral one. |
| R-308 | P2 | `web/lib/freshnessRail.ts:72`, with `:49-50`, `:68`; styling at `web/app/globals.css:10955-10958` | The magnitude of a failure is the operator's only cue for whether to page someone, and it is capped at one interval. `msOverdue` is `overdue ? Math.max(0, elapsed) : 0` where `elapsed = now - lastSampleAt` and `lastSampleAt = nextSampleAt - intervalMs`, so `elapsed` is structurally bounded by a single interval. If the ivrank writer is dead for a week the overdue note resets to "0m 00s past the run" at 22:10 UTC each night and never exceeds "23h 59m" — a seven-day outage and a twenty-minute one look the same. Only the "As of" date carries the true age, and it is rendered in the same colour as a current one. |
| R-309 | P2 | `web/components/FreshnessRail.tsx:41-47`; sibling `web/lib/refreshSchedule.ts:64-71`; second clock at `web/components/IvRankPanel.tsx:145` | Two unlabeled clocks on different timezones sit inches apart on one rail. `targetLabel` formats the slot with `toLocaleTimeString([], {…})` and buckets Today/Tomorrow via `localDayKey` — the BROWSER's timezone, with no timezone marker — while the "As of" value beside it is an ET session DATE and the panel's other clock at `IvRankPanel.tsx:145` is also unlabeled local. For any user east of UTC+2 the 22:10 UTC daily slot renders "Tomorrow 00:10" against an ET session anchor. The sibling helper `nextRefreshLabel` deliberately suffixes "UTC" for exactly this reason. |
| R-310 | P1 | `web/app/api/menthorq/cta/image/route.tsx:311`, input read at `:91`, rate limit at `:292` | Nothing between the on-disk JSON and the rasterizer bounds the work, so one bad overnight extraction turns an image route into an OOM or CPU pin on the web host. `height = 50 + sectionCount * (36 + 28) + totalRows * 28 + 20` is computed straight from row count with no clamp, and `totalRows` is whatever the vision extractor wrote into `data/menthorq_cache/cta_*.json` — read at `:91` with `readFile` + `JSON.parse` and no size check. A malformed or duplicated extraction with a few thousand rows produces a 920 x 50,000+ px canvas that satori and resvg must lay out and rasterize (roughly 4 bytes/px, hundreds of MB) inside a `runtime = "nodejs"` route that exports no `maxDuration`, at 20 requests/minute. |
| R-311 | P1 | `web/lib/og-fonts.ts:8-11`; consumed at `web/app/api/menthorq/cta/image/route.tsx:302` | A partial asset deploy converts a retryable read error into a permanent, self-hiding outage of the whole share-image pipeline. `if (!fontRegular)` guards the cache at `:8`, but `fontRegular` is assigned at `:10` BEFORE `fontBold` is read at `:11`. If the Bold file is missing or unreadable the first call throws after poisoning the cache; every later call skips the load and hands `ImageResponse` `{ name: "IBM Plex Mono", data: fontBold /* null */, weight: 700 }`. The CTA image route and every other OG route then fail permanently with an opaque satori error until the process restarts. |
| R-312 | P1 | `web/lib/chat.ts:328-341`; driven from `web/components/ChatPanel.tsx:288`, cancel surface at `:364` | An operator cannot interrupt a long assistant turn, and the panel keeps re-rendering the transcript after it has been closed. `streamMessage` chops the full reply into 120-char pieces and awaits `sleep(8)` per piece, calling `setMessages` with a full `messages.map()` each time. There is no cap on `parts.length` and no cancellation token: a 400 KB tool-heavy reply is roughly 3,300 chunks — about 27 s of forced typing with about 3,300 full-array re-maps — and `ChatPanel` has no `AbortController` anywhere (only `cancelProposal` at `:364`, which cancels a proposal, not the stream). Unmounting the panel mid-turn does not stop the loop. |
| R-313 | P1 | `web/lib/agent/scannerProposal.ts:54`, `:63` | A missing datum rides into an order suggestion instead of blocking it. In the same template literal `iv_rv_edge` is guarded (`Number.isFinite(result.iv_rv_edge) ? … : "---"`) but `range_score` is interpolated raw, so a scanner run whose Python engine omits `range_score` yields the actionable statement "SPY short strangle: IV/RV edge 12.4, range score undefined." `alternativesFrom` at `:63` has the same hole — `SCORE ${Math.round(row.score)}` renders `SCORE NaN`. Neither path suppresses the proposal, and `buildScannerProposal` returns non-null only for engine-actionable candidates, so this reaches the operator as a recommendation. |
| R-314 | P2 | `web/components/ChatPanel.tsx:261`; server at `web/app/api/assistant/route.ts:50-61` | A single long-lived chat tab can spend unbounded model budget with no ceiling and no stop button. Every send maps the ENTIRE transcript into `conversation` and posts it whole; `safeMessages` validates role and type but caps neither message count nor per-message length, and forwards the lot into `runAssistantLoop` under `maxDuration = 300`. Token cost per session grows O(n^2) in turns, and there is no client-side abort to stop a run that has gone long. |
| R-315 | P2 | `web/app/api/menthorq/cta/image/route.tsx:102-106`, checked at `:298` | An unknown `?section=` returns a successful-looking blank plate instead of an error a consumer can react to. `loadLatestCta` returns `{ ...data, tables: {} }` when the named section is absent from the cache; `{}` is truthy, so the `if (!data?.tables)` 404 at `:298` does not fire, `activeSections` is empty, and the route returns **200** with a 70 px image containing only the "CTA POSITIONING" title bar. |
| R-316 | P2 | `web/lib/og-theme.ts:10`, `:12`, `:14`; current tokens at `web/app/globals.css:100`, `:101`, `:112` | The artifact posted publicly is off-brand and below the WCAG floor the app itself was fixed to clear. `BRAND.panelRaised = "#151c22"`, `BRAND.border = "#1e293b"`, `BRAND.muted = "#94a3b8"` and `BRAND.faint = "#475569"` are the pre-accessibility values; `globals.css` now defines `--bg-panel-raised: #1e252b /* was #151c22 */`, `--line-grid: #2e3947 /* was #1e293b — hairline 1.26 -> 1.57 */` and `--text-muted: #758192 /* was #475569 — 2.43 -> 4.65 */`, each with the contrast failure that motivated it recorded inline. The share and OG images still render the deprecated values. |
| R-317 | P2 | `web/components/ShareReportModal.tsx:81-83`, catch at `:111` | A share outage is reported to the operator as a parse error, and the button is stuck disabled with no way out but a reload. `const data = await res.json() as ShareResponse;` runs BEFORE the `!res.ok` branch at `:83`, so a 502/504 HTML error page or a plain-text 500 from `/api/menthorq/cta/share` throws inside `res.json()` and the catch at `:111` surfaces `"Unexpected token '<', \"<html>\"… is not valid JSON"` instead of the real failure. Neither fetch carries an `AbortController` or a timeout, so a hung share endpoint pins `sharing = true`. |
| R-318 | P2 | `web/components/CtaPage.tsx:100`, `:106` | The callout overstates the extreme by up to ten percentile points in a line an operator reads as a measurement. `bondShortCount` counts rows with `pctile(b.percentile_3m) <= 10` at `:100`, but the copy at `:106` asserts `${bondShortCount} bond contracts at 0th pctile, full duration short.` Two contracts at the 8th and 9th percentile are published as "2 bond contracts at 0th pctile". |
| R-319 | P2 | `scripts/clients/journal_basis.py:228`, `:240-241`, `:246`, `:260-263`; id namespaces at `scripts/ib_execute.py:336`, `scripts/tests/test_monitor_daemon/test_expiry_sweep.py:192`; schema at `scripts/db/migrations/0001_init.sql:71` | REL-071's new keyset pager can silently skip a concurrently written journal row, reaching the same dropped-row outcome through the transport the fix itself introduced. `trade_id` is `TEXT PRIMARY KEY` and the journal has heterogeneous id namespaces — date-prefixed ids from the placer (`ib_execute.py:336`), `ep-`-prefixed ids from the expiry sweep, Flex ids from rehydrate. The cursor at `:228` is `WHERE trade_id > ?`, i.e. LEXICOGRAPHIC, so enumeration order is not insertion order: once the cursor has advanced past the digit-prefixed range into `ep-…`, a fill written concurrently by the realtime daemon (its id starts with a digit) sorts below the cursor and is never returned. The row is not "unusable", it simply never arrives, so `_unusable_fill_key` cannot see it. This runs inside `service_cycle("orders-sync")` (`scripts/ib_orders.py:300`) while the fill writer is live. The other boundaries are correct and were checked: exactly `_JOURNAL_PAGE_SIZE == 200` rows loops once more and terminates on the empty page, duplicate sort keys are impossible on a primary key, and the non-advancing-cursor `RuntimeError` is unreachable-by-design rather than off-by-one. |

### Audited, clean in this delta (walked and verified — silence is not clearance)

- **Order-entry copy churn.** Most of the diff in `orders/place/route.ts`,
  `OrderActionsContext.tsx`, `nakedShortGuard.ts`, `computeLinearRisk.ts`,
  `OrderConfirmSummary.tsx`, `FuturesOrderForm.tsx` and `IndexOptionOrderForm.tsx` is
  em-dash-to-punctuation copy with no behavioural delta. `LocateFeeChip.tsx` is a genuine fix
  (both the `Invalid Date` render and the `data.source !== "none"` fail-through that crashed
  the confirm step).
- **`oldestQuoteTimestamp` fail-closed plumbing** (`pricesProtocol.ts:234-252`) — a null or
  unparseable leg ISO returns `undefined`, and all five call sites pass the result through, so
  `hasLiveQuote` correctly relabels a stale combo to CLOSE. Intentional, documented, correct.
- **`refreshSchedule.ts`** — `IV_RANK_REFRESH` is genuinely pinned to
  `cloud/services/radon-ivrank.timer` by `web/tests/refresh-schedule.test.ts:55-57`, and
  `nextRefreshUtc` handles both cadences and the passed-slot rollover correctly. The rail's
  defects are in how the derived slot is *interpreted*, not in the derivation.
- **`web/app/api/ivrank/route.ts:70-72`** serves on `result.ok && result.fresh`, not `ok`
  alone — not an R-125 instance.
- **`scripts/ci/path_filter.py`** fails safe in every direction probed: unknown trees force
  both gates (`:131-134`), an empty or all-zero `BASE_SHA` yields `[]` which `classify` maps to
  `(True, True)`, and `.claude/`, `tasks/`, `notebooks/` non-doc files route to both.
- **`vitest.config.ts`** only removed `retry`; nothing was narrowed, and both files the comment
  names raise their own 20 s budget.
- **`cloud/config/installed-units.sha256`** — all 116 hashes match their current unit files,
  including the bumped `radon-demo-mirror.service`. No `continue-on-error` survives in `ci.yml`.
  `required-status-checks.json` is honestly empty and its consumer degrades to the `needs:`
  assertion rather than passing vacuously.
- **Migration replay** — all 55 DDL migrations use `IF NOT EXISTS` and 0050 remains the only
  real `ALTER TABLE` (R-153, already filed).
- **`_record_error_health` additions** in `fetch_credit_spread.py:465-483` and
  `fetch_iei_hyg.py:412-430` are correct: the raise path always leaves a row, the heartbeat is
  best-effort and cannot mask the original exception, and `except Exception → record → raise`
  in both Equibles fetchers does cover `EquiblesClient()` construction. The Equibles client's
  retry is bounded (`_MAX_RETRY_AFTER = 60.0`, `max_retries`, exponential backoff) with a
  timeout on every request.
- **`web/lib/shareReportPath.ts`** — the `?path=` traversal guard is correct (exact parent-dir
  equality plus a per-type basename pattern); no bypass found. **`web/lib/useSort.ts`** handles
  nulls explicitly with no throwing comparator. **`optionsChainUtils.computeNetOptionQuote`**
  returns `undefined` for manual/unquoted legs so `hasLiveQuote` reads false.
- **Brand enforcement** — no raw hex and no panel radius above 4px in the audited components
  (`ShareReportModal` uses 3px throughout). The `--wash-*` token block in `globals.css:17-19`
  resolves `--warn` on the same element as `[data-theme="light"]`, so the lazy-substitution
  claim in its comment holds. The one gap is the OG/share theme, filed at R-316.
- **`withSessionIbDailyPnl`** (`ibDailyPnlSession.ts:82-91`) is behaviour-identical for the
  pre-existing 2-arg shape and correctly threaded from `portfolio.last_sync`. Sign conventions
  in `computeStockRtMv` / `positionDirectionSign` are correct throughout the changed hunks, and
  `resolveNaturalSpreadQuote`'s GCD divisor cannot be zero.
- **The REL-071 keyset pager's numeric boundaries** are correct: exactly 200 rows loops once
  more and terminates on the empty page, duplicate sort keys are impossible on a primary key,
  and the non-advancing-cursor `RuntimeError` is unreachable-by-design rather than off-by-one.
  Only the lexicographic *ordering* across id namespaces is defective (R-319).
- **`RegimePanel` / `GexPanel` / `VcgPanel` / `BacktestPanel` / `RegimeRail` /
  `WorkspaceSections` / `sectionTooltips` / `utils` / `MetricCards` / `AssetDeck` /
  `HostMetricsStrip` / `[ticker]/not-found`** — changes in this range are copy edits with no
  behavioural content.

## Delta audit 2026-08-28

**Range:** `789aabea..HEAD` (`c6d08fbd`) — 24 commits, 262 files. Anchor verified
(`git rev-parse --verify 789aabea^{commit}` resolves to `789aabeaae4ae4c6d60a586102738284cdb26609`;
`git merge-base --is-ancestor 789aabea HEAD` confirms it is an ancestor). Per the 2026-08-22 lesson
the range was split at the last commit touching `RELIABILITY_LOG.md` (`cc2ac65e`, the squashed
REL-094…REL-108 remediation merge, PR #111): seven subsystem walks were scoped to the feature
commits (58 source files after dropping tests, `web/e2e`, docs, `site/` and JSON fixtures), each
capped at ~12 files, and an eighth walk reviewed the remediation half as a regression review.

**No branch collision.** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'reliability/*'` returned nothing before
numbering began — every prior nightly branch is merged and deleted. Numbering starts at R-320 /
REL-109, continuing the frozen contract.

**Standing sweeps** ran in the lead context before the walks reported. Sweeps 1-5 and 7 hold: the
halt chokepoints are present at `ib_execute.py:444`, `ib_place_order.py:242`,
`ib_order_manage.py:183`, `monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:477` and four FastAPI routes;
`_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` is intact at `server.py:4982` and enforced at `:5091`/`:5169`;
`check_order_limits` is wired, and last week's gap (`ib_execute.py` had the halt but no limits) is
now closed at `:454`; the exit-orders ack-poll is present (`_ack_perm_id`, `_confirm_placement`);
`daemon_state` still routes through the bounded writer. Both new units (`vixts`, `disk-cleanup`)
are in BOTH watchdog catalogs. Sweep 6 found R-351 — `exit_order_service.py` places a live order
with neither guard — which no scoped walk could have seen, since no walk's file list contained it.

**Two of last week's P0 fixes do not hold.** R-320 (REL-094) and R-324 (REL-102a) are both defects
in the 2026-08-27 remediation, and R-325 re-opens NF-8 for the second consecutive week. R-320 was
reproduced by executing the fix's own test seed in the lead context. Four of the eight claims the
regression walk checked were confirmed holding and are listed below.

### Findings

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-320 | P0 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:289-295, :263-266` | **REL-094's P0 fix closes only half of R-274.** `_unusable_fill_key` returns `_bucket_key(payload)` whenever the key normalises, so contract corruption that still produces a *wrong* key never reaches the shape test and forms its own phantom bucket. Reproduced in the lead context against the fix's own seed (`test_journal_realized.py:379-395`): the healthy triple yields `{'c1': 3000.0}`, while `strike=0`, `strike='0'`, `strike=600` and `expiry='20260819'` each yield `{'c1': 4000.0}` with NO warning logged. Second mechanism, same function: `_is_option_shaped` tests `str(payload.get(field) or '').strip()`, so a numeric `0` strike is falsy and reads as field-absent, and journal payloads carry no `sec_type` key at all (real payload keys are `action, commission, contracts, date, expiry, fill_price, ib_exec_id, multiplier, right, strike, ticker, total_cost`), making the `OPT` early-return at `:257` dead for production rows. The four added tests inject only `''`, `None`, `'JAN 2027'` and a missing key — every one fails normalisation, so the acceptance criteria are blind to the surviving half. |
| R-321 | P0 | `scripts/perf_twr_builder.py:632, :620, :551, :1626` | **The mirrored-flow coverage bound fails open, and the payload severity explicitly defers to it.** `_query_turso` returns `None` on ANY exception (`:551-552`) and `load_flows_coverage_through` returns `None` on an empty `twr_subperiods` (`:620-621`); `bound_observations_to_coverage` then does `if not covered_through: return observations` (`:632-633`), passing the FULL NAV series through unbounded. Because `bounded` is non-empty, the `FlowSet.failed('mirrored_flows_cover_nothing_...')` escape at `:1626` never fires. Every session past the mirror's real coverage is chained asserting an implicit flow of 0.0, so a real deposit publishes as investment return — the docstring at `:629-630` names this as the exact class the function exists to prevent. The comment at `:1640-1643` justifies keeping `FLOWS_SOURCE_MIRROR` at `info` on the grounds that "the mirror's age is policed by the coverage bound below, not by the severity" — the one mechanism it defers to is the one that fails open, so status stays `ok`. |
| R-322 | P0 | `web/components/InstrumentDetailModal.tsx:229` | The opening (non-close-out) branch of `riskInput` builds `netPremium` from `parsedPrice` (the raw limit input) where `totalCost` (`:174`), `description` (`:175-177`) and the close-out branch (`:218`) all correctly use the `riskPrice` computed at `:173`. For a `STP` order `pricesValidForOrderType` returns `stopOk` alone (`web/lib/order/stopOrder.ts:35`), so a blank Limit field with a valid Stop passes `isValid` while `parsedPrice = parseFloat('') = NaN`. `NaN` flows into `useOrderRisk`, making every max-loss / max-gain / breakeven `NaN`, and `okToSubmit` is `coverageStatus === 'resolved'` (`useOrderRisk.ts:848`) with no finiteness check — so the ticket permits submit on a meaningless risk verdict. `OrderTab.tsx:496` does it correctly. |
| R-323 | P0 | `scripts/flex_delivery_ingest.py:37-38` | The Activity branch runs two independent persisting writers back-to-back with no transaction and no short-circuit: `cash_code = cash_flow_sync.main([...])` at `:37`, then `perf_twr_builder.build_and_persist(from_file=..., persist=True)` at `:38` UNCONDITIONALLY, with `cash_code` only inspected afterwards to shape the return dict at `:40`. `upsert_cash_flow_rows` chunks its writes (`writer.py:604-618`), so a mid-upsert Turso failure leaves earlier chunks committed; the TWR series is then computed over half-written cash flows and persisted as authoritative. A crash between the two lines leaves the same split-brain with no record at all. |
| R-324 | P1 | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:579-586` | REL-102(a) corrected `stage-release.needs` to mirror `deploy.needs` (both now carry `web-coverage` and `py-coverage`), but `stage-release.if` omits the two coverage result checks that `deploy.if` carries at `:666`/`:668`. GitHub drops the implicit `success()` requirement on `needs` as soon as the `if` contains a status function, and `!cancelled()` is one — so a failing `web-coverage` correctly skips `deploy` while `stage-release` still runs and SSHes to `secrets.VPS_HOST` (`:596`) to execute the pushed commit's own `deploy.sh` on the live trading host. `test_ci_gate_integrity.py:28-49` compares `needs` name sets only and never inspects the `if:` expression, so it stays green. Same "mechanism present, coverage incomplete" shape as R-299. |
| R-325 | P1 | `scripts/tests/test_watchdog_catalog_parity.py:73-81, :47` | REL-103(c) widened `_exec_targets` to resolve `-m package.module` and `.js`, but the blindness is in `_names_in`, which only sees literal or `SERVICE`/`SERVICE_NAME`/`HEALTH_SERVICE`-constant arguments. Running the module's own `_health_names_written_by` over `cloud/services/*.timer` in the lead context: **25 of 54 timer-backed units yield an empty name set**, and `EXEMPT = {}` so not one is declared exempt. `test_every_timer_backed_writer_is_in_both_catalogs` iterates only the names it resolved, so a unit it cannot parse is skipped rather than flagged, and `test_the_enumeration_finds_something` asserts only that the dict is non-empty. At least ten of the 25 demonstrably DO write health rows the test cannot see (`bpi_scan.py:689` via a `service_cycle` helper, `perf_twr_builder.py:1746` via `_PERF_TWR_SERVICE` which the `_SERVICE_CONST` regex does not match, `disk_cleanup.py:577`, `db_backup.py:555`, `media_backup.py:292`, `archive_portfolio_snapshots.py:307`). NF-8 re-opened for the second consecutive week. |
| R-326 | P1 | `scripts/flex_delivery_ingest.py:30, scripts/db/migrations/0059_flex_deliveries.sql:3` | The `flex_deliveries` dedup table is inert. `content_sha256` is computed at `:30` and only echoed into the result dict (`:42`, `:56`); the table appears nowhere in the repo outside its own migration, so nothing inserts a fingerprint and nothing consults one. The migration's comment — "content_sha256 is the PK so the same file is never applied twice" — is false, as are `period_from`/`period_to`, which nothing populates. Ingest idempotency rests entirely on the row-level upsert keys, which R-329 shows are ordinal-derived and unstable across a reissued statement. |
| R-327 | P1 | `scripts/journal_rehydrate.py:733-745` | The Turso journal write is a per-entry loop with no transaction, and an exception partway through returns `{'ok': False, 'imported': 0}` even though earlier entries are already committed. A 40-entry file that 502s on entry 26 leaves 25 trades in the canonical `journal` store while reporting that nothing was written; `flex_delivery_ingest.py:57` surfaces `imported: 0` and the operator re-runs against a store they believe is untouched. |
| R-328 | P1 | `scripts/cash_flow_sync.py:798-800` | The `FlexSendDisabled` handler emits `_emit_status('ok', 'file_ingest_only', ...)` and returns `EXIT_OK`. The scheduled daily unit invokes `cash_flow_sync.py` with neither `--from-file` nor `--sendrequest`, so every day it takes this branch, reports healthy and exits 0 while ingesting nothing. `cash-flow-sync` carries a 25h open / 4d closed freshness window (`scripts/watchdog/services.py:72`), and a green `ok` row every night means that window can never fire — the exact stale-data condition it exists to catch, and the inverse of the fail-closed intent stated at `flex_send.py:1-4`. |
| R-329 | P1 | `scripts/cash_flow_sync.py:451-453` | The duplicate-`transactionID` disambiguator derives its synthetic id from DOCUMENT POSITION (`unique_id = txn_id if occurrence == 0 else f'{txn_id}#{occurrence}'`) rather than row content, so the id of a given economic row is unstable across statement regenerations. IBKR reissuing a statement with one of a duplicate trio dropped, reordered, or with a row inserted ahead produces different ids for the same economics; `upsert_cash_flow_rows` is insert/update-only with no delete pass, so the orphan survives as a phantom row and the cash-flow total is overstated, or amounts shift a slot and double-count. `dropped_duplicate_ids` (`:850`) can now never fire, so the old visibility warning is dead. |
| R-330 | P1 | `scripts/trade_blotter/flex_query.py:188-194, :203-205` | The trades ingest calls `FlexQueryFetcher._parse_xml`, whose per-row parse is fail-OPEN: each `<Trade>` is wrapped in `try/except Exception` that prints a warning and `continue`s, and any `<Trade>` missing `symbol` silently returns `None`. A file whose last 60 of 200 executions carry a corrupted attribute imports 140 fills and returns `{'ok': True, 'imported': 140}` (`journal_rehydrate.py:747-753`), `flex_delivery_ingest.py:54` sets `ok: True`, and the process exits 0. Sixty fills are missing from the canonical trades store behind a green exit, and the dropped rows are not reflected in `skipped` either. The file-level gate is sound (a truncated document raises); this is the row level. |
| R-331 | P1 | `scripts/fetch_vixts.py:231-255` | `run()`'s only `try`/`except` covers the `CboeClient` constructor (`:220-229`). Everything after it — `_fetch_all` at `:232`, `parse_index_csv`, `join_series`, `ensure_plausible_series` at `:246`, `_write_db` — is uncovered, so any raise exits the oneshot with NO `service_health` row and yesterday's `ok` left standing. This is the R-276 class recurring in a file shipped the same week R-276 was filed against `fetch_vixcor`, which now carries the fix and an explicit R-276 comment. `vixts_math.py:120`'s claim that "raising keeps the run retryable and writes an error heartbeat" is false. |
| R-332 | P1 | `web/app/api/vixts/route.ts:52` | The route serves on `result.ok` alone, so the `VIXTS_MAX_AGE_MS` budget declared at `:21` and passed at `:49` is computed and discarded. Every sibling Cboe/daily route uses `result.ok && result.fresh ? result.data : staleCollapse(...)` — `vixcor/route.ts:72` is the direct twin. A dead `radon-vixts.service` therefore keeps serving a week-old snapshot with no `stale` or `missing` marker, and `VixTsPanel.tsx:189` renders a confident coloured BACKWARDATION badge and ratio for a dead feed. |
| R-333 | P1 | `scripts/fetch_vixts.py:236-240` | The all-304 path builds `payload = {**cached, 'scan_time': scan_time}` and calls `_write_db(..., rows_changed=False)` with no `health_error`, restamping unchanged data as fresh and heartbeating `ok` without checking that `cached['data_date']` is anywhere near the current session. `fetch_vixcor.restate_cached_payload(cached, scan_time=..., now=...)` re-ages the verdict and can return a `health_error`; this does not. A stuck CDN edge answering 304 indefinitely keeps `scan_time` minutes old and the 26h watchdog window green while the ratio is frozen at an old session. |
| R-334 | P1 | `scripts/ib_option_chain.py:99-103` | The new `_connect_with_retry` catches bare `except Exception`, so it retries every failure class identically to the transient handshake timeout it was written for — including 2FA-pending, a logged-out Gateway, and the auto-allocator's client-id exhaustion. `ib_option_chain.py` is not in `_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` (`server.py:4982`, which holds only `ib_place_order.py`), so the `auth_state != 'authenticated'` refusal at `server.py:5091` never applies to it. Against a Gateway awaiting 2FA the script now burns 3 handshakes plus 2s of sleep instead of failing in 4s, and each attempt re-enters `_connect_auto_allocate` (`ib_client.py:406-441`), which walks all 30 ids in `SUBPROCESS_ID_RANGE` on an in-use error — up to 90 serialised handshakes on the same queue `ib_place_order.py` must win to transmit an order. |
| R-335 | P1 | `scripts/ib_realtime_server.js:101-107` | `sdNotifyViaSocat` spawns a child with no `"error"` listener and immediately writes to its stdin. A missing or unspawnable `socat` reports ENOENT asynchronously via an `error` event, and `child.stdin.end(state)` additionally errors on the never-opened pipe; both fire after the surrounding `try` at `:118-121` has returned, so neither can be caught. On a host where `NOTIFY_SOCKET` and `WATCHDOG_USEC` are set but `systemd-notify` is absent, Node raises `ERR_UNHANDLED_ERROR` and the realtime market-data process exits — once at `READY=1` (`:406`) and again every `heartbeatMs` (`:414`), a crash loop that drops every WebSocket quote subscriber. |
| R-336 | P1 | `web/lib/orders/sessionWindow.ts:75` | `classifyOrderSession` derives its verdict from `marketStateAt`, which models neither US holidays nor early closes — `web/lib/useMarketHours.ts:11-12` says so, and the only calendar logic is a weekend test at `:30` plus fixed 09:30-16:00 minutes at `:34` — yet the chip states a definitive fill claim. At 14:30 ET on an early-close day or any weekday holiday the chip reads `RTH` with tone `rth`, every resting DAY order is presented as still fillable, and the `EXPIRES` warning at `:55` that would otherwise fire post-close is suppressed. |
| R-337 | P1 | `web/lib/orders/sessionWindow.ts:55` | `chipLabel` recognises only the literal `"DAY"`, but a grouped combo row carries `tif: "MIXED"` when its legs disagree (`web/lib/openOrderCombos.ts:559`), so a combo containing DAY legs falls through to the GTC branch and is labelled `NEXT RTH` — the label meaning "survives to the next session". A vertical with one DAY and one GTC leg reads as resting overnight; in fact the DAY leg is cancelled at the close and the GTC leg rests alone as a naked short. Rendered on both `MobileOrderList.tsx:441` and `WorkspaceSections.tsx:3223`. |
| R-338 | P1 | `web/lib/orders/sessionWindow.ts:41` | `sessionEligibility` returns `"extended"` for `FUT`/`FOP` unconditionally, ignoring the order's own `outsideRth` flag, where the STK rule at `:42` correctly requires it. Futures are placeable and `web/app/api/orders/place/route.ts:327` auto-derives the flag as `body.outsideRth ?? getMarketStateFromDate() !== 'open'`, so a futures order entered at 11:00 ET is transmitted with `outsideRth: false`; at 18:00 ET the chip still renders `EXT LIVE` with the hint "Can fill after 16:00 ET" for an order that is inert until the next morning. |

| R-347 | P1 | `scripts/ib_sync.py:1305-1307` | `build_fill_basis` prefers `Execution.avgPrice` over the per-execution `price` while weighting it by that single execution's `shares` (`:1303-1304`). IB reports `avgPrice` as the running average over the ORDER's cumulative quantity, so one order for 25 filling 10 @ $5.00 then 15 @ $6.00 yields lots `(10, 5.00)` and `(15, 5.60)`. `_session_fill_cover` (`:830`) computes vwap `(10x5.00 + 15x5.60)/25 = $5.36` against a true $5.60, sets `entry_cost = $13,400` against a real $14,000, and stamps `basis_source = 'session_fills'` (`:926`) so it outranks IB's own `avgCost` and the journal. AVG ENTRY, Initial Value, Total P&L and Today P&L all inherit the error. |
| R-348 | P1 | `scripts/ib_sync.py:820-821 vs :860` | `_session_fill_cover` (a4b882d1) and `_session_fill_open_date` (0b1baddc) use DIFFERENT coverage tests, so a same-session round trip takes today's basis but keeps a stale `entry_date`. The former matches newest same-sign lots and skips opposite-sign lots entirely (`:821`); the latter requires the NET signed session quantity to equal live size (`:860`). Holding 25 long overnight, selling 25 and rebuying 25 intraday gives the cover an exact same-sign match (basis becomes today's reopen price, `basis_source = 'session_fills'`) while the open date sees `net = 0 != 25` and returns `None`, falling back to the journal's earliest date. Total P&L is then measured off today's basis while Today P&L uses yesterday's close — the same contradiction 0b1baddc was written to kill, reached through the intraday round trip instead. |

A client-side abort does not stop an already-spawned FastAPI subprocess, so two 300s `flow_report.py` runs occupy the general lane — the operator's own Refresh manufactures the capacity shed the delta's retry was added to survive. A scan that never completed, a FastAPI 401, or a persistent 502 capacity shed therefore all render as a FRESH directional verdict with no error banner. | R-354 | P2 | `scripts/api/server.py:459-476, :2387-2394` | `_run_script_retrying_capacity` is bounded by attempt count but has no total wall-clock deadline and no in-flight dedupe at its only call site. `POST /flow-analysis/{ticker}` passes `timeout=300, retries=2, delay_s=8`, so worst-case single-request wall clock is `3 x 300 + 16 = 916s` with the FastAPI worker held throughout — against a proxy that gives up at 130s (`web/app/api/flow-analysis/[ticker]/route.ts:108`) and then takes the stale-cache branch. | R-368 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/disk_cleanup.py:462-480, :606-634` | There is no dry-run mode, no cap on bytes or paths deleted per run, and no plausibility refusal when the computed target set is unexpectedly large. `main()` calls `run_cleanup()` with no arguments and every category's deletions execute immediately, so a selector regression — a widened `RELEASE_NAME_RE` at `:110`, a `PROTECTED_PATHS` typo — reaches production as an unrecoverable rmtree on its first firing rather than as a dry-run log line. |

| R-371 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/disk_cleanup.py:378-383, :336` | `_run` uses `check=True`, so the first failing `docker image rm` aborts the entire loop and every remaining prunable image is skipped, with the `reclaimed` accounting discarded. | R-372 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/db_backup.py:462-470` | `sync_offbox` never verifies that an uploaded object landed at the expected size — `client.upload_file` returns and the run immediately adds the LOCAL `path.stat().st_size` to `bytes_uploaded` with no `head_object` confirmation. | R-373 | P2 | `scripts/ib_sync.py:831` | `_session_fill_cover` hardcodes `entry_cost = need * vwap * 100.0` when the contract's own multiplier is available and read correctly elsewhere — `fetch_positions` persists `float(getattr(contract, 'multiplier', ...))` at `:946`, but `build_fill_basis` keys only on `symbol\|expiry\|right\|strike` (`:1296-1300`) and discards it. A corporate-action-adjusted OCC contract (multiplier 10 or 50) or a mini option covered by session fills gets an `entry_cost` wrong by the multiplier ratio, and because `basis_source = 'session_fills'` it beats the journal's correct lot basis, corrupting AVG ENTRY, Initial Value and `max_risk` (`:574`). |
| R-374 | P2 | `scripts/ib_sync.py:924-926` | The session-fill basis override is applied PER LEG with no all-legs gate, while the position-level `basis_source` and `session_fill_date` both require every leg (`:590-593`, `:640-646`). Rolling one leg of a vertical today and leaving the other overnight gives the rolled leg today's session VWAP and the held leg IB's lagged `avgCost`, so `total_entry_cost` — which feeds `max_risk` for defined-risk structures (`:571-574`) — is a sum corresponding to no actual trade, while `session_fill_date` is `None` so `entry_date` stays old. |
| R-375 | P2 | `web/app/api/blotter/route.ts:53, :81` | A journal read returning zero rows is served as an authoritative empty blotter with HTTP 200: `readJournalRows` returns `null` on `result.rows.length === 0`, `buildFromJournal` returns `null`, and GET falls to `emptyBlotter()` — `closed_trades: 0, realized_pnl: 0, as_of: ""`. A Turso replica pointed at the wrong database, or a journal not yet rehydrated, is indistinguishable from a genuinely flat book; `useBlotter`'s `extractTimestamp` maps the empty string to `null` so no timestamp contradicts it, and POST now returns 404 unconditionally (`:98-104`) so the operator cannot force a rebuild. |

Introduced by the fix; `web/tests/share-image-bounds.test.ts` tests only `computeCtaImageHeight` and never the loop. |
| R-377 | P2 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:11-14, :63-64` | Only the `_unusable_fill_key` docstring was corrected by REL-094. The module header still promises "A contract only yields figures when its journal history is COMPLETE", and `realized_pnl_by_exec_id` still says option rows without a full contract key are simply "ignored" — the exact claim REL-094 was raised to retract. A reader auditing this path concludes non-normalising rows are excluded by design and stops, which is how the strike-0 case in R-320 survived two remediation passes. |

### Confirmed holding (2026-08-27 remediation regression review)

- **REL-101(a)** — the coverage gate in `fetch_equibles_ats_venue_share.py:684-699` sits BEFORE `_write_db_cache` at `:701`, and `_CYCLE_FATAL = (EquiblesAuthError, EquiblesRateLimitError)` at `:609` is caught at `:637` and re-raised at `:642` ahead of the per-ticker handler. A thin cycle cannot replace the prior snapshot or emit `ok`.
- **REL-105(b)** — `web/lib/og-fonts.ts:18-22` resolves both `readFile`s in one `Promise.all` and assigns `cached` only after both succeed, so a missing Bold face rejects the whole call and the next call retries. No partial-load poisoning path exists.
- **REL-105(a) clamp** — `ctaImageLayout.ts:26-44` clamps sections to 12 and rows to `MAX_IMAGE_ROWS = 400`, and `route.tsx:322` computes the height from the already-truncated tables, which is the same value `ImageResponse` receives at `:391`. (The truncation loop it introduced is R-376.)
- **REL-102(a), `needs` level** — `test_ci_gate_integrity.py:28-35` derives the gate from `jobs["deploy"]["needs"]` with no hard-coded frozenset, and the fault-injection case at `:85-102` proves a gate job added to `deploy` alone is caught. The gap is the `if:` expression (R-324), not the `needs` list.

### Backlog

| Task | Finding | Scope | Acceptance criteria (incl. verification) |
|---|---|---|---|
| REL-109 | R-320, R-377 | **P0 — finish closing R-274: contract corruption that NORMALISES must poison too.** REL-094 keys usability on `_bucket_key` returning `None`; extend it to a plausibility domain checked by the caller — `strike > 0`, `right` exactly `C`/`P`, `expiry` parsing to a real date inside the contract's lifetime — and treat a shaped-but-implausible row as unusable. ## Delta audit 2026-08-29

**Range:** `c6d08fbd..HEAD` (`f7b5eeb9`) — 37 commits, 191 files. Anchor verified
(`git rev-parse --verify c6d08fbd^{commit}` resolves to `c6d08fbd367e704fd0b13aa0b569e23a177cb121`;
`git merge-base --is-ancestor c6d08fbd HEAD` confirms it is an ancestor). Per the 2026-08-22 lesson
the range was split at the last commit touching `RELIABILITY_LOG.md` (`02133f8b`, the squashed
REL-109…REL-130 remediation merge, PR #127): eight subsystem walks were scoped to the 35 feature
commits (54 source files after dropping tests, `web/e2e`, docs, `site/` and `context/`), each
capped at ~12 files, and a ninth walk reviewed the remediation half as a regression review.

**No branch collision.** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'reliability/*'` was run BEFORE numbering and
returned only `reliability/2026-08-28`, which is merged. Numbering starts at R-378 / REL-131,
continuing the frozen contract.

**Standing sweeps** ran in the lead context before the walks reported. Sweeps 1-5 hold: the halt
chokepoints are present at `ib_execute.py:444`, `ib_place_order.py:242`, `ib_order_manage.py:183`,
`exit_order_service.py:345`, `monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:477` and `server.py:2634`;
`_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` is intact at `server.py:5073` and enforced at `:5182`/`:5260`;
`check_order_limits` is wired at `ib_place_order.py:255`, `ib_execute.py:455` and
`exit_order_service.py:352` (REL-129 shipped the guards, not the deletion); the exit-orders
ack-poll is present (`_ack_perm_id:191`, `_confirm_placement:678`); `upsert_daemon_state` still
routes through the bounded Hrana transport (`writer.py:2025-2037`). The whole-repo manifest sweep
is clean: hashing every file under `cloud/services/` against `installed-units.sha256` gives 0
mismatches, 0 stale entries, and the only unpinned units are `radon-llm-index.{service,timer}`,
both carrying `not-installed:` allowlist entries.

**Sweep 6 found two more order sites** (R-427, R-428) that no scoped walk could see, because
neither file is in the delta: `monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py` and `ib_order_manage.py`
both carry the halt and not the limits. **Sweep 7 was run over all 55 timer-backed units** using the
parity test's OWN resolver, not a reimplementation — `_health_names_written_by` yields an empty
name set for **16 of 55**, down from last week's 25 of 54, and every one is exempt so the test is
legitimately green. But eight of those exemptions are labelled `gap: writes no service_health row`
about units that heartbeat on every fire (R-412), and the two GENUINE gaps — `forecast-nightly` and
`signals-refresh` — are in neither catalog (R-402).

**Regression review of `02133f8b` (REL-109…REL-130).** Four fixes confirmed holding by execution,
two do not fully hold:

- **REL-110 (R-321) HOLDS.** Stubbing `_query_turso` at both causes and calling the real
  `_apply_mirrored_flow_coverage` against a 3-session NAV series with an $80k jump on the uncovered
  session returns `FlowsStatus.FAILED` with an empty `by_date` for both, and only the raising cause
  emits `FLOWS_COVERAGE_QUERY_FAILED` — the two meanings are genuinely separated. `build_payload`
  short-circuits on `not flows.is_usable` into `_suppressed_payload`, so the deposit cannot publish
  as an 80% return. Partial coverage still trims rather than over-refusing.
- **REL-111 (R-322) HOLDS, and wider than filed.** `summaryFiguresAreFinite`
  (`useOrderRisk.ts:303-317`) joins the gate at ALL FOUR `okToSubmit` sites that can return true
  (`:741`, `:770-771`, `:831-834`, `:880-881`); the finding named one. Its field list is every
  numeric-typed field in `OrderPresentationSummary`, and `maxLossUnbounded` correctly survives as a
  null figure rather than a non-finite number, so the Gate-1 advisory path is untouched.
- **REL-129 (R-351) HOLDS.** `exit_order_service.py:343-360` calls the halt then the limits, both
  BEFORE `qualify_contracts` at `:385` and `place_order` at `:424`, so a halted host never opens the
  socket. The module was not deleted and consistently so — the installer and both client-id
  reservations (`ib_client.py:118`, `utils/ib_connection.py:23`) are intact. The operator question
  about retiring the daemon was correctly left open rather than resolved unattended.
- **REL-123 (R-341) HOLDS.** The lock is in-process (`disk_cleanup.py:123-157`, `O_NOFOLLOW` +
  `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`), taken at `:829` before `run_cleanup`, returning `EX_TEMPFAIL` at `:836`, with
  no `/home/radon` path in the `User=root` ExecStart. `cloud/tests/test_root_execution_paths.py` and
  `test_disk_cleanup_safety.py`: 42 passed.
- **REL-112 (R-323, R-361) is PARTIAL — R-379.** Both halves shipped correctly, but the delivery
  fingerprint is claimed before any writer runs and is never released, so the failure the fix
  correctly stops publishing is now permanently unrepairable and reports `ok` on retry.
- **REL-114 (R-325) is PARTIAL — R-412.** The parser widening is real and the count improved, but
  the exemption reasons the fix added as the anti-recurrence mechanism are factually false for 8 of
  16 entries, and the reason assertion checks only the prefix.

**NF-8 is re-opened for the THIRD consecutive week** (R-412), and NF-9 recurs in the job this delta
catalogued (R-400). The week's theme is **suppression**: five separate mechanisms shipped in this
range to stop a false page, and four of them are broader than the flap they were written for
(R-382, R-395, R-397, R-398, R-399).

**Cross-walk convergence.** Three findings were reached independently by two walks each and merged
before numbering: R-379 (Flex claim, from the Flex walk and the regression walk), R-412
(EXEMPT_UNITS, from standing sweep 7 and the regression walk), R-402 (`forecast-nightly`, from
sweep 7 and the observability walk). One walk claim was REJECTED by the lead: the control-plane walk
filed drop-in drift as invisible to `drift_audit`; reading `_live_unit_counter` shows the opposite —
the auditor will go permanently RED on all five app units, which is filed as R-392 with the
rejected half recorded in the row.

Concrete: `TRADE BEST \| XLK 210C` links `?expiry=2027-01-15&legs=BUY:1x210C&src=leap`; IB omits the Jan-2027 LEAP; the builder arms **BUY 1x 2026-09-04 210C** with the mid populated and `isValidPrice` true, under a ticket reading PREFILLED FROM LEAP SCAN.
