Two returns fire there: `66` when any `CONTROL_PLANE_SOURCES` entry is missing from `$CLOUD_SOURCE`, and `75` when `/var/lib/radon/ib-gateway-transition.json` exists. The five `services/radon-*.service.d/runtime-container.conf` entries added at `:46-50` exist only at SHAs at or after `702ae26a`, and the INSTALLED helper is always the new one, so any rollback to an earlier release enumerates sources the restored checkout does not contain and returns 66 with radon-api, radon-nextjs, radon-relay, radon-monitor and radon-newsfeed all down. Unlike `install_manifest_units` (`UNIT_BACKUP_PREFIX` journal) and bootstrap (`rollback_bundle`), `refresh_control_plane` has no backup and no restore. The 75 path means an IB 2FA transition in flight during a deploy now takes the app tier down as a side effect. |
| R-381 | P0 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:26-27`, `:192-193`, `:150`; `cloud/scripts/bootstrap-control-plane.sh:81-82`, `:398-400`; `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:151-152` | **The app containers are given read-write access to the control plane's own state directory.** `STATE_DIR=/var/lib/radon` and the run line mounts `-v "${STATE_DIR}:/var/lib/radon"` — the whole directory, not `media/`. That directory holds `control-plane-manifest.sha256` and `control-plane-ready` (installed 0644 root, but inside a dir created 0750 **radon:radon**), the root deploy state at `deploy/active-units`, and the IB 2FA lease / control guard / transition files. The container runs as uid/gid `radon`, and write permission on the PARENT directory is all that `unlink`/`rename` needs. So the newsfeed container — headless Chromium, `--ipc host`, `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_SANDBOX=0`, parsing hostile pages — can delete `control-plane-ready` (breaking `deploy.sh:204-212`'s readiness gate), swap the manifest digest, or rename `deploy/` away and destroy the unit-install transaction journal a failed deploy needs to revert. Host mode had the same reach, but confinement is the entire point of this drop-in. |
| R-382 | P0 | `scripts/health_service/probes.py:131-142` (`DEPENDENCY_UNITS`), `:246-254`; `scripts/watchdog/external_probe.py:94` | **`degraded` has no dwell bound, so a permanently dead `radon-monitor` is edge-green forever.** `35071d85` moved `radon-newsfeed.service` and `radon-monitor.service` into `DEPENDENCY_UNITS` to absorb a `Restart=always` flap, but `aggregate_state` is a pure function of ONE instantaneous snapshot — verified: there is no timestamp, dwell counter or consecutive-sample input anywhere in `scripts/health_service/` outside `turso_http.py`. `unit_coarse_state` collapses `failed`, `inactive` and `deactivating` all to `down`, so a unit that died two seconds ago and one masked for a week are the same input. Any dependency in `_DOWNISH` returns `degraded`, which off-box converts to `{"ok": 1, "detail": "edge_ok:aggregate_degraded"}` — an explicit non-page. `radon-monitor` is the fill / order / journal daemon, not a sidecar: with it `failed` and api/relay/nextjs up, the aggregate is `degraded` indefinitely and the only backstop is the `fill-monitor` heartbeat (`services.py:91`), which is `requires_ib: True` with `closed: 3 * _DAY`, so a Friday-evening death is unpaged until Monday. Before this delta that same state paged `aggregate_down` P1. |
| R-383 | P0 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:367-381`, `:412-422` | **The new taint flag withholds CORRECT realized P&L on every cross-writer dual-written contract, falling back to IB's known-wrong figure.** `basis_tainted` is set on ANY fingerprint suppression of a same-direction fill, but the fingerprint is `(contract, date, signed_qty)` — it cannot see price, so it cannot distinguish the false positive the comment worries about (two distinct equal-size partials at different prices) from the TRUE duplicate the suppression exists for (REL-024 / R-049: one fill written by the realtime daemon under a dotted API execId and again by Flex rehydrate under a numeric `tradeID`, identical price and quantity). In the true-duplicate case the post-suppression basis is exactly right, yet every later figure for that contract is discarded. Executed in the walk: a seed that yields `{'c1': 3000.0}` clean yields `{}` once the by-design duplicate row is present, and `c1` falls back to IB's `realizedPNL` — the drifted avgCost number this module exists to replace (module docstring: SLV 2026-08-24, IB `+$18,511` vs true `+$30,069`, an **$11,558** understatement). Dual-write is the NORMAL production state for any contract Flex has rehydrated. No existing test covers the same-price duplicate: `test_a_suppressed_opening_partial_withholds_every_later_figure:733` deliberately uses $1.00 vs $2.00. |
| R-384 | P0 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:183`, `:202`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:174`, `:193` | **A TERM-killed weekend cycle dies with no dead-man comment and no Pushover.** The only traps armed are `ERR` (`on_crash`) and `EXIT` (lock release); nothing is armed on `INT`/`TERM`/`HUP` in either twin — verified by `grep -n '^\s*trap ' ` over both files, which returns exactly five lines each, all `ERR` or `EXIT`. Bash does run the `EXIT` trap on an untrapped SIGTERM, so the lock releases and the process exits 143 — but `on_crash` is never invoked, so no issue comment and no page are emitted. launchd's `ExitTimeOut` kill, `launchctl bootout`, an operator `kill`, or a reboot mid-cycle therefore produce exactly the same observable as "the runner did not fire", and `SKILL.md` §Dead-man explicitly instructs the operator to read quiet as "still running" — so the operator waits instead of investigating. |
| R-385 | P0 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:321`, `:327`, `:347-349` | **The continuation-round re-ground runs with the ERR trap disarmed and `set -e` on, so any git failure ends the run silently, mid-backlog.** `trap - ERR` at `:321` stays in force across the whole round loop, `set -e` is restored at `:327`, and the re-ground at `:347-349` (`git checkout -f`, `git reset --hard`, `git clean -fdq`) is bare — unlike round 1's `ground_truth`, which is wrapped at `:310-315`. Concrete: the cap SIGTERMs `claude` mid-`git commit`, leaving `.git/index.lock`; round 2's `git checkout -f main` fails; errexit terminates `main` with no ERR trap, so no `report` runs, `run_phase`'s reporting block at `:354-378` is never reached, and in `cycle` mode the whole daily run ends with no comment and no page while the backlog is unfinished. |
| R-386 | P0 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:279`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:292` | **The wall-clock cap is unenforceable, and a wedged round holds the runner lock forever.** `timeout "$remain" claude ...` passes neither `--kill-after` nor `--foreground`, so only SIGTERM is sent and only to `claude`. A `claude` blocked on a hung child (a stalled `git push`, an unreaped subagent) never exits, `timeout` waits indefinitely, and `remain` / `CAP_SECS` / `CYCLE_DEADLINE` become advisory — the 20h bound at `:228` cannot save a bound nothing enforces. The run then holds `.weekend-runner.lock` indefinitely; launchd will not start a second instance of a running label, so EVERY subsequent 00:00 fire is dropped and the dead-man goes quiet permanently. Orphaned grandchildren also keep writing into the clone while `:349` runs `git clean -fdq`. |
| R-387 | P1 | `scripts/leap_scanner_uw.py:443-450`, filters at `:422-425` | `pick_best_mispriced_leap` is `max(leaps, key=lambda leap: (hv_20 - leap.iv, leap.oi))`, and `hv_20` is constant across the group — so this is purely argmin(IV), with `oi` breaking only an exact tie on an IV rounded to 2dp, which essentially never happens. The docstring's "ties go to the deeper open interest — better fill" is inoperative. `get_leap_options` rejects only `iv == 0`, so `oi=0` / `volume=0` contracts survive into the pool, and the lowest quoted IV in a delta bucket is systematically the stalest or least-traded quote. That contract is then serialised as `best_leap` and deep-linked as the headline TRADE BEST order. |
| R-388 | P1 | `scripts/leap_scanner_uw.py:541-545`, `:797-810`; `web/components/LeapScanner.tsx:60-65`, `:103-104`, `:212-222`; `web/lib/types.ts:698-708` | `best_leap` is assigned under `if gap_20 > best_gap or best_leap is None`, so a ticker mispriced only via `gap_60` gets a `best_leap` while `best_gap` stays at its `0` initialiser — rendering a `MISPRICED` row whose clickable anchor text is `signed(r.best_gap)` = `+0.0` while still arming a live contract. `widestMispriced` then ranks the fleet-wide TRADE BEST button by that same `best_gap`, so a ticker whose linked contract's real gap is 18 vol points loses the headline slot to one whose contract gap is 6. The honest per-contract figure already exists in the payload (`best_leap_payload` at `:806`, typed as `LeapBestContract.gap`) and no component reads it. |
| R-389 | P1 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:253-255`, `:266`, `:79`, `:115`, `:171`, `:234-239`; `scripts/watchdog/services.py:105` | **A stale IBKR `outgoing` directory reports healthy forever, and the missed-delivery gate is structurally unreachable.** `_sftp` batches `cd`/`ls` and `get` but issues no `rm` or `rename`, so delivered files are never removed remotely; the inbox is only trimmed locally by `retain_newest_gpg`. Once IBKR delivers once, `names` is never empty again, so the only missed-delivery detector (`:234-239`) can never fire. If IBKR stops delivering, every run re-pulls the same statement, each file returns `outcome: "duplicate"` which passes `result.get("ok", True)`, `ingested` increments, and `_heartbeat("ok")` fires. The `flex-pull` 26h window added this delta sees a fresh `ok` row twice daily and never pages while cash flows and TWR silently freeze. |
| R-390 | P1 | `scripts/cash_flow_sync.py:534-568` (`_cash_transfer_row`) vs `:465-495`; `:500-529` (`_disambiguated_id`); `:457-462` | **A cash movement present in both `<CashTransaction>` and `<Transfer>` is double-booked even when the `transactionID` is identical.** The Transfer row's `raw_type` is `f"Transfer:{xfer_type}:{direction}"` while the CashTransaction row's is the bare IBKR `type`; `_disambiguated_id` fingerprints on `raw_type\|amount\|date\|description`, so the two hash to DIFFERENT ids and both are appended and upserted. Worse, the `counts` pre-pass now mixes both sections, so the shared id is marked `duplicated` and the divergent-suffix path is guaranteed rather than one row being dropped. `CLAUDE.md` documents the NAV query (`1442520`) as carrying Cash Transactions AND Transfers in one document, so this is the expected shape, not an edge case: external capital flow is doubled and the TWR denominator is wrong. |
| R-391 | P1 | `cloud/services/radon-relay.service.d/runtime-container.conf:5-6`; `cloud/services/radon-monitor.service.d/runtime-container.conf:5-6`; base `cloud/services/radon-relay.service:18-20`, `cloud/services/radon-monitor.service:22-23`; `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:194-197` | **The containerisation drop-ins silently delete the liveness watchdog on the only two units that have one, reverting REL-008 and DUR-17.** Base `radon-relay.service` is `Type=notify` + `WatchdogSec=45`; base `radon-monitor.service` is `Type=notify` + `WatchdogSec=900` — the latter installed BY REL-008 specifically to catch the alive-but-dead loop wedge. Both drop-ins override to `Type=simple` + `WatchdogSec=infinity`, so systemd stops waiting for `READY=1` and stops requiring keepalives. A relay that stops delivering ticks, or a monitor daemon wedged on an IB socket, previously got SIGABRT and a restart; after the cutover it sits `active (running)` indefinitely with a dead socket. This also makes the `NOTIFY_SOCKET` / `WATCHDOG_USEC` plumbing at `:194-197` dead code, since `WATCHDOG_USEC` is never set under `WatchdogSec=infinity`. |
| R-392 | P1 | `cloud/scripts/drift_audit.py:506-512` (`_live_unit_counter`), `:541-546`; `cloud/config/drift-allowlist.conf` (2 entries, neither for a drop-in) | **The daily drift auditor will go permanently red on all five app units the moment the container drop-ins are installed.** `_check_units` compares `_live_unit_counter(live_path)` — which merges the installed unit WITH its `<unit>.d/*.conf` drop-ins — against `merge_unit_counters([_read_repo(repo_path)])`, the repo BASE unit only, never merged with `cloud/services/<unit>.service.d/*.conf`. So `User=root`, `Type=simple`, `WatchdogSec=infinity`, `NotifyAccess=all`, `Environment=RADON_RUNTIME=container`, the `ExecStartPre=` reset and both `ExecStart=` lines all read as live-only settings, producing `unit-mismatch:radon-api.service` and four siblings on every run. Verified: the allowlist contains exactly two entries, both `not-installed:radon-llm-index.*`, so nothing acknowledges them and the R-058 expiry ratchet cannot apply. A permanently-red `config-drift` buries every real drift. (The audit-walk filed the opposite reading — that drift is invisible to the auditor between deploys; the lead read `_live_unit_counter` and rejected that half. `stale-dropin` correctly will not fire, because a repo counterpart does exist.) |
| R-393 | P1 | `cloud/tests/test_root_execution_paths.py:53-57` (`_unit_texts`) | **The root-execution guard cannot see the only artifacts that set `User=root`.** `_unit_texts()` iterates `SERVICES_DIR.iterdir()` non-recursively and filters `path.suffix == ".service"`, so every `*.service.d/*.conf` is outside the scan. The drop-ins are precisely what flips five `User=radon` units to `User=root`, and they reset `ExecStartPre=` and reinstate `ExecStart=` from scratch. Today's bodies are clean (`/usr/local/sbin/radon-app-runtime`), but the class-level guard that exists to reject a `User=root` ExecStart under `/home/radon` — the guard that redirected REL-123's remedy last week — will not fire if a future edit puts `ExecStartPre=/home/radon/radon/.venv/bin/python ...` back into a drop-in, which is exactly what the `.conf.example` comments warn about. |
| R-394 | P1 | `cloud/scripts/bootstrap-control-plane.sh:332-341`; `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:1123-1139` (`refresh_install_file`), `:160` | **The drop-in content gate exists only on the path that almost never runs.** `bootstrap-control-plane.sh` validates every drop-in (`Type=simple`, ExecStart is `radon-app-runtime`, `ExecStartPre=` reset, no Gateway mention) — and it is also the only path that never adds them to `SYSTEMD_CANDIDATES`, so they skip `systemd-analyze verify` too. The path that actually installs them on every deploy, `refresh_install_file`, validates only `sudoers.d/*` (visudo) and the three shell helpers (`bash -n`); its `case` has no arm for `services/*`, so a drop-in body is copied into `/etc/systemd/system` and `daemon-reload`ed with zero checks. Since `CLOUD_SOURCE` is the radon-writable `/home/radon/radon/cloud`, a malformed or edited drop-in reaches the root-run unit definition of all five app services without passing the gate this delta added. |
| R-395 | P1 | `scripts/data_refresh.py:81-94` (`_heartbeat_soft_fail`); `scripts/watchdog/check.py:280-293`; `scripts/incident_watchdog/classify.py:71-78` | **The new soft-fail heartbeat disarms BOTH watchdog channels for cri-scan and vcg-scan.** The row is written with `next_attempt_at = now + 900s`, but `next_attempt_at` is not "the next cadence fire" anywhere else in this repo — it is a writer's own circuit-breaker embargo, and two consumers read it as documented-normal quiet. `check.py:280-293` suppresses the fired alert once `consecutive_failures > HYSTERESIS_THRESHOLD` (=2) while `embargo_until > now`, and `classify.py`'s `_is_expected_quiet` drops the row from `service-health-degraded` with NO hysteresis at all. Because a fresh 15-minute embargo is rewritten every cycle, it is always in the future at every evaluation: the error fires exactly once (at `consecutive_failures == 2`) and is suppressed forever after, while the incident watchdog never reports it at all. The row simultaneously refreshes `updated_at`, so `_check_stale` reports cri-scan `fresh` against its 35m window. IB auth wedges at 09:40, cri_scan times out on all 26 remaining fires of the session, the operator gets ONE page at ~10:10, and `data-refresh` itself still heartbeats `ok` via `service_cycle` at `:185`. |
| R-396 | P1 | `scripts/bpi_scan.py:680-690`, `:691-692`, `:693`, `:722-735` | When the reserve check skips `_persist_index`, the payload is still put into `results`, `_write_disk_mirror` stamps `data/bpi.json` with a brand-new `generated_at`, and the `service_cycle` context exits cleanly with `cycle.finished_at = generated_at` — so `service_health[bpi]` is `ok`. Nothing distinguishes a run that wrote all three indices to Turso from one that wrote none. A tarpitted Yahoo sweep burns the budget on NDX, SPX and RUT skip their writes, `bpi_history` has no rows for the session; under the repo's Turso-first read rule the BPI chart shows a gap while the health row is green and the JSON mirror claims fresh data. |
| R-397 | P1 | `scripts/health_service/probes.py:83-93` (`unit_coarse_state`), `:255-260` | **`activating (auto-restart)` is the systemd signature of a crash loop, and this delta maps it to `degraded`.** `f7b5eeb9`'s message says the sidecar "spends the flap in activating", which is true — but a unit that NEVER starts successfully also spends 100% of its life in `activating`, with `SubState=auto-restart`. `unit_coarse_state(active_state, sub_state)` takes `sub_state` as a parameter and the body never references it (verified by reading the whole function), so `("activating", "auto-restart")` and `("activating", "start-pre")` are indistinguishable and both return `starting`, which the new branch converts to `degraded`. Ship a bad `radon-newsfeed` config: the unit restarts every 5s forever, never reaching `active`, `/status` reports `degraded` on every sample, off-box writes `ok=1`, and the scraper never produces one article with nothing at the edge saying so. |
| R-398 | P1 | `scripts/health_service/probes.py:246` (dependency-downish branch) vs `:255` (serving-`starting` branch) | **The dependency suppression is ordered ahead of the serving-path `starting` check, so a sidecar failure swallows a serving-path signal.** A serving-path unit in `reloading`/`activating` is reported as `starting`, which `probe.py:456-468` treats as neither degraded nor healthy, hence `down`, hence a page. But if ANY dependency unit is simultaneously downish, the earlier branch wins and the aggregate becomes `degraded` and `ok=1`. Concrete: `systemctl reload radon-nextjs` wedges in `ExecReload` for the full `TimeoutSec` (the old process still holds :3000, so `probe_tcp` reports `up` and no probe contradicts it) WHILE `radon-newsfeed.service` is `failed` — silent. Remove the newsfeed failure from the same scenario and it pages `aggregate_down`. A second unrelated failure made the first one invisible. |
| R-399 | P1 | `scripts/watchdog/external_probe.py:76-96` (`_local_aggregate_clears_offbox_down`), `:47-60` (`_read_local_aggregate`), called at `:183` | **`degraded` was made sufficient to clear a validated off-box `aggregate_down`, and `degraded` is no longer evidence of a brief flap.** The docstring justifies accepting `degraded` with "sidecar/broker only; ping already proved the edge is serving" — true of the stale row being cleared, not of current host state. `_read_local_aggregate` never inspects `generated_at`, so there is no freshness assertion on the payload used as recovery evidence. Sequence: T0 the off-box observer samples while radon-api genuinely reports `down` and writes `ok=0, reason=aggregate_down`; the GitHub schedule slips 45 min; T0+60s systemd has restarted radon-api but `radon-monitor` is dead, local `/status` is `degraded`, the clearer returns True, and `check_external_probe` returns `fired=False, "off-box aggregate recovered locally"`. The api outage is never paged AND the live monitor outage is laundered as recovered. |
| R-400 | P1 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:227-231`, `:239`, `:256`, `:261`; catalogued this delta at `scripts/watchdog/services.py:103-105`, `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:150` | **NF-9, in the job this same delta catalogued: `flex-pull` writes a health row on the happy path only.** `run()` has no outer `try`/`finally`. The first `except` at `:230` is a narrow prefix wrapping only `validate_ssh_config` + `list_remote_gpg` and catching only `FlexSftpError`, so an `OSError`/`TimeoutExpired` from the sftp runner escapes. The per-file handler at `:256` catches `(FlexSftpError, FlexClassifyError, OSError)`, covering neither `TimeoutExpired` from `_gpg_decrypt` nor arbitrary exceptions out of `ingest_xml` (Turso write failure, `ET.ParseError`, `KeyError`). `_ensure_inbox` and `retain_newest_gpg` sit outside every handler. Turso unreachable during `ingest_xml` therefore exits the unit non-zero with NO row written, the previous `ok` row stays newest, and the 26h/4d windows added this delta keep `flex-pull` green until Tuesday. |
| R-401 | P1 | `scripts/health_service/serve.py:92-128` (`ProbeCache`) vs `:174-181` (`UnitStateCache.snapshot`); consumed at `scripts/health_service/probes.py:216-221`, `:145-151` | **Probe evidence has no staleness gate, unlike unit evidence — the asymmetry that decides whether the new dependency partitioning is trustworthy.** `aggregate_state` refuses to trust unit states older than `UNIT_STATE_MAX_AGE_SECS = 30` via `_unit_evidence_current`, but folds `probe_results` in unconditionally with no age at all: `ProbeCache` has no `_updated` field and `snapshot()` returns a bare dict, where `UnitStateCache.snapshot()` returns `(value, age)`. `refresh_once` swallows every exception and keeps the last value, so if the `health-probe-cache` daemon thread dies the last-good probe dict is served as current indefinitely, and `/status` will report `radon-api: up` from an hours-old snapshot the moment the unit reads `active` again. |
| R-402 | P1 | `cloud/services/radon-forecast-nightly.timer:5-6`, `cloud/services/radon-forecast-nightly.service:12`, `scripts/nightly_forecast.py`; `cloud/services/radon-signals-refresh.timer`, `scripts/run_signals_refresh.sh`; `scripts/watchdog/services.py`, `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts` | **The two genuine catalog gaps the standing sweep found, both timer-backed and both in NEITHER catalog.** Enumerated in the lead context over all 55 `cloud/services/*.timer` units: `forecast-nightly` and `signals-refresh` are absent from `SCHEDULED_SERVICES` and from `serviceHealthWindows.ts` (grep for each name returns only prose comments referencing the timer, never a key). `scripts/nightly_forecast.py` contains zero `service_health` references on any path, and `radon-signals-refresh.timer` is an HOURLY `Mon..Fri 09..16:05 ET` job. Additionally, ZERO units in `cloud/services/*.service` carry an `OnFailure=` directive, so there is no systemd-level backstop either: the nightly Chronos-2 backfill throws, the unit goes `failed`, nothing writes a row, the watchdog has no key to age-check, `/admin` shows nothing, and the forecast tables silently stop advancing. |
| R-403 | P1 | `web/lib/regimeLiveStrip.ts:105` (`MAX_PREVIOUS_CLOSE_GAP_DAYS = 7`), enforced at `:140`; `web/components/RegimeStrip.tsx:24-35` | The new previous-close anchor is required only to fall WITHIN 7 calendar days of `sessionDate`, not to BE `sessionDate`. The comment justifies it as covering a holiday-extended weekend, but the worst legitimate holiday gap is 3 days (Labor Day 2026-09-07: `lastCompletedSessionDate` = `2026-09-07`, newest row `2026-09-04`). Failure: `radon-cri.timer` last succeeded Mon 2026-08-24; on Fri 2026-08-28 14:00 ET `sessionDate` is `2026-08-27` and the newest history row is `2026-08-24` — gap 3, accepted. The strip then renders live VIX against MONDAY's close as a coloured, arrowed "day change" percentage: a 4-session move labelled as today's, with no stale marker. |
| R-404 | P1 | `web/lib/regimeLiveStrip.ts:162-165`, `:124-125`; `web/app/api/regime/route.ts:101-103`; `web/components/RegimePanel.tsx:207` | The degraded path still falls back to `prices.VIX.close` — the tick-9 relay value the same commit's docblock says "can be sessions behind" — because the guard is `data?.history?.length` and empty history IS the degraded case: the regime route sets `history: []` whenever `raw.history` is not an array (upstream failure / `missing: true` / EMPTY_CRI). On a weekend with the CRI route serving a `missing` payload, `market_open` is absent so `RegimePanel` defaults `marketOpen = true`, `liveVix` is the relay's last cached Friday tick and `vixClose` is a two-session-old tick-9 close — and the strip draws a signed, coloured day change with no stale marker. The pre-fix bug ships unchanged in the one state where the data is least trustworthy. |
| R-405 | P1 | `vitest.config.ts:60`; `web/lib/holdTime.ts:15`, `:20-24`, `:40`; no `TZ` in `cloud/services/*.service`, `docker/`, or `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | **The suite now pins a timezone production does not have.** `TZ: "America/New_York"` was added to the vitest env; nothing pins `TZ` for the runtime, so the Next.js server and every `radon-*` unit run UTC. Code that reads LOCAL date fields off a parsed timestamp is therefore green under ET and different in production: `holdTime.ts:15` (`new Date(input)`) feeding `isSameLocalDay` and `isEarlierLocalDay`'s `dayNumber`. An exit stamped `2026-08-29T01:00:00Z` is Aug 28 in the pinned suite and Aug 29 on the UTC server, so same-day-trade classification and the entry-before-exit rejection flip between test and production. The pin makes TZ-independence untestable rather than guaranteed. Distinct from R-202/R-240: globs, timeouts and projects are unchanged, the diff touches only `env`. |
| R-406 | P1 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:356-357`, `:412-413`, `:415` | `basis_tainted` and `position_may_be_short` are function-scoped and set-once. Line `:412-413` already recognises that `position_qty == 0` resets the basis (`avg_basis_per_unit = 0.0`), but the taint flags are not cleared there, so any position opened AFTER the contract goes flat is replayed against a basis with no dependency whatsoever on the pre-flat suppression — and is still withheld. Executed: cycle 1 (`BUY 10 @ $1` dual-written, `SELL 10 @ $2` to flat) plus cycle 2 (`BUY 10 @ $4`, `SELL 10 @ $9`, true realized $5,000) returns `{}` where the clean journal returns `{'c1': 1000.0, 'c2': 5000.0}`. A LEAP or a repeatedly-traded weekly re-entered even once after a single dual-written open loses its journal figure for the rest of the journal's history. |
| R-407 | P1 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:311-315` (`_is_plausible_contract`) | **The plausibility domain REL-109 shipped checks that the expiry is a CALENDAR-valid date, not that it precedes the row's own fill date — so a field-swap corruption still fabricates P&L with no warning.** The `strike > 0` and `right in {C,P}` halves hold (verified by execution: `strike=0`, `strike='0'`, `strike=-60.0`, `right='X'`, `right=''`, `expiry='20261340'`, `expiry='20260230'` all now return `{}` with one WARNING each, against last week's silent `{'c1': 4000.0}`). But `expiry='19700101'` and a swap that writes the trade date into `expiry` both pass, mint a well-formed key, form a phantom bucket that takes its cost out of the real one, and publish `$4,000` against a true `$3,000` — a 33% overstatement, zero warnings. The backlog brief for REL-109 specified "expiry parsing to a real date inside the contract's lifetime"; the lifetime half was not implemented, and the row's own `date` field (already in the payload, already used by `contract_fill_fingerprint`) makes it decidable. |
| R-408 | P1 | `scripts/clients/journal_realized.py:187-192` (`apply_journal_realized_pnl`); `web/lib/types.ts:286-288` | `apply_journal_realized_pnl` faithfully stamps `realizedPNLSource: "journal" \| "ib"` and preserves `ibRealizedPNL`, but grepping `web/components` and `web/app` for `realizedPNLSource` returns ZERO hits — the field is declared in `types.ts`, asserted in five test files, and read by nothing that renders. Every withholding path in this module therefore degrades to IB's drifted number on `/orders` with no visual marker, the only trace being a WARNING in a systemd journal nobody watches during a session. That is what turns a conservative fallback into a silent wrong number: for the SLV shape the module documents, an $11,558 discrepancy rendered as if it were the journal-derived truth. |
| R-409 | P1 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:57`, `:98-100`, `:125-134`, `:49-53`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:238`, `:89-91`, `:116-125` | **Every network call in the wrapper is unbounded, including the dead-man channel itself.** `git fetch origin --quiet`, `gh pr list`, and `gh issue list\|create\|comment` all run with no wall-clock bound; `fetch_origin_with_retry` bounds the ATTEMPT COUNT (3) but not the time per attempt, and the comment at `:49-53` claims the port-22 blackhole now "surfaces within minutes" — nothing makes that true. A VPN flap that establishes TCP and then stalls hangs `git fetch` with no keepalive. Worse, a hung `gh issue comment` inside `on_crash` hangs the wrapper WHILE IT IS TRYING TO REPORT ITS OWN DEATH, holding the lock and wedging the daily cycle. |
| R-410 | P1 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:188`, `:199`, `:301`, `:312`, `:19-21`, `:119`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:179`, `:190`, `:275` | **The four failure paths most likely to mean "the loop is dead" deliberately suppress Pushover.** The third `report` argument suppresses the page, which the comment at `:119` justifies for INTERIM continuation rounds — but `REFUSED` (no runner marker), `REFUSED` (lock held), `SKIPPED` (cycle budget) and `GROUND TRUTH FAILED` are terminal outcomes where the agent never ran at all. A reused-pid stale lock makes every daily fire exit 3 in under a second, forever, with the only signal a comment on a rolling issue nobody watches. The file header at `:19-21` and SKILL.md §Dead-man both promise the opposite. |
| R-411 | P1 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:31-45`, `:47`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:41-55`, `:57` | **The mkdir lock has a create-then-write race and an unconditional, non-ownership-checked release.** `mkdir "$dir"` at `:33` is atomic but the pid is written separately at `:43`. A second run landing in that window fails `mkdir`, reads an empty `pid` (`held=""`), and because the liveness test is guarded by `[[ -n "$held" ]]` it skips `kill -0` entirely, declares the lock stale, `rm -rf`s it and takes it — two cycles then run `git clean -fdq` in the same clone, the exact catastrophe the comment at `:26-30` says the lock prevents. `release_runner_lock` then `rm -rf`s the directory without checking the pid still equals `$$`, so the first run's EXIT trap unlocks the second run's tree on its way out. |
| R-412 | P1 | `scripts/tests/test_watchdog_catalog_parity.py:84-90` (`_HEALTH_CALLS`), `:93-101` (`_names_in`), `:343-375` (`EXEMPT_UNITS`), `:413-425` | **NF-8, third consecutive week: REL-114 improved the count but its exemption reasons are false for 8 of 16 entries.** Running the test module's OWN `_timer_backed_services()` / `_health_names_written_by()` in the lead context gives **16 of 55** timer-backed units with an empty name set, down from 25 of 54 — and every one is in `EXEMPT_UNITS`, so the new assertion is legitimately green. But `EXEMPT_UNITS` labels ten of them `gap: writes no service_health row`, and that is wrong for eight: `db_backup.py:53` (`SERVICE_NAME = "db-backup"`, 3 write sites), `db_retention_sweep.py:21` (`db-retention`, 4), `drift_audit.py:90` (`config-drift`, 5), `media_backup.py:42` (4), `archive_portfolio_snapshots.py:64` (3), `host_metrics_sampler.py:45`, `grok_page_responder.py` (2) and the two wrapper `.sh` targets all heartbeat on every fire, and seven of those names are in BOTH catalogs. The root cause is that `_HEALTH_CALLS` lists `mirror_scan_snapshot`, `record_service_health`, `service_cycle`, `recordServiceHealth` but NOT `write_service_health`, the bounded-stdlib writer this whole family uses — and `_names_in` reads the call's first argument, which for `write_service_health("ok", ...)` is a state literal stripped by `_STATE_LITERALS`, not a service name. `test_every_exempt_unit_states_a_reason` checks only that the reason STARTS WITH `parser:` or `gap:`, never that it is true, and `test_every_exempt_unit_still_lacks_a_resolvable_name` guarantees the parser will never dislodge the false text. Reached independently by the standing sweep and the remediation-regression walk. |
| R-413 | P2 | `web/components/ticker-detail/OptionsChainTab.tsx:1146-1156` | `nextLegs` is built straight from `chainUrl.urlLegs` with no check that `leg.strike` exists among the fetched strikes. A LEAP fractional strike (e.g. 517.5, accepted by `parseLegsParam`'s `(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)`) that the chain does not list produces a phantom leg row with `limitPrice: null` and no quote. Transmit stays disabled because `isValidPrice` requires a parsed limit, so this is not a money defect — but it renders a contract the venue does not have as if the scanner had selected it, with no error. |
| R-414 | P2 | `web/components/ticker-detail/OptionsChainTab.tsx:78-87` (`prefillLabelForSource`) | The `default` branch fires when `src` is absent, misspelled, or from a future seeder, so a hand-edited or shared `?legs=SELL:1x100P` URL is stamped PREFILLED FROM THETA HARVESTER — a scanner verdict asserted over a contract no scanner produced. |
| R-415 | P2 | `web/components/LeapScanner.tsx:120`, `:122-130` | `new Date(lastSync).toLocaleTimeString()` prints a bare clock with no date, so a three-day-old `data/leap.json` reads as today's scan. That was cosmetic before this delta; it now sits inline with the new `TRADE BEST` order-entry link, which has no staleness gate at all. Combined with R-095 (a partially-failed LEAP run still writes `leap.json` and an `ok` health row), a stale or truncated scan promotes a contract into the order builder as a fresh verdict. |
| R-416 | P2 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:34` (`FIRST_DELIVERY_DATE`), `:206-211`; `cloud/services/radon-flex-pull.timer:7-8` | The "empty outgoing is an error after 2026-08-31" cutover is a hardcoded constant with no env override, and 2026-08-31 is a **Monday** while the timer fires `Tue..Sat` — so the last run inside the `<=` grace window is Sat 2026-08-29 and the first scheduled run after it (Tue 2026-09-01) is already past the cutover. The grace buys zero scheduled runs. Any slip in IBKR onboarding pages `error` twice a day indefinitely and needs a code deploy plus a CI cycle to silence. `Persistent=true` also means a host down over the boundary replays the run under whatever the wall clock then says. |
| R-417 | P2 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:61-74`, `:38-42` (`REQUIRED_CONFIG`); `cloud/services/radon-flex-pull.service:21` | `_sftp` passes no `timeout=` to the runner and `REQUIRED_CONFIG` does not require `ConnectTimeout`/`ServerAliveInterval`, so a hung IBKR sFTP session blocks until systemd's `TimeoutStartSec=120` SIGKILLs the process — which means `_heartbeat` never runs and there is no `error` row at all, only a `failed` unit, surfaced a day later by the 26h window. |
| R-418 | P2 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:49-58` (`validate_ssh_config`) | The host-key validation is a raw substring scan over the whole file. It rejects only the literals `accept-new` and `stricthostkeychecking no`; OpenSSH also accepts `StrictHostKeyChecking off`, which is not in the reject list. The required-line check is an unscoped `in text`, so a commented-out `# StrictHostKeyChecking yes` or one scoped to an unrelated `Host` block satisfies it — and since ssh_config is first-match-wins, an `off` directive placed above the required literal wins at connect time while validation passes. The module docstring claims a pinned known_hosts but `UserKnownHostsFile` is never required. |
| R-419 | P2 | `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:192-203` (`_default_ingest`), `:252` | `_default_ingest` does `del source_path`, discarding the real inbox filename, and hands `ingest_xml` a `NamedTemporaryFile` path that `finally` unlinks. `flex_deliveries.source_path` — the only column linking a fingerprint back to a delivered file — is therefore permanently a dead `/tmp/tmpXXXX.xml` inside the unit's `PrivateTmp` namespace, so no operator can trace which statement produced which cash flows. |
| R-420 | P2 | `cloud/services/radon-api.service.d/runtime-container.conf.example:1-4` and four siblings, plus `cloud/services/radon-.service.d/runtime-container.conf.example:1-10`; `cloud/scripts/bootstrap-control-plane.sh:142-146`; `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:46-50`, `:1123-1139` | All six `.conf.example` files still read "Example only. MUST NOT be installed by setup-vps.sh or bootstrap-control-plane.sh. Default runtime remains host binaries" and describe a manual, one-unit-at-a-time, after-hours cutover — while bootstrap and the deploy helper now install the real `.conf` for all five automatically on the next push. Separately, on any host where an operator already followed those instructions and hand-copied a drop-in to that exact path, `refresh_install_file` overwrites it with no backup and no journal entry, unlike `install_manifest_units`' `UNIT_BACKUP_PREFIX` snapshot, so operator customisation disappears silently. |
| R-421 | P2 | `scripts/bpi_scan.py:660`, `:680`; `cloud/services/radon-bpi.service:34` | `persist_deadline = deadline + PERSIST_RESERVE_S` = start + 6300 + 600 = start + 6900, which is precisely `TimeoutStartSec=6900` — so `time.monotonic() >= persist_deadline` can only become true at the instant systemd already SIGTERMs the unit, making the guard unreachable on the first index. The single absolute point is also shared by the loop: if NDX consumes the whole 6300s budget, SPX and RUT fetch instantly and both evaluate the guard at t~6301, pass it, and start ~600s persists each into a 599s remaining window, so two of three are still SIGTERMed mid-`upsert_bpi_history_rows`. The reserve is neither divided per index nor decremented by work already done, so R-225's half-written `bpi_history` is still reachable. |
| R-422 | P2 | `scripts/data_refresh.py:45-49` (`_SCRIPT_SERVICES`); `scripts/watchdog/services.py:54`, `:564`; `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:281` | `_SCRIPT_SERVICES` maps `gex_scan.py -> "gex-scan"`, but `gex-scan` is absent from `SCHEDULED_SERVICES` (verified programmatically: the set difference of `_SCRIPT_SERVICES.values()` against `SCHEDULED_SERVICES` is exactly `{'gex-scan'}`), and the error bucket is derived as `SCHEDULED_SERVICES.keys()` minus `watchdog-alerts` — so gex-scan is in neither a stale bucket nor the error bucket and `check.py` never evaluates it. Its only registration is `serviceHealthWindows.ts:281`, still `category: "on-demand"` even though this delta puts it on the 15-minute RTH timer. A gex_scan that fails every cycle writes an error row that pages nobody. |
| R-423 | P2 | `scripts/data_refresh.py:190-193` vs `scripts/api/server.py:3281`; `scripts/cri_scan.py` (no `flock`, no in-flight marker) | The timer child budget was raised to 180s because "cri regularly needs 60-103s on a live IB path", but `/regime/scan` still spawns the same script at `timeout=120` — so the slow-IB runs the raise was made to accommodate are exactly the ones the browser path SIGKILLs at 120s, which then arms `SCAN_GATES["regime"]` for 60s and 502s the panel. Separately, `cri_scan.py` holds no lock, so a browser POST landing during a timer fire runs a second cri_scan concurrently for up to 180s, both racing the same `data/cri.json` write and the same IB client-id range. |
| R-424 | P2 | `scripts/api/server.py:3202-3207`, `:3180-3194`, `:4062` | The 256-gate overflow path is correctly fail-closed (`mark_failure()` immediately precedes `_admit()`, so the shared gate can never reach `mark_success()`, and `_gex_cache_for_ticker` filters by ticker so no wrong-ticker payload is served) — but when the map saturates the only externally visible artifact is a 429 whose detail reads `scan-overflow scan backing off after a failure`: no log line, no `service_health` row, no counter, nothing naming saturation. `_evict_idle_scan_gate` cannot evict while >256 subjects are polled inside the 120s `SCAN_COOLDOWN_S`, so the condition is sticky. A dashboard widening its ticker list past 256 silently 429s every novel ticker indefinitely. |

At 1280x720 with `/api/options/chain` returning an error, the bottom of the multi-line failure block is unreachable by any scroll gesture. |
| R-426 | P2 | `scripts/reliability_weekend.sh:157`, `:245`, `:281`; `scripts/testing_weekend.sh:148`, `:294` | `phase_status`'s TRUNCATED detection greps the whole cumulative phase log (`grep -qF "$BG_CEILING_MARKER" "$run_log"`), and `RUN_LOG` is per PHASE, not per round, appended by every retry and all continuation rounds. So a background-ceiling message emitted in round 1 makes a genuinely complete round 8 report `TRUNCATED (background work killed...)` — the new code inverts into a permanent false alarm on exactly the long remediations it was added to protect, telling the operator a finished PR "must not be read as a finished run". |

The FastAPI `/orders/modify` route DOES bound it (`check_modify_limits` at `:2986-2995`, REL-005 / R-145), so the web path is covered and this is filed P2 rather than P1 — but the script is directly invocable on the host and the guard lives in the caller rather than at the placement funnel, which is the inversion `ib_place_order.py` was restructured to avoid. |

### Audited, clean

- `scripts/api/scan_gate.py` — the added `reset()` (`:70-72`) is a correct total disarm used only by
  `_reset_scan_gates`; no new state, no interaction with the cooldown/backoff ordering.
- `scripts/incident_watchdog/__main__.py` — `import sys` at `:18` precedes the `sys.path.insert` at
  `:27-29`, and `.store`/`.probes` are imported at `:31-33` after it, so the `db.service_cycle`
  resolution is genuinely fixed for the `python -m` entrypoint. No new suppression.
- `web/components/FreshnessRail.tsx` — a `data-fill-pct` test anchor only; every figure still
  derives from `computeFreshnessRail(schedule, ...)` off the pinned timer constants, and no cadence
  string is hardcoded.
- `cloud/config/installed-units.sha256` / `auto-sync-units.txt` / `drift-allowlist.conf` — verified
  by hashing: both new `radon-flex-pull` hashes match, both units are added and sorted, and NO
  suppression entry was added for them, so drift on them is live.
- `flex-pull` watchdog windows — `services.py:103-105` and `serviceHealthWindows.ts:150` match
  `radon-flex-pull.timer` (`Tue..Sat 07:30` + `08:30 ET`): the widest real gap is Sat 08:30 to Tue
  07:30 (~71h), inside the 4d closed window. The defect is the emitter (R-400), not the window.
- `cloud/services/radon-refresh.service` — comment-only; `TimeoutStartSec=480` still bounds under
  the 900s period.
- `web/components/mobile/{MetricCell,MobilePositionList}.tsx`, `web/components/ShareReportModal.tsx`
  — `data-testid` / `testId` passthrough only; no behaviour, no gate.
- `web/components/GarchConvergenceScanner.tsx` — `garchOrderHref` correctly gates on
  `gates_passed && lagger`, emits no `legs`, and the displayed lagger matches the href target.
- `web/components/WorkspaceSections.tsx`, `StrengthConfirmationScanner.tsx` — the new scanner order
  links are ticker-only with no contract params, so no prefill class applies.
- `web/e2e/{regime-cta-share-pattern,mobile-same-day-pnl-parity,ivrank-tab}.spec.ts` — strictly
  tightened (class locators to testids; derived figures pinned against a frozen clock).
- `PYTHONPATH=/home/radon/radon/scripts` — resolves inside the image and collides with no stdlib or
  declared-dependency module name. It does put `scripts/` on `sys.path` for radon-api only in
  container mode, so import resolution differs host vs container; worth knowing, not a defect today.
- LICENSE / LICENSE-APACHE / LICENSE-MIT / `.env.example` / `cloud/.env.example` — licensing and
  comment-only; the env comments correctly state the sFTP path does not consume `IB_FLEX_TOKEN`.

| Task | Finding | Scope | Acceptance criteria (incl. verification) |
|---|---|---|---|

Independently, drop legs whose strike is absent from the loaded chain and surface an explicit "prefill contract unavailable" notice instead of a phantom leg row (R-413). | Fault injection, red first: mount `OptionsChainTab` with `?expiry=2027-01-15&legs=BUY:1x210C&src=leap` and an `expirations` stub containing only 2026 dates — RED asserts `orderLegs` is non-empty with `expiry === "20260904"`; GREEN asserts zero legs, no `prefillLabel`, and no surviving `legs` param in the `router.replace` call. Second case: `?legs=BUY:1x517.5C` against a chain stub lacking 517.5 asserts no leg row and a visible degraded notice. |

## Delta audit 2026-08-30

**Range:** `f7b5eeb9..HEAD` (`fda36450`) — 154 commits, 351 files. Anchor verified
(`git rev-parse --verify f7b5eeb9^{commit}` resolves to `f7b5eeb91839103b74b17ea5ae92e1e1d056f963`;
`git merge-base --is-ancestor f7b5eeb9 HEAD` confirms it is an ancestor). The range contains BOTH
weekend loops' own remediation merges (PR #139 `reliability/2026-08-29`, 21 commits; PR #140
`testing/2026-08-29`, 47 commits) plus the operator's own P1 fixes logged as REL-149/REL-150, so per
the 2026-08-22 lesson it was split by branch ancestry rather than at one commit: `git log
f7b5eeb9..HEAD ^origin/reliability/2026-08-29 ^origin/testing/2026-08-29` gives the 68 feature
commits (232 source files after dropping tests, `web/e2e`, docs, `site/` and `context/`; 130 of those
are `web/app/api/**/route.ts` touched only by the two-line `radonCapability` export from 1947cda8,
leaving ~110 substantive files). Eight subsystem walks were scoped to those, each capped at ~13
files, and a ninth EXECUTING walk reviewed the 2026-08-29 remediation (REL-131…REL-148) and the
operator's REL-149/REL-150 for regressions.

**No branch collision.** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'reliability/*'` was run BEFORE numbering and
returned only `reliability/2026-08-28` and `reliability/2026-08-29`, both merged. **Numbering starts
at R-432, not R-429:** the operator's incident fixes on 2026-08-29 consumed R-429, R-430 and R-431
in commit messages, `cloud/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/cloud-services.md` and the RELIABILITY_LOG.md rows
REL-149 / REL-150 without a findings row in this document; those three ids are treated as taken
under the frozen-contract rule and are NOT re-filed here. Backlog continues at REL-151.

**Standing sweeps** ran in the lead context before the walks reported. Sweeps 1-5 hold: the halt
chokepoints are present at `exit_order_service.py:345`, `ib_order_manage.py:183`,
`ib_execute.py:444`, `ib_place_order.py:242`, `monitor_daemon/handlers/exit_orders.py:477` and
`server.py:2722`; `_NON_IDEMPOTENT_IB_SCRIPTS` is intact at `server.py:5229` and enforced at
`:5338`/`:5416`; `check_order_limits` / `check_quantity_limit` are wired at every one of the six
`place_order` sites (`ib_execute.py:455`, `ib_order_manage.py:275-277`, `exit_order_service.py:352`,
`ib_place_order.py:255`, `clients/ib_client.py:722-724`, `handlers/exit_orders.py:689`); the
exit-orders ack-poll is present (`_ack_perm_id:191`, `_confirm_placement:197`, used at `:710`);
`upsert_daemon_state` still routes through `_hrana_execute` (`writer.py:2163`). **Sweep 6 is clean
for the first time in three weeks** — every order-placing module carries both guards (REL-145 held
by execution) — but the executing walk found the funnel's notional bound is skipped, not failed, on a
price-less order (R-467, latent: no `MarketOrder` builder exists). **Manifest sweep:** hashing every
file under `cloud/services/` against `installed-units.sha256` gives 0 mismatches and 0 stale entries;
the unpinned files are `radon-llm-index.{service,timer}` and the new `radon-mktnews.service`, all
three carrying `not-installed:` allowlist entries, plus `journald-radon.conf` and the
`radon-.service.d/*.conf.example` template, which the manifest test excludes by design. **Sweep 7**
over all 57 timer-backed units using the parity test's own iteration: 0 units with an empty
health-name set outside `EXEMPT_UNITS`, and every exempt entry verified TRUE by the executing walk
(watchdog buckets heartbeat `watchdog-alerts`, `signals-refresh` POSTs two catalogued scanners,
`portfolio-sync` is at `services.py:80`). NF-8 is CLOSED this week after three consecutive
re-openings. The two new timers (`model-catalog`, `dispersion`) are in both catalogs with matching
26h windows and service names that match the writers exactly — but both writers heartbeat `ok`
through their own failure modes (R-455, R-434), which is the NF-9 shape landing in the same delta
that catalogued them. A lead-context ad-hoc call of `_health_names_written_by` over
`cloud/services/*.service` reported six exempt-but-resolving units; the walk's run through the
test's OWN `_timer_backed_services` iteration reported zero, and reading
`test_every_exempt_unit_still_lacks_a_resolvable_name` confirms the test already rejects a resolving
exemption. The 2026-08-28 lesson applied again: call the test's functions with the test's inputs.

**Regression review of REL-131…REL-150.** Verified by EXECUTION (targeted test files in isolation
plus scratch cases the shipped tests do not cover). Six hold, three are PARTIAL:

- **REL-131 (R-378, R-413) HOLDS.** `3 passed`; the mismatch branch burns `appliedLegsParamRef`
  (`OptionsChainTab.tsx:1159`), `syncUrl` drops `legs`+`src` on the expiry rewrite
  (`useChainUrlState.ts:159-162`), and a second navigation to the same URL cannot re-arm because the
  burned signature equals `legsParamRaw`.
- **REL-133 (R-380, R-393, R-394) HOLDS after the operator's rewrite.** `18 passed`; in `ci.yml` the
  RECOVER_ONLY pass (`:984`) and `sync-control-plane.sh` (`:990-992`) both run BEFORE `deploy.sh`
  (`:993`) and therefore before `stop_services_for_transition` (`deploy.sh:1296`); bootstrap only
  `daemon-reload`s; the post-stop `if ! refresh_control_plane; then start_services_after_transition`
  guard is intact (`:1308-1312`).
- **REL-134 (R-381) HOLDS after 3189d15a/3846ddf3.** `4 passed`; `cmd_run` binds only `DATA_DIR`,
  `MEDIA_DIR`, `LEASE_DIR` (`:358-360`); the notify mount is a single socket file (`:365`); no
  `/var/lib/radon` parent bind.
- **REL-135 (R-382) HOLDS for the shipped claim** (`899s -> degraded`, `901s -> down`, `10 passed`)
  and 857bbad0 does not bypass it — but the dwell is in-process memory covering units only (R-468).
- **REL-141 (R-402, R-412) HOLDS.** `25 passed`; 0 exempt-but-resolving by the test's own iteration.
- **REL-145 (R-427, R-428) HOLDS for the shipped claims.** `11 passed`; over-cap qty and
  over-notional LMT refused at `IBClient.place_order` naming the env limit; the tripwire enumerates
  all six modules. The price-less gap is R-467.
- **REL-132 (R-379) is PARTIAL — R-436.** Release succeeds -> holds (`release calls: 1`); release
  fails on the same Turso outage -> next run is `True duplicate` and `flex_sftp_pull` passes `ok=True`.
- **REL-150 (R-431) is PARTIAL — R-432.** The prune's "in use" is `docker ps`; with one previous
  unit down the previous pair is pruned and rollback re-pulls mid-gate.
- **REL-149 (R-429, R-430):** `40 passed` / `20 passed`; the proxy survives the `exec docker`
  handoff and a proxy crash fails loud via `WatchdogSec`. The socket is `0666` (R-439).

**Severity decisions in the lead.** Three walk ratings were RAISED: R-433 (Clerk guard dead on the
production path, walk P2 -> P1, because a "refuses to boot" auth fix verified only by string-presence
tests is the 2026-08-27 test-scope lesson recurring on an auth boundary); R-434 (DISPERSION Yahoo as
silent scheduled primary, walk P2 -> P1, because the defect is a scheduled violation of CLAUDE.md
rule 7 with no signal, not a data-quality question); R-435 is filed P1 WITH CONTINGENCY (the
`radon-mktnews` unit is `not-installed:` today; `setup-vps.sh:99` enables it on a fresh host). One
walk rating was LOWERED: R-436 (REL-132 partial) from the original R-379's P0 to P1 because the
outcome now requires the same Turso outage to fail both the write and the release.

**Cross-walk convergence.** Four findings were reached independently by two walks each and merged
before numbering: R-432 (image prune — control-plane walk via the concurrent-prepull window, executing
walk via the stopped-unit case), R-439 (notify socket mode — control-plane and regression walks),
R-455 (model-catalog `ok`-on-failure — watchdog and model-catalog walks), and R-460 (hub client cap —
the hub's missing reaper from walk 7a and the hook's leaked socket from walk 7b, filed as ONE
finding because fixing either side alone leaves the cap reachable). Walk 3's NF-10 answers for the
two new deploy-window suppressions (7a45f294, 857bbad0) were verified in the lead: both are bounded
(journal < 3600s, marker window `[-900, +60]`), ordered after the `aggregate_down` local-clear, and
still require the local serving path to be green — filed as clean, not as findings.

**The week's theme is the operator's own hot-fix path.** Four of the five P1s and eight of the P2s are
in code the operator wrote under incident pressure on 2026-08-29 (REL-149/REL-150, 020865ad,
642299c2) or in the 24h-old DISPERSION and headlines features. Every one of them ships with a test
that is green, and in three cases (R-432, R-433, R-436) the test pins the happy path the incident
needed while the finding lives in the branch the incident did not exercise.

### Findings

| ID | Sev | Where | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-432 | P1 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:186`, `:192-194`; `cloud/config/sudoers.d/radon-deploy:1`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:780-804`, `:816`, `:853-855`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:1227`, `:1232`, `:1155-1158`; `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:129-133`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:1604` | **REL-150 (R-431) is PARTIAL: the image prune it added takes no lock and defines "in use" as `docker ps`, so (a) a prepull for the NEXT push that lands while the app tier is stopped deletes both the release being activated and its rollback pair, and (b) even with no concurrent push, a previous release with ONE unit down at prepull time loses that image and "a rollback never pulls" is false.** Reached independently by the control-plane walk (a) and the executing regression walk (b), which ran `prune_stale_app_images` against a fake docker: previous fully running -> no `rmi`; previous with relay+nextjs+newsfeed down (the 2026-08-29 start-limit-hit shape REL-149 describes) -> `rmi ghcr.io/joemccann/radon-node:<PREV>`; `docker ps` empty -> both previous images removed. The comment at `:132-133` promises the previous release survives until the deploy after next; the rollback path at `deploy.sh:1604` calls `recover_pending_transition` with no prepull, so `run` for the previous SHA re-pulls 4.8G inside the rollback gate or exits 69 if GHCR is unreachable — the exact mid-pull rollback failure REL-150 was written to end. The shipped prune test stages the previous pair as running. `prune_stale_app_images` keeps only `$target` plus whatever `docker ps --format '{{.Image}}'` lists (`:186`, `:192-193`) and `rmi`s every other 40-hex pair (`:194`); `cmd_pull` holds no lock and sudoers grants `pull [0-9a-f]*` unconditionally. The `prepull-images` job (`ci.yml:780-804`) runs under its own concurrency group `ci-prepull-images-<ref>` (verified in the lead: it is not `deploy-production`, and it does not `needs: deploy`), so it SSHes `sudo radon-app-runtime pull "$SHA2"` the moment SHA2's gates are green — while SHA1's deploy may be inside `stop-clean` -> `restart-managed` (`deploy.sh:1227` -> `:1232`) or the rollback window (`:1155-1158`) with all five `--rm` containers gone. `docker ps` is then empty, docker no longer refuses the `rmi`, and SHA1's pair AND the previous pair are removed; `run` falls through to the registry probe and re-downloads 4.8G after teardown, which is exactly the R-431 gate-fails-mid-pull outage, now for the rollback as well. Triggered by ordinary stacked pushes; the walk's own `Rejected` list confirms the prestage prune at `deploy.sh:696` is safe because it runs under the deploy lock with the tier up. Fix: compute the protected set from the transition journal's previous SHA (or the green marker) rather than `docker ps`, take `/home/radon/.radon-deploy.lock` non-blocking around the prune (skip when held), and never prune when `docker ps` lists no `radon-*` container. |
| R-433 | P1 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:407-408`; `docker/app/Dockerfile.node:96`; `docker/app/next-clerk-guard.sh:9-33`; `cloud/tests/test_app_images.py:169`; `cloud/tests/test_app_plane_cutover_safety.py:220` | **The "refuse to boot a Next image baked without Clerk publishableKey" guard (642299c2) never executes in production.** The Dockerfile sets `CMD ["/usr/local/bin/next-clerk-guard"]` (`Dockerfile.node:96`, no `ENTRYPOINT`), but the production path is `ExecStart=radon-app-runtime run %n`, and `cmd_run` appends `bun run start` as the container command for `radon-nextjs.service` (`:407-408`, verified in the lead), which makes Docker discard `CMD`; the env-vs-bundle check at `next-clerk-guard.sh:30-33` is dead code on the only path that matters. The two tests that "cover" it assert only that the string `next-clerk-guard` appears in the Dockerfile. The build-time gates (`Dockerfile.node:69-71`, `:80`; `app-images.yml:70-75`) still fail an EMPTY bake, so the residual exposure is the case the guard alone covered: `/etc/radon/env` carrying a different `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` than the one inlined in `.next/static` now boots silently with the frontend on one Clerk instance and the middleware on another. Filed P1 rather than the walk's P2 because a fix that ships as "refuses to boot" and is verified by string-presence tests is the 2026-08-27 lesson (audit the test's scope) recurring on an auth boundary. The guard also accepts any `pk_test_`/`pk_live_` value of length >= 20 (`:12-23`). Fix: run the guard as the nextjs container command (drop the explicit `bun run start` or pass the guard path) and add a `_run_line` test asserting it. |
| R-434 | P1 | `scripts/fetch_dispersion.py:136-140`, `:205-208`, `:318-324`, `:484`; `scripts/watchdog/services.py:231`; `web/lib/serviceHealthWindows.ts:246`; `web/components/DispersionPanel.tsx:286` | **A permanently failing IB rung in the DISPERSION sweep heartbeats `ok` every night while Yahoo silently serves the whole 516-symbol universe — Yahoo becomes the de facto scheduled primary source, which CLAUDE.md rule 7 forbids, with no operator signal.** `_ib_gateway_unavailable` (`:136-140`) and the connect `except` (`:205-208`, `# noqa: BLE001 — Yahoo rung covers the whole universe`) both return `{}` with only a `_log`; `fetch_closes_ladder` (`:318-324`) then hands every symbol to Yahoo and `record_service_health(SERVICE, state, ...)` at `:484` writes `ok` with `error=None` because no error object was ever produced. Both catalogs pin `requires_ib: False` (`services.py:231`, `serviceHealthWindows.ts:246`) so the watchdog never correlates it with an IB outage, and the only trace — `payload.source.prices == "yahoo"` — is never rendered: the panel footnote at `DispersionPanel.tsx:286` is the static string from `web/lib/dispersion.ts:29`. Scenario: gateway port closed or the auto-allocate range exhausted at 22:20 UTC every night -> every row from then on is Yahoo-built and green. Raised to P1 from the walk's P2 in the lead: the defect is not data quality (Yahoo closes are close enough) but that the repo's one hard data-source rule can be violated on a schedule invisibly; the honest state is `ok` with an `ib_rung_dead` error class, or `degraded`. Fix: when `fetch.ib_ok == 0` on a sweep run heartbeat with a non-fatal error class and render `source.prices` in the strip. |
| R-435 | P1 | `scripts/mktnews/client.js:83-106`; `scripts/mktnews/normalize.js:26`; `scripts/mktnews/protocol.js:44-46`; `scripts/mktnews/hub.js:201-204`; `cloud/config/drift-allowlist.conf:25` | **A half-open upstream socket is never detected by the MKTNews hub, so the headlines tape freezes silently as "live" and never reconnects until someone restarts the unit.** `connectMktnews` reacts only to `open`/`error`/`close` (`client.js:83-97`) and `scheduleReconnect` is reached solely from the `close` handler (`:96`, `:100-106`, verified in the lead); there is no ping, no idle timer, and the upstream's periodic `time` heartbeat frames that `protocol.js:44-46` classifies are discarded by `normalize.js:26` (`msg.kind === "time" ... return null`) rather than used as a liveness clock. When the upstream drops without a FIN (NAT/LB idle timeout, host reboot) `ws` keeps `readyState` OPEN indefinitely, no frame arrives, no reconnect is scheduled, and the hub's last broadcast was `upstream-open` (`hub.js:202`), so every dashboard renders a frozen tape as live (the web half is R-463). The process stays `active (running)`, so systemd, `units.py` and the drift audit all see a healthy unit. **Contingency:** `radon-mktnews.service` is `not-installed:` allowlisted today (`drift-allowlist.conf:25`, "enable after first green local run") and `setup-vps.sh:99` enables it on a fresh host, so this is P1 on install and latent until then. Fix: an idle watchdog that `terminate()`s and reconnects when no frame (including `time`) arrives within N seconds, broadcasting `upstream-down`. |
| R-436 | P1 | `scripts/flex_delivery_ingest.py:81-88`, `:90-95`; `scripts/db/writer.py:706-711`; `scripts/flex_sftp_pull.py:385-395`; `scripts/tests/test_flex_delivery_claim_release.py` | **REL-132 (R-379) is PARTIAL: the claim release is a single best-effort `DELETE` against the same Turso the writer just failed on, so the outage that half-writes `cash_flows` also keeps the claim, and the next run of the same bytes is `ok`/`duplicate` again — the P0 outcome R-379 named, now gated on a correlated failure.** `ingest_xml` releases via `_release_claim` (`:82-84`, `:86-87`), which swallows any exception to stderr (`:93-95`); `release_flex_delivery` is one `DELETE` + `commit` with no retry (`writer.py:706-711`). Verified by execution in the regression walk: with the writer raising mid-chunk and the release raising `ConnectionError`, the output was `B: raised Hrana 5xx mid-chunk \| release attempted: 1` and the simulated next run returned `B next run: True duplicate`; `flex_sftp_pull.py:386` passes `ok=True` for a duplicate, so the 08:30 re-pull heartbeats the batch as fine over a half-applied `cash_flows`. Case A (release succeeds) HOLDS — `release calls: 1`, the partial chunks are re-ingestable via upsert — and the shipped `test_a_raising_writer_releases_the_claim` exercises only that case. Filed P1 rather than P0 because a Turso outage must span both the write and the release; the same outage doing both is the realistic shape. Fix: make `claim_flex_delivery` record `status='in_progress'` and treat a stale in-progress row as claimable (or retry the release with backoff and page on failure). |
| R-437 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/sync-control-plane.sh:36-38`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:263-270`, `:1296`, `:1309-1313`; `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:1235-1272`; `cloud/scripts/bootstrap-control-plane.sh:313`, `:321-327`, `:406` | A bundle that bootstrap REJECTED is silently re-installed by `refresh-control-plane-privileged` with the app tier stopped, because the deploy-job sync discards bootstrap's verdict and the new preflight arm treats "installed differs" as "apply after promote". `sync-control-plane.sh` returns 0 on any non-75 failure (`:36-38`), so a bootstrap `die` on `bash -n` (`bootstrap:313`), polkit/python validation (`:321-327`) or `systemd-analyze` (`:406`) becomes a log line; `preflight_control_plane` reaches the new arm at `deploy.sh:263-270` and continues with a warning; `restart_services` stops the tier (`:1296`) and runs the privileged refresh (`:1309`) whose `refresh_install_file` validates only sudoers, three helpers and drop-ins (`helper:1235-1272`) — no arm for `/usr/local/sbin/radon-app-runtime` (the ExecStart of all five app units), `polkit/*.rules`, `drift_audit.py` / `disk_cleanup.py`, nor `systemd-analyze` for `services/*.service`. The R-380 fallback at `:1311` restarts the tier through the freshly installed runtime, so a bad runtime leaves no path back without root SSH. Overlaps R-140 / R-394 for unit files; the new element is the swallowed verdict plus the preflight arm. Fix: propagate a non-75 bootstrap failure as a hard preflight error, or give `refresh_install_file` bootstrap's validator table. |
| R-438 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:283-294`, `:354`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:1664` | `render_env_file` (020865ad, the unquoted `/etc/radon/env` copy) runs inside `$(...)` at `:354`, where errexit is not inherited, so a failed `sed`/`mkdir`/ENOSPC on `/run` still prints the output path after `>` has already truncated the previous copy (`:286-292`), and `printf '%s\n' "$out"` (`:293`) runs regardless. The walk verified the shape with bash: `set -euo pipefail; v="$(f)"` where `f` runs a failing `sed` returned the path, a 0-byte file, and continued. Under the old `--env-file /etc/radon/env` docker failed loudly; now `radon-api`/`monitor`/`relay` can come up with no `UW_TOKEN`, TWS or Clerk values, and `preflight_env` (`deploy.sh:1664`) only ever checks the host file, so the deploy gate can pass on a service that is silently unauthenticated. The rendered file itself is clean (root `0600` via `umask 077`, tmpfs, rewritten per start). Fix: render in the main shell before `set --` with `\|\| exit 71` and refuse an output whose line count differs from `$ENV_FILE`. |
| R-439 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:231`, `:262`, `:246-247`; `cloud/services/radon-relay.service.d/runtime-container.conf:3`; `cloud/services/radon-monitor.service.d/runtime-container.conf:3` | The REL-149 notify proxy binds its per-unit socket and sets it `0o666` (`:231`, verified in the lead) in a `0755` `/run/radon-app-runtime` dir (`:262`), and forwards every received datagram verbatim to `$NOTIFY_SOCKET` from a PID inside the unit cgroup (`:246-247`) under `NotifyAccess=all`. The proxy exists precisely because systemd drops datagrams from outside the cgroup; this reopens that door to every host process — including the unprivileged `radon` user that runs dozens of timers — so anyone who writes `WATCHDOG=1` to `/run/radon-app-runtime/radon-relay.service.sock` keeps a wedged relay `active (running)` (the R-391 wedge class), and `MAINPID=` is relayed too. Reached by both the control-plane walk and the regression walk; the shipped `test_notify_proxy_relays_ready_and_watchdog_datagrams` pins the relay of READY/WATCHDOG but not the socket mode or the sender. Fix: `chown` the socket to the container uid with mode `0600` and forward only `READY=1` / `WATCHDOG=1` / `STATUS=` lines. |
| R-440 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh:166-167`, `:1452-1465`; `cloud/scripts/bootstrap-control-plane.sh:277-279`, `:486`, `:260-264`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:933-938` | An unattended `sync-control-plane` that overruns the 300s root budget (`ROOT_SYNC_ACTION_TIMEOUT`, `:166-167`) is TERMed and then KILLed after 5s (`:1452-1465`); bootstrap handles TERM via `exit 143` -> `cleanup` (`:277-279`) but KILL runs nothing, and by then `rm -f -- "$READY_PATH"` (`:486`) has fired with the bundle half-installed (even the TERM path deliberately leaves readiness withdrawn once `daemon-reload` ran, `:260-264`). `sync-control-plane.sh` maps the 124 to exit 0 and this deploy fails preflight loudly, but the NEXT deploy job sees no marker at `ci.yml:935` and calls `deploy_with_legacy_runner` on a host whose units carry container drop-ins. Bootstrap previously ran only interactively over SSH, so the KILL path is new exposure from R-430. Fix: bootstrap restores readiness from backup on any exit when the manifest was not committed, and `ci.yml:935` treats a missing marker on a host with `runtime-container.conf` drop-ins as a hard refusal. |
| R-441 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/radon-app-runtime.sh:156-159`, `:62-63`, `:89-99`; `.github/workflows/app-images.yml:65-67`, `:95-98`; `cloud/scripts/deploy.sh:1734` | `cmd_pull` returns "already local" whenever both `radon-{python,node}:<sha>` tags exist locally (`:156-159`); nothing compares the local `RepoDigest` to the registry manifest (`image_in_registry` `:62-63` and `resolve_image` `:89-99` are existence-only). The node image's content depends on repo variables at build time (`app-images.yml:65-67`, `:95-98`), so the documented remediation for a wrong or rotated `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` value — re-run the workflow for HEAD — pushes a different image under the identical SHA tag, `prepull-images` and `deploy.sh:1734` both report "exact release image pair already local", and production keeps serving the old bake with no error anywhere. The pair a SHA runs is whichever run first landed on the host, not the run the deploy gated on. Fix: compare `docker manifest inspect` digest with local `RepoDigests` before skipping, or pull unconditionally (a current image is a no-op pull). |
| R-442 | P2 | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:86-96`; `.github/workflows/app-images.yml:43-46`, `:91-94` | The `app-images` caller job declares only an `if:` on main-push and no `needs:` (`ci.yml:86-96`), and the callee pushes the SHA tag and the moving `:latest` tag in one BuildKit operation (`app-images.yml:43-46`, `:91-94`), so a commit that fails gitleaks or every suite still overwrites the public GHCR `:latest` pointer with an image containing the Next client bundle and `scripts/`. No deploy impact — `resolve_image` refuses non-SHA tags (`radon-app-runtime.sh:55-58`, `:87-99`) and the prune skips `latest` (`:191`) — so this is supply-chain hygiene for a publicly resolvable tag. Fix: `needs: [secret-scan]` on the caller, or push `:latest` from a step gated on the tests. |
| R-443 | P2 | `docker/app/.dockerignore:3`; `docker/app/Dockerfile.node:3-4`, `:61-62`; `.gitignore:83-86`, `:141` | `.dockerignore` excludes only the literal `**/.env` (`:3`), while `COPY scripts/ ./scripts` and `COPY web/ ./web` (`:61-62`) take everything else in the context; the gitignored secret files the repo itself enumerates (`.gitignore:83-86` `.env.local`, `.env.*.local`, `.env.production`; `:141` `.env.ib-mode`) are not matched, and the Dockerfile header (`:3-4`) instructs building from the repo root on a workstation where such files exist. CI is unaffected (clean checkout); this bites a laptop build pushed under a 40-hex tag with a PAT, which the runtime would then accept. Fix: `**/.env*` with `!**/.env.example` and `!**/.env.local.example`. |
| R-444 | P2 | `docs/operations.md:169`; `cloud/caddy/Caddyfile:79-81`, `:91-95`, `:176-182`; `scripts/health_probe/probe.py:451-477` | After cd6af110 / b8eda2b6 every failure mode of `/edge-health/status` returns 200: `/edge-health/ping` is static 200 (`Caddyfile:79-81`), an upstream 502/503/504 is rewritten to 200 `{"reachable":false}` (`:91-95`), and a Caddy-synthesized dial-refused on `/edge-health/status` gets the same body (`:176-182`); the daemon itself never emits a non-200 on `/status` (`serve.py:213-243`). A status-code-only external uptime monitor therefore sees UP in every state except Caddy dead. The repo's own prober is safe — the synthetic body has no `schema_version`/`ok`/`overall_state`, which `_classify_status_payload` treats as `invalid` (`probe.py:451-477`) — and the mapping is bounded to the two health paths, but `docs/operations.md:169` still says the path "returns `502` when the daemon process is down", which is the discriminating check the incident runbook points operators at. Fix: update the caveat to "200 with `reachable:false` and no `ok` field" and pin the external monitor on the body, not the status. |
| R-445 | P2 | `cloud/scripts/db_backup.py:58`, `:178-186`, `:737-739`, `:744`, `:550`, `:525` | 1cb81bc9 cut local dump retention to 7d (`RETENTION_DAYS`, `:58`), but `run_backup` prunes every `*.sql.gz` older than that (`:737-739`) BEFORE the off-box leg runs (`:744`), and `select_prunable` (`:178-186`) considers only mtime, never whether `sync_offbox` confirmed the object in B2; `sync_offbox` is budget-bounded and defers its tail (`:550`), and its docstring still promises "the existing 30-dump local window backfills" (`:525`) — that slack is now 7 nights. Scenario: B2 credentials rotate or `AccessDenied` (non-transient, not retried by 87233bf1) for eight nights; each night pages `error`, but on night 8 the night-1 dump is unlinked unheard, leaving a permanent hole in the 365-day off-box series. Stale 30-day text remains at `:37`, `:94`, `:112`, `:525`, `:566`. Fix: prune only names present in the `remote` listing (move the local prune after `sync_offbox`, or skip dumps absent from B2 while a B2 config exists). |
| R-446 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_dispersion.py:63-65`, `:153-158`, `:281-283`, `:318-324`, `:554-556` | The IB and Yahoo rungs share one 600s deadline (`:63-65`): `_ib_sweep` checks it only before issuing each request (`:156-158`), so an HMDS-inactive gateway (authenticated, port listening, every `reqHistoricalDataAsync` returning `[]` on its own timeout) burns ~3225s of per-request timeouts across 516 symbols / 8 slots and crosses the deadline around round 12. `fetch_closes_ladder` then passes the SAME spent deadline to `_fetch_yahoo_incremental`, whose first-iteration check (`:281-283`) breaks immediately — the walk verified `_fetch_yahoo_incremental(['AAPL'], monotonic()-1)` returns `{}` with "sweep budget spent at 0/1". | R-448 | P2 | `scripts/fetch_dispersion.py:562-569`, `:572-580`, `:650-653`, `:66-67` | `_ensure_window_bridges_gap` (`:562-569`) raises `RuntimeError` when `sessions[0] > stored_max`, `run` records `cycle_failed` and re-raises, and `INCREMENTAL_DURATION` is the constant `1 M` (`:66`).
